For tasks related to meeting teachers, relatives, kings, ministers, and others that yield auspicious results, one should interact using the active side. This guarantees the accomplishment of the desired task in mind.

---

---

### Verse 93
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अग्निचोराधर्मकर्म अन्येषां वादिनिग्रहः ।
कर्तव्याः खलु रिक्तायां जयलाभसुखार्थिभिः ॥ ९३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
agnicorādharmakarma anyeṣāṃ vādinigrahaḥ /
kartavyāḥ khalu riktāyāṃ jayalābhasukhārthibhiḥ // 93 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
agni - fire; cora - thief; adharma-karma - unrighteous deeds; anyeṣāṃ - of others; vādi-nigrahaḥ - subduing opponents in debate; kartavyāḥ - should be done; khalu - indeed; riktāyāṃ - during the empty (inactive) side; jaya-lābha-sukha-arthibhiḥ - by those desiring victory, profit, and happiness.
**English Translation:**  
Dealing with fire, confronting thieves, countering unrighteous deeds, or subduing opponents in disputes should be undertaken using the inactive (empty) side by those who desire victory, profit, and happiness.

---

---

### Verse 94
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दूरदेशे विधातव्यं गमनं तु निशाकरे ।
अभ्यर्णे देशदीप्ते तु तरणाविति केचन ॥ ९४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dūradeśe vidhātavyaṃ gamanaṃ tu niśākare /
abhyarṇe deśadīpte tu taraṇāviti kecana // 94 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dūra-deśe - to a distant land; vidhātavyaṃ - should be undertaken; gamanaṃ - travel; tu - indeed; niśā-kare - in the moon (Iḍā); abhyarṇe - to a nearby place; deśa-dīpte - in an illuminated/bright region; tu - but; taraṇau - in the sun (Piṅgalā); iti - thus; kecana - some (say).
**English Translation:**  
Travel to a distant land should be undertaken when the lunar Nadi is flowing. Conversely, for traveling to nearby places, it should be done when the solar Nadi is active, according to some scholars.

---

---

### Verse 95
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यत्किञ्चित्पूर्वमुद्दिष्टं लाभादि समरागमे ।
तत्सर्वं पूर्णनाडीषु जायते निर्विकल्पकम् ॥ ९५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yatkiñcitpūrvamuddiṣṭaṃ lābhādi samarāgame /
tatsarvaṃ pūrṇanāḍīṣu jāyate nirvikalpakam // 95 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yat kiñcit - whatever; pūrvam - earlier; uddiṣṭaṃ - was instructed; lābha-ādi - profit, etc.; samara-āgame - in the advent of war; tat sarvaṃ - all that; pūrṇa-nāḍīṣu - in the full (active) Nadis; jāyate - happens/arises; nirvikalpakam - without a doubt (certainly).
**English Translation:**  
Whatever benefits and profits were described earlier concerning the onset of battle, all of them undoubtedly come to fruition if actions are performed with the active (full) Nadi.

---

---

### Verse 96
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शून्यनाड्यां विपर्यस्तं यत्पूर्वं प्रतिपादितम् ।
जायते नान्यथा चैव यथा सर्वज्ञभाषितम् ॥ ९६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śūnyanāḍyāṃ viparyastaṃ yatpūrvaṃ pratipāditam /
jāyate nānyathā caiva yathā sarvajñabhāṣitam // 96 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śūnya-nāḍyāṃ - in the empty (inactive) Nadi; viparyastaṃ - reversed; yat - which; pūrvaṃ - earlier; pratipāditam - established/explained; jāyate - happens; na anyathā - not otherwise; ca eva - and indeed; yathā - as; sarva-jña-bhāṣitam - spoken by the omniscient Lord Shiva.
**English Translation:**  
However, if actions are performed during the flow of the empty Nadi, the results established previously are completely reversed. It happens exactly as spoken by the omniscient Lord Shiva, and not otherwise.

---

---

### Verse 97
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
व्यवहारे वशोच्चाटे द्वेषिविद्यादिविश्वके ।
कुपितस्वामिचौराद्ये पूर्णस्थाः स्युर्भयङ्कराः ॥ ९७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vyavahāre vaśoccāṭe dveṣividyādiviśvake /
kupitasvāmicaurādye pūrṇasthāḥ syurbhayaṅkarāḥ // 97 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vyavahāre - in worldly transactions/lawsuits; vaśa-uccāṭe - in subduing or driving away (foes); dveṣi - enemies; vidyā-ādi - mystical sciences, etc.; viśvake - all-encompassing; kupita-svāmi - angry master; caura-ādye - thieves and the like; pūrṇa-sthāḥ - positioned in the full/active flow; syuḥ - they become; bhayaṃ-karāḥ - terrifying/formidable.
**English Translation:**  
In worldly transactions, lawsuits, magical arts for subduing or driving away enemies, and dealing with an angry master or thieves, those who operate aligning with their active (full) Nadi become formidable and fearsome to their opponents.

---

---

### Verse 98
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दूराध्वनि शशी भद्रो निर्विघ्नोऽभीष्टसिद्धिदः ।
प्रवेशकार्यहेतौ च सूर्यनाडी प्रशस्यते ॥ ९८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dūrādhvani śaśī bhadro nirvighno'bhīṣṭasiddhidaḥ /
praveśakāryahetau ca sūryanāḍī praśasyate // 98 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dūra-adhvani - in a long journey; śaśī - the moon; bhadraḥ - auspicious; nirvighnaḥ - free from obstacles; abhīṣṭa-siddhi-daḥ - bestowing the desired success; praveśa-kārya-hetau - for the purpose of entering (a house, city, etc.); ca - and; sūrya-nāḍī - the solar Nadi; praśasyate - is praised.
**English Translation:**  
For embarking on a long journey, the lunar Nadi is auspicious, providing a path free of obstacles and fulfilling one's desires. However, for the purpose of entering a house, city, or commencing tasks, the solar Nadi is highly praised.

---

---

### Verse 99
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अयोग्ये योग्यता नाड्या योग्यस्थानेऽप्ययोग्यता ।
कार्यानुबन्धनो जीवो यथा रन्ध्रं तथा चरेत् ॥ ९९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ayogye yogyatā nāḍyā yogyasthāne'pyayogyatā /
kāryānubandhano jīvo yathā randhraṃ tathā caret // 99 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ayogye - in the unfit (task); yogyatā - fitness; nāḍyā - by the Nadi; yogya-sthāne - in the fit situation; api - even; ayogyatā - unfitness; kārya-anubandhanaḥ - bound by actions/tasks; jīvaḥ - the living being; yathā - as; randhraṃ - the nostril (Nadi); tathā - so; caret - should act.
**English Translation:**  
The state of the Nadi determines fitness in an otherwise unfit task, and unfitness even in an appropriate task. Since living beings are bound by their activities, one must act according to the flow of breath in the nostril.

---

---

### Verse 100
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रचारे विषं सहते सूर्ये बलिवशं नयेत् ।
सुषुम्नायां भवेन्मोक्ष एको देवस्त्रिधा स्थितः ॥ १०० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
candracāre viṣaṃ sahate sūrye balivaśaṃ nayet /
suṣumnāyāṃ bhavenmokṣa eko devastridhā sthitaḥ // 100 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candra-cāre - in the flow of the moon; viṣaṃ - poison/offense; sahate - tolerates/destroys; sūrye - in the sun; bali - the powerful one; vaśaṃ nayet - brings under control; suṣumnāyāṃ - in the Suṣumnā Nadi; bhavet - is; mokṣaḥ - liberation; ekaḥ devaḥ - one deity; tridhā - in three ways; sthitaḥ - situated.
**English Translation:**  
During the lunar flow, one can tolerate or neutralize poison/offenses. During the solar flow, one can bring powerful opponents under control. When the Suṣumnā flows, liberation is attained. Thus, the one divine principle (the breath) exists in three different states.

---

---

### Verse 101
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शुभान्यशुभकार्याणि क्रियन्तेऽहर्निशं यदा ।
तदा कार्यानुरोधेन कार्यं नाडीप्रचालनम् ॥ १०१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śubhānyaśubhakāryāṇi kriyante'harniśaṃ yadā /
tadā kāryānurodhena kāryaṃ nāḍīpracālanam // 101 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śubhāni - auspicious; aśubha - inauspicious; kāryāṇi - tasks; kriyante - are done; ahar-niśaṃ - day and night; yadā - when; tadā - then; kārya-anurodhena - according to the requirement of the task; kāryaṃ - should be done; nāḍī-pracālanam - the manipulation/activation of the Nadi.
**English Translation:**  
When both auspicious and inauspicious tasks are to be performed continuously day and night, one should intentionally change or activate the Nadi according to the specific requirement of the task at hand.

---

---

### Verse 102
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
स्थिरकर्माण्यलङ्कारे दूराध्वगमने तथा ।
आश्रमे धर्मप्रासादे वस्तूनां संग्रहेऽपि च ॥ १०२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sthirakarmāṇyalaṅkāre dūrādhvagamane tathā /
āśrame dharmaprāsāde vastūnāṃ saṅgrahe'pi ca // 102 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sthira-karmāṇi - stable/permanent acts; alaṅkāre - in wearing ornaments; dūra-adhva-gamane - in travelling long distances; tathā - and; āśrame - for entering a hermitage; dharma-prāsāde - in building a temple of dharma; vastūnāṃ - of objects; saṅgrahe - in the collection; api ca - and also.
**English Translation:**  
For undertaking permanent or stable acts, wearing new ornaments, travelling long distances, entering an ashrama, building religious temples, and for the collection of items... (continued in next verses, specifying lunar Nadi).

---

---

### Verse 103
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वापीकूपतडागादिप्रतिष्ठास्तम्भदेवयोः ।
यात्रादाने विवाहे च वस्त्रालङ्कारभूषणे ॥ १०३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāpīkūpataḍāgādipratiṣṭhāstambhadevayoḥ /
yātrādāne vivāhe ca vastrālaṅkārabhūṣaṇe // 103 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāpī - step-wells; kūpa - wells; taḍāga - ponds; ādi - etc.; pratiṣṭhā - installation; stambha - pillars; devayoḥ - and deities; yātrā-dāne - in undertaking journeys and giving charity; vivāhe - in marriages; ca - and; vastra-alaṅkāra-bhūṣaṇe - in wearing clothes and ornaments.
**English Translation:**  
...in constructing and consecrating step-wells, wells, ponds, pillars, and deities; during journeys, making charities, marriage ceremonies, and putting on new garments and jewels...

---

---

### Verse 104
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शान्तिके पौष्टिके चैव दिव्याौषधिरसायने ।
स्वस्वामिदर्शने मित्रे वाणिज्ये कणसंग्रहे ॥ १०४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śāntike pauṣṭike caiva divyauṣadhirasāyane /
svasvāmidarśane mitre vāṇijye kaṇasaṅgrahe // 104 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śāntike - in pacifying rituals; pauṣṭike - in nourishing/prospering rituals; ca eva - and indeed; divya-auṣadhi - divine medicines; rasāyane - alchemical elixirs; sva-svāmi-darśane - in seeing one's master; mitre - in meeting a friend; vāṇijye - in trade/commerce; kaṇa-saṅgrahe - in the collection of grain.
**English Translation:**  
...in performing pacification and prosperity rituals, making or taking divine medicines and elixirs, meeting one's master or friends, conducting trade, and storing grains...

---

---

### Verse 105
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गृहप्रवेशे सेवायां कृषौ च बीजवापने ।
शुभकर्मणि सन्धौ च निर्गमे च शुभः शशी ॥ १०५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
gṛhapraveśe sevāyāṃ kṛṣau ca bījavāpane /
śubhakarmaṇi sandhau ca nirgame ca śubhaḥ śaśī // 105 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
gṛha-praveśe - in entering a house; sevāyāṃ - in rendering service; kṛṣau - in agriculture; ca - and; bīja-vāpane - in sowing seeds; śubha-karmaṇi - in auspicious tasks; sandhau - in making peace/treaties; ca - and; nirgame - in going out; ca - and; śubhaḥ - is auspicious; śaśī - the moon (Iḍā).
**English Translation:**  
...in entering a new house, offering service, agriculture, sowing seeds, undertaking any auspicious activities, negotiating peace treaties, and setting out—the flow of the lunar Nadi is highly auspicious.

---

---

### Verse 106
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
विद्यारम्भादिकार्येषु बान्धवानां च दर्शने ।
जन्ममोक्षे च धर्मे च दीक्षायां मन्त्रसाधने ॥ १०६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vidyārambhādikāryeṣu bāndhavānāṃ ca darśane /
janmamokṣe ca dharme ca dīkṣāyāṃ mantrasādhane // 106 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vidyā-ārambha-ādi - beginning of education, etc.; kāryeṣu - in tasks; bāndhavānāṃ - of relatives; ca - and; darśane - in seeing; janma-mokṣe - in understanding birth and liberation; ca - and; dharme - in righteous acts; ca - and; dīkṣāyāṃ - in initiation; mantra-sādhane - in mastering mantras.
**English Translation:**  
Also, for initiating education and similar tasks, meeting relatives, contemplating life and liberation, practicing righteous deeds, receiving spiritual initiation, and mastering mantras...

---

---

### Verse 107
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
कालविज्ञानसूत्रे च चतुष्पादगृहागमे ।
कालव्याधिचिकित्सायां स्वामिसम्बोधने तथा ॥ १०७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
kālavijñānasūtre ca catuṣpādagṛhāgame /
kālavyādhicikitsāyāṃ svāmisambodhane tathā // 107 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
kāla-vijñāna-sūtre - in the study of time and its indicators; ca - and; catuṣpāda - four-footed animals; gṛha-āgame - in bringing to the house; kāla-vyādhi - diseases of time/death; cikitsāyāṃ - in the medical treatment; svāmi-sambodhane - in addressing/appealing to the master; tathā - and.
**English Translation:**  
...in comprehending the science of time, bringing cattle or four-footed animals home, treating chronic or severe diseases, and making an appeal to one's master...

---

---

### Verse 108
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गजाश्वारोहणे धन्विगजाश्वानां च बन्धने ।
परोपकरणे चैव निधीनां स्थापने तथा ॥ १०८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
gajāśvārohaṇe dhanvigajāśvānāṃ ca bandhane /
paropakaraṇe caiva nidhīnāṃ sthāpane tathā // 108 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
gaja-aśva - elephant and horse; ārohaṇe - in riding; dhanvi - holding a bow/archer; gaja-aśvānāṃ - of elephants and horses; ca - and; bandhane - in tying/taming; para-upakaraṇe - in doing good to others; ca eva - and indeed; nidhīnāṃ - of treasures; sthāpane - in storing/safekeeping; tathā - and.
**English Translation:**  
...for mounting horses and elephants, practicing archery, tying or taming horses and elephants, performing charitable acts for others, and safely storing treasure...

---

---

### Verse 109
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गीतवाद्यादिके नृत्ये शास्त्राणां च विचारणे ।
पुरग्रामनिवेशे च तिलकक्षेत्रधारणे ॥ १०९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
gītavādyādike nṛtye śāstrāṇāṃ ca vicāraṇe /
puragrāmaniveśe ca tilakakṣetradhāraṇe // 109 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
gīta-vādya-ādike - in singing, playing instruments, etc.; nṛtye - in dancing; śāstrāṇāṃ - of scriptures; ca - and; vicāraṇe - in contemplating/debating; pura-grāma - city and village; niveśe - in entering/settling; ca - and; tilaka-kṣetra-dhāraṇe - in wearing a tilaka and acquiring land.
**English Translation:**  
...for singing, playing musical instruments, dancing, discussing the scriptures, settling in a new village or city, wearing an auspicious mark on the forehead, and acquiring land...

---

---

### Verse 110
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आर्तौ शोके विषादे च ज्वरपीडामूर्च्छितेऽपि वा ।
स्वजनस्वामिसम्बन्धे अन्नादिकाष्ठसंग्रहे ॥ ११० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ārtau śoke viṣāde ca jvarapīḍāmūrcchite'pi vā /
svajanasvāmisambandhe annādikāṣṭhasaṅgrahe // 110 //
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ārtau - in suffering; śoke - in grief; viṣāde - in depression/despair; ca - and; jvara-pīḍā - affliction of fever; mūrcchite - in fainting; api vā - or even; svajana-svāmi-sambandhe - in relations with one's kin and master; anna-ādi - food, etc.; kāṣṭha - firewood; saṅgrahe - in gathering/collecting.
**English Translation:**  
...in times of suffering, grief, despair, affliction by fever, or fainting; in maintaining relationships with one's kin and master, and in gathering food, firewood, and supplies—the flow of the lunar Nadi is considered beneficial.

---

---

### Verse 111
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ पञ्चमहाभूततत्त्वज्ञानम् —  
पृच्छामि देवदेवेश देहस्थं तत्त्वनिर्णयम् ।  
येन विज्ञानमात्रेण योगी भवति सर्ववित् ॥ १११ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha pañcamahābhūtatattvajñānam —*  
*pṛcchāmi devadeveśa dehasthaṁ tattvanirṇayam |*  
*yena vijñātamātreṇa yogī bhavati sarvavit || 111 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*pṛcchāmi* = I inquire; *devadeveśa* = O God of gods; *dehastham tattvanirṇayam* = the definitive science of Tattvas inside the body; *yena vijñātamātreṇa* = by the mere knowledge of which; *yogī bhavati sarvavit* = a yogi becomes omniscient (all-knowing).

**English Translation:**  
Goddess Pārvatī said: O Supreme God of gods! I now inquire into the definitive science of the Five Elements (*Tattvas*) residing within the human body, by the mere realization of which a yogi becomes all-knowing (*Sarvavit*).

---

---

---

### Verse 112
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पञ्च तत्त्वानि देहेऽस्मिन् पृथ्व्यादीनि क्रमेण तु ।  
तस्य सूक्ष्मो व्ययो नास्ति ज्ञेयं तत्त्वविचाक्षणैः ॥ ११२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*pañca tattvāni dehe'smin pṛthvyādīni krameṇa tu |*  
*tasya sūkṣmo vyayo nāsti jñeyaṁ tattvavicākṣaṇaiḥ || 112 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*pañca tattvāni* = five elements; *dehe asmin* = within this body; *pṛthvyādīni krameṇa tu* = Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether in order; *tasya sūkṣmaḥ vyayaḥ nāsti* = its subtle decay does not occur; *jñeyam tattvavicākṣaṇaiḥ* = to be known by discerning seers.

**English Translation:**  
Within this physical frame reside the **Five Great Elements** (*Pañca Mahābhūta*): (1) **Earth** (*Pṛthvī*), (2) **Water** (*Ap / Jala*), (3) **Fire** (*Tejas / Agni*), (4) **Air** (*Vāyu*), and (5) **Ether** (*Ākāśa*).

---

---

---

### Verse 113
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथ्वी आपस्तथा तेजो वायुराकाशमेव च ।  
पञ्चधातूमयो पिण्डः पञ्चतत्त्वात्मकः स्मृतः ॥ ११३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*pṛthvī āpastathā tejo vāyurākāśameva ca |*  
*pañcadhātūmayo piṇḍaḥ pañcatattvātmakaḥ smṛtaḥ || 113 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*pṛthvī āpaḥ tathā tejaḥ* = Earth, Water, and Fire; *vāyuḥ ākāśam eva ca* = Air and Ether indeed; *pañcadhātūmayaḥ piṇḍaḥ* = human body composed of five fundamental humors; *pañcatattvātmakaḥ smṛtaḥ* = recognized as an embodiment of the Five Elements.

**English Translation:**  
Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether—these five elements constitute the human body (*Piṇḍa*). Every cell and movement inside the human vessel is governed by these five elemental vibrations.

---

---

---

### Verse 114
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ तत्त्ववर्णाः —  
पीता श्वेता तथा रक्ता नीला बिन्दुसमन्विता ।  
तत्त्वानां मण्डलानि स्युः सर्वशास्त्रेषु सम्मता ॥ ११४ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha tattvavarṇāḥ —*  
*pītā śvetā tathā raktā nīlā bindusamanvitā |*  
*tattvānāṁ maṇdalāni syuḥ sarvaśāstreṣu sammatā || 114 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*pītā* = Yellow (Earth); *śvetā* = White (Water); *tathā raktā* = Red (Fire); *nīlā* = Blue/Dark (Air); *bindusamanvitā* = Multi-colored / filled with dots (Ether); *tattvānām maṇdalāni syuḥ* = elemental mandalas; *sarvaśāstreṣu sammatā* = agreed upon in all Shastras.

**English Translation:**  
**Colors of the Five Elements:**  
- **Earth (*Pṛthvī*)**: Yellow (*Pīta*)
- **Water (*Ap*)**: Pure White (*Śveta*)
- **Fire (*Tejas*)**: Crimson Red (*Rakta*)
- **Air (*Vāyu*)**: Deep Blue / Sky Blue (*Nīla*)
- **Ether (*Ākāśa*)**: Transparent / Multi-colored dots (*Bindu-samanvita*)

---

---

---

### Verse 115
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ तत्त्वाकृतयः —  
चतुरस्रं चार्धचन्द्रं त्रिकोणं वृत्तमेव च ।  
बिन्दुयुक्तं च नभसो मण्डलं तत्त्वलक्षणम् ॥ ११५ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha tattvākṛtayaḥ —*  
*caturasraṁ cārdhacandraṁ trikoṇaṁ vṛttameva ca |*  
*binduyuktaṁ ca nabhaso maṇdalaṁ tattvalakṣaṇam || 115 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*caturasram* = Square (Earth); *cārdhacandram* = Crescent Moon (Water); *trikoṇam* = Triangle (Fire); *vṛttam eva ca* = Circle (Air); *binduyuktam ca nabhasaḥ* = Point-filled expanse of Ether; *maṇdalam tattvalakṣaṇam* = characteristic geometrical mandalas of elements.

**English Translation:**  
**Geometric Shapes of the Five Elements:**  
- **Earth**: Square (*Caturasra*)
- **Water**: Crescent / Half-Moon (*Ardhacandra*)
- **Fire**: Upward Triangle (*Trikoṇa*)
- **Air**: Circle (*Vṛtta*)
- **Ether**: Formless / Point-filled expanse (*Bindu*)

---

---

---

### Verse 116
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ तत्त्वरसाः —  
मधुरः काषायकश्चैव तीक्ष्णोऽम्लः कटुकस्तथा ।  
तत्त्वानां च रसा ज्ञेयाः पृथ्व्यादीनां क्रमेण तु ॥ ११६ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha tattvarasāḥ —*  
*madhuraḥ kāṣāyakaścaiva tīkṣṇo'mlaḥ kaṭukastathā |*  
*tattvānāṁ ca rasā jñeyāḥ pṛthvyādīnāṁ krameṇa tu || 116 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*madhuraḥ* = Sweet (Earth); *kāṣāyakaḥ ca eva* = Astringent/Salty (Water); *tīkṣṇaḥ* = Pungent/Spicy (Fire); *amlaḥ* = Sour (Air); *kaṭukaḥ tathā* = Bitter (Ether); *tattvānām ca rasā jñeyāḥ* = tastes of the elements to be known; *pṛthvyādīnām krameṇa tu* = in sequential order from Earth onward.

**English Translation:**  
**Tastes (*Rasa*) of the Five Elements:**  
- **Earth**: Sweet (*Madhura*)
- **Water**: Astringent / Salty (*Kaṣāya*)
- **Fire**: Pungent / Spicy (*Tīkṣṇa*)
- **Air**: Sour (*Amla*)
- **Ether**: Bitter (*Kaṭuka*)

---

---

---

### Verse 117
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ तत्त्वगतिप्रमाणम् —  
द्वादाशाङ्गुलि पृथ्वी च वारुणं षोडशाङ्गुलम् ।  
अष्टाङ्गुलं वहेदग्निः मारुतं च चतुर्धशम् ॥ ११७ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha tattvagatipramāṇam —*  
*dvādāśāṅguli pṛthvī ca vāruṇaṁ ṣoḍaśāṅgulam |*  
*aṣṭāṅgulaṁ vahedagniḥ mārutaṁ ca caturdhaśam || 117 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*dvādāśāṅguli pṛthvī ca* = Earth breath extends 12 finger-widths; *vāruṇam ṣoḍaśāṅgulam* = Water breath extends 16 finger-widths; *aṣṭāṅgulam vahet agniḥ* = Fire breath extends 8 finger-widths; *mārutam ca caturaṅgulam* = Air breath extends 4 finger-widths.

**English Translation:**  
**Length of Exhalation (*Aṅgula*) for Each Element:**  
- **Earth (*Pṛthvī*)**: 12 finger-widths (*Dvādaśāṅgula*)
- **Water (*Ap*)**: 16 finger-widths (*Ṣoḍaśāṅgula*)
- **Fire (*Tejas*)**: 8 finger-widths (*Aṣṭāṅgula*)
- **Air (*Vāyu*)**: 4 finger-widths (*Caturaṅgula*)
- **Ether (*Ākāśa*)**: Internal / Subtle movement (*Sūkṣma*)

---

---

---

### Verse 118
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मध्यमवाहिनी पृथ्वी अधोवाहि तथोदकम् ।  
ऊर्ध्ववाहिनो तेजश्च तिर्यग्वाहि च मारुतम् ॥ ११८ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*madhyamavāhinī pṛthvī adhovāhi tathodakam |*  
*ūrdhvavāhino tejaśca tiryagvāhi ca mārutam || 118 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*madhyamavāhinī pṛthvī* = Earth flows through middle of nostril; *adhovāhi tathodakam* = Water flows downward; *ūrdhvavāhino tejaśca* = Fire flows upward; *tiryagvāhi ca mārutam* = Air flows diagonally/sideways.

**English Translation:**  
**Direction of Breath Movement within Nostril:**  
- **Earth**: Flows steadily through the **center** of the nostril opening.
- **Water**: Flows **downward** toward the lower lip.
- **Fire**: Flows **upward** toward the bridge of the nose.
- **Air**: Flows **diagonally / sideways** across the nostril walls.
- **Ether**: Moves subtly in all directions or stays stationary.

---

---

### Verse 119
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
खरोष्ट्रमहिषादीनां गजाश्वारोहणे तथा ।
नदीजलावतरणे भेषजे लिपिलिखने ॥ ११९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
kharoṣṭramahiṣādīnāṃ gajāśvārohaṇe tathā |
nadījalāvataraṇe bheṣaje lipilikhane || 119 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
khara — donkey; uṣṭra — camel; mahiṣa — buffalo; ādīnāṃ — and others; gaja — elephant; aśva — horse; ārohaṇe — in riding; tathā — as well as; nadī — river; jala — water; avataraṇe — crossing/entering; bheṣaje — taking medicine; lipi — script; likhane — in writing.
**English Translation:**  
(The Sun Nadi is favorable) for riding animals such as donkeys, camels, buffaloes, elephants, and horses, as well as for entering river waters, taking medicines, and writing documents.

---

---

### Verse 120
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मारणमोहनस्तम्भे विद्वेषोच्चाटने वशे ।
प्रेषणे कर्षणे क्षोभे दाने च क्रयविक्रये ॥ १२० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
māraṇamohanastambhe vidveṣoccāṭane vaśe |
preṣaṇe karṣaṇe kṣobhe dāne ca krayavikraye || 120 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
māraṇa — destruction; mohana — delusion; stambhe — in paralyzing; vidveṣa — creating enmity; uccāṭane — in eradicating; vaśe — in subjugating; preṣaṇe — in sending/directing; karṣaṇe — in pulling/ploughing; kṣobhe — in agitating; dāne — in giving charity; ca — and; kraya — buying; vikraye — in selling.
**English Translation:**  
(The Sun Nadi is auspicious) for aggressive tantric acts such as Marana (destruction), Mohana (delusion), Stambhana (paralyzing), Vidveshana (creating enmity), Uccatana (eradication), and Vashikarana (subjugation), as well as for directing, agricultural activities, creating agitation, giving donations, and engaging in buying and selling.

---

---

### Verse 121
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रेताकर्षणविद्वेषशत्रुनिग्रहणेऽपि च ।
खड्गहस्ते रिपुयुद्धे भोगराजदर्शने ।
भोजने स्नाने व्यवहारे दीप्तकार्ये रविः शुभः ॥ १२१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pretākarṣaṇavidveṣaśatrunigrahaṇe'pi ca |
khaḍgahaste ripuyuddhe bhogarājadarśane |
bhojane snāne vyavahāre dīptakārye raviḥ śubhaḥ || 121 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
preta — spirit; ākarṣaṇa — summoning; vidveṣa — enmity; śatru — enemy; nigrahaṇe — in subduing; api — also; ca — and; khaḍga — sword; haste — in hand; ripu — enemy; yuddhe — in battle; bhoga — enjoyment; rāja — king; darśane — in seeing/meeting; bhojane — in eating; snāne — in bathing; vyavahāre — in worldly transactions; dīpta-kārye — in fiery tasks; raviḥ — the Sun (Nadi); śubhaḥ — is auspicious.
**English Translation:**  
The Sun Nadi (Pingala) is auspicious for summoning spirits, creating enmity, subduing enemies, taking up swords in battle against foes, partaking in enjoyments, meeting kings, eating, bathing, conducting worldly transactions, and performing any demanding or fiery actions.

---

---

### Verse 122
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
भुक्तमार्गेण मन्दाग्नौ स्त्रीणां वश्यादिकर्मणि ।
शयनं सूर्यवाहेन कर्तव्यं सर्वदा बुधैः ॥ १२२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
bhuktamārgeṇa mandāgnau strīṇāṃ vaśyādikarmaṇi |
śayanaṃ sūryavāhena kartavyaṃ sarvadā budhaiḥ || 122 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
bhukta-mārgeṇa — through the path of digesting food; manda-agnau — when digestive fire is weak; strīṇāṃ — of women; vaśya-ādi — attracting/subjugating and others; karmaṇi — in the act; śayanaṃ — sleeping; sūrya-vāhena — during the flow of the Sun Nadi; kartavyaṃ — should be done; sarvadā — always; budhaiḥ — by the wise.
**English Translation:**  
The wise should always engage in activities to stimulate a weak digestive fire after eating, perform acts of attracting women, and go to sleep while the Sun Nadi (Pingala) is flowing.

---

---

### Verse 123
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
क्रूराणि सर्वकर्माणि चराणि विविधानि च ।
तानि सिद्ध्यन्ति सूर्येण नात्र कार्या विचारणा ॥ १२३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
krūrāṇi sarvakarmāṇi carāṇi vividhāni ca |
tāni siddhyanti sūryeṇa nātra kāryā vicāraṇā || 123 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
krūrāṇi — cruel/harsh; sarva — all; karmāṇi — actions; carāṇi — mobile/dynamic; vividhāni — various; ca — and; tāni — they; siddhyanti — are accomplished; sūryeṇa — by the Sun (Nadi); na — not; atra — here; kāryā — to be done; vicāraṇā — doubt/hesitation.
**English Translation:**  
All harsh, severe, and varied dynamic actions are successfully accomplished during the flow of the Sun Nadi. One should harbor no doubts about this.

---

---

### Verse 124
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
क्षणं वामं क्षणं दक्षं यदा वहति मारुतः ।
सुषुम्ना सा च विज्ञेया सर्वकार्यहरा स्मृता ॥ १२४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
kṣaṇaṃ vāmaṃ kṣaṇaṃ dakṣaṃ yadā vahati mārutaḥ |
suṣumnā sā ca vijñeyā sarvakāryaharā smṛtā || 124 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
kṣaṇaṃ — for a moment; vāmaṃ — left; kṣaṇaṃ — for a moment; dakṣaṃ — right; yadā — when; vahati — flows; mārutaḥ — the breath; suṣumnā — Sushumna (the central Nadi); sā — that; ca — and; vijñeyā — should be known; sarva-kārya — all actions; harā — destroying; smṛtā — is remembered/stated.
**English Translation:**  
When the breath flows alternately through the left nostril for a moment and the right nostril for a moment, it should be known as the Sushumna Nadi. It is stated to be destructive to all worldly actions.

---

---

### Verse 125
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तस्यां नाड्यां स्थितो वह्निर्यथा कालस्वरूपकः ।
विषवत् तं विजानीयात् सर्वकार्यविनाशनम् ॥ १२५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tasyāṃ nāḍyāṃ sthito vahniryathā kālasvarūpakaḥ |
viṣavat taṃ vijānīyāt sarvakāryavināśanam || 125 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tasyāṃ — in that; nāḍyāṃ — in the Nadi (Sushumna); sthitaḥ — situated; vahniḥ — fire; yathā — just like; kāla — death/time; svarūpakaḥ — having the form of; viṣavat — like poison; taṃ — that; vijānīyāt — one should know; sarva — all; kārya — actions; vināśanam — destroyer.
**English Translation:**  
The fire situated in that Nadi (Sushumna) is like the embodiment of Time (Death) itself. One should know it to be like poison, acting as the destroyer of all (worldly) actions.

---

---

### Verse 126
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यदानुक्रममुल्लङ्घ्य यस्य नाडीद्वयं वहेत् ।
तदा तस्य विजानीयादशुभं नात्र संशयः ॥ १२६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yadānukramamullaṅghya yasya nāḍīdvayaṃ vahet |
tadā tasya vijānīyādaśubhaṃ nātra saṃśayaḥ || 126 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yadā — when; anukramam — natural sequence; ullaṅghya — bypassing; yasya — whose; nāḍī-dvayaṃ — both Nadis; vahet — flows; tadā — then; tasya — for him; vijānīyāt — one should know; aśubhaṃ — inauspiciousness; na — no; atra — here; saṃśayaḥ — doubt.
**English Translation:**  
When a person's breath flows through both Nadis simultaneously, bypassing its natural alternating sequence, one should recognize it as an inauspicious sign for that person. There is no doubt about this.

---

---

### Verse 127
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
क्षणं वामं क्षणं दक्षं विषमं भावमादिशेत् ।
विपरीतं फलं ज्ञेयं ज्ञातव्यं च वरानने ॥ १२७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
kṣaṇaṃ vāmaṃ kṣaṇaṃ dakṣaṃ viṣamaṃ bhāvamādiśet |
viparītaṃ phalaṃ jñeyaṃ jñātavyaṃ ca varānane || 127 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
kṣaṇaṃ — a moment; vāmaṃ — left; kṣaṇaṃ — a moment; dakṣaṃ — right; viṣamaṃ — irregular; bhāvam — state; ādiśet — indicates; viparītaṃ — opposite/adverse; phalaṃ — result; jñeyaṃ — should be known; jñātavyaṃ — must be understood; ca — and; vara-ānane — O beautiful-faced one.
**English Translation:**  
When the breath shifts erratically—flowing left for a moment and right for a moment—it indicates an irregular state. O beautiful-faced one, it must be understood that this state yields adverse or opposite results.

---

---

### Verse 128
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
उभयोरेव संचारं विषवन्तं विदुर्बुधाः ।
न कुर्यात् क्रूरसौम्यानि तस्मिन् सर्वं विफलं भवेत् ॥ १२८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ubhayoreva saṃcāraṃ viṣavantaṃ vidurbudhāḥ |
na kuryāt krūrasaumyāni tasmin sarvaṃ viphalaṃ bhavet || 128 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ubhayoh — of both; eva — indeed; saṃcāraṃ — flow; viṣa-vantaṃ — poisonous; viduḥ — know; budhāḥ — the wise; na — not; kuryāt — should do; krūra — harsh; saumyāni — or gentle (actions); tasmin — in that (state); sarvaṃ — everything; viphalaṃ — fruitless; bhavet — becomes.
**English Translation:**  
The wise consider the simultaneous flow in both nostrils to be like poison. One should not perform either harsh or gentle actions during this time, as everything done in this state becomes fruitless.

---

---

### Verse 129
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीविते मरणे प्रश्ने लाभलाभे जयाजये ।
विषमे विपरीते च संस्मरेज्जगदीश्वरम् ॥ १२९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvite maraṇe praśne lābhālābhe jayājaye |
viṣame viparīte ca saṃsmarejjagadīśvaram || 129 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvite — regarding life; maraṇe — regarding death; praśne — in queries; lābha-alābhe — in profit and loss; jaya-ajaye — in victory and defeat; viṣame — in an irregular (flow); viparīte — in opposite (flow); ca — and; saṃsmaret — one should remember; jagat-īśvaram — the Lord of the Universe.
**English Translation:**  
During queries concerning life or death, profit or loss, and victory or defeat, if the breath is flowing irregularly or adversely, one should simply remember and pray to the Lord of the Universe.

---

---

### Verse 130
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ईश्वरचिन्तनं कार्यं योगाभ्यासादिकर्म च ।
अन्यत् तत्र न कर्तव्यं जयलाभसुखैषिभिः ॥ १३० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
īśvaracintanaṃ kāryaṃ yogābhyāsādikarma ca |
anyat tatra na kartavyaṃ jayalābhasukhaiṣibhiḥ || 130 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
īśvara — Lord; cintanaṃ — contemplating; kāryaṃ — should be done; yoga-abhyāsa-ādi — yoga practice, etc.; karma — action; ca — and; anyat — other; tatra — there (during Sushumna); na — not; kartavyaṃ — should be done; jaya — victory; lābha — profit; sukha — happiness; eṣibhiḥ — by those who desire.
**English Translation:**  
During this time (Sushumna flow), one should engage in contemplating the Lord and practicing Yoga. Those desiring victory, profit, and worldly happiness should not undertake any other tasks.

---

---

### Verse 131
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सूर्येण वहमानायां सुषुम्नायां च वा युजि ।
शापं दद्याद् वरं दद्यात् सर्वं भवति तदन्यथा ॥ १३१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sūryeṇa vahamānāyāṃ suṣumnāyāṃ ca vā yuji |
śāpaṃ dadyād varaṃ dadyāt sarvaṃ bhavati tadanyathā || 131 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sūryeṇa — with the Sun (Nadi); vahamānāyāṃ — while flowing; suṣumnāyāṃ — in Sushumna; ca — and; vā — or; yuji — when joined; śāpaṃ — curse; dadyāt — if one gives; varaṃ — boon; dadyāt — if one gives; sarvaṃ — all; bhavati — becomes; tat — that; anyathā — otherwise.
**English Translation:**  
If one gives a curse or grants a boon while the breath alternates continuously between the Sun Nadi and the Sushumna, all such utterances become otherwise (i.e., they fail to manifest and become fruitless).

---

---

### Verse 132
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
नाडीसंक्रमणे काले तत्त्वसंक्रमणेऽपि च ।
शुभं किंचिन्न कर्तव्यं पुण्यदानानि कोटिधा ॥ १३२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
nāḍīsaṃkramaṇe kāle tattvasaṃkramaṇe'pi ca |
śubhaṃ kiṃcinna kartavyaṃ puṇyadānāni koṭidhā || 132 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nāḍī — Nadi; saṃkramaṇe — during transition; kāle — time; tattva — elements; saṃkramaṇe — during transition; api — also; ca — and; śubhaṃ — auspicious; kiṃcit — anything; na — not; kartavyaṃ — should be done; puṇya — merits; dānāni — charities; koṭidhā — millions of times.
**English Translation:**  
During the time of transition from one Nadi to another, or during the transition between the Tattvas (elements), no auspicious worldly acts should be performed, nor should one even attempt to give millions in charity (as it yields no worldly benefit).

---

---

### Verse 133
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
विषमस्योदयो यत्र मनसाऽपि न चिन्तयेत् ।
यात्रा हानिकरी तस्य मृत्युः क्लेशो न संशयः ॥ १३३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
viṣamasyodayo yatra manasā'pi na cintayet |
yātrā hānikarī tasya mṛtyuḥ kleśo na saṃśayaḥ || 133 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
viṣamasya — of the irregular (flow); udayaḥ — rising; yatra — where; manasā — in mind; api — even; na — not; cintayet — one should think; yātrā — journey; hāni-karī — causing harm; tasya — of him; mṛtyuḥ — death; kleśaḥ — suffering; na — no; saṃśayaḥ — doubt.
**English Translation:**  
When the irregular flow (Sushumna) emerges, one should not even mentally contemplate any plans. A journey undertaken during this time causes harm, leading to suffering or even death; there is no doubt about this.

---

---

### Verse 134
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पुरो वामा ऊर्ध्वश्चन्द्रो दक्षोऽधः पृष्ठतो रविः ।
पूर्णरिक्तविवेकोऽयं ज्ञातव्यो देशिकैः सदा ॥ १३४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
puro vāmā ūrdhvaścandro dakṣo'dhaḥ pṛṣṭhato raviḥ |
pūrṇariktaviveko'yaṃ jñātavyo deśikaiḥ sadā || 134 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
puraḥ — in front; vāmā — left; ūrdhvaḥ — above; candraḥ — Moon; dakṣaḥ — right; adhaḥ — below; pṛṣṭhataḥ — behind; raviḥ — Sun; pūrṇa — full; rikta — empty; vivekaḥ — discrimination; ayaṃ — this; jñātavyaḥ — should be known; deśikaiḥ — by the preceptors; sadā — always.
**English Translation:**  
The Moon Nadi is considered full in the front, left, and upper directions, while the Sun Nadi is full on the right, below, and behind. This discrimination between full (active) and empty (inactive) sides should always be known by wise preceptors.

---

---

### Verse 135
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ऊर्ध्वं वामाग्रतो दूतो ज्ञेयो वामपथि स्थितः ।
पृष्ठे दक्षे तथाऽधस्तात् सूर्यवाहो गतः शुभः ॥ १३५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ūrdhvaṃ vāmāgrato dūto jñeyo vāmapathi sthitaḥ |
pṛṣṭhe dakṣe tathā'dhastāt sūryavāho gataḥ śubhaḥ || 135 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ūrdhvaṃ — above; vāma — left; agrataḥ — in front; dūtaḥ — messenger; jñeyaḥ — is to be known; vāma-pathi — on the left path (Moon Nadi); sthitaḥ — situated; pṛṣṭhe — behind; dakṣe — on the right; tathā — and; adhastāt — below; sūrya-vāhaḥ — flow of the Sun; gataḥ — gone; śubhaḥ — auspicious.
**English Translation:**  
If a messenger arrives and stands above, on the left, or in front while the left path (Moon Nadi) is active, it should be known as favorable. Similarly, if they stand behind, on the right, or below while the Sun Nadi is flowing, it is considered auspicious.

---

---

### Verse 136
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अनादिर्विषमः संधिर्निराहारो निरञ्जनः ।
परे सूक्ष्मे विलीयेत सा संध्या संधिरुच्यते ॥ १३६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
anādirviṣamaḥ saṃdhirnirāhāro nirañjanaḥ |
pare sūkṣme vilīyeta sā saṃdhyā saṃdhirucyate || 136 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
anādiḥ — beginningless; viṣamaḥ — irregular (Sushumna); saṃdhiḥ — junction; nirāhāraḥ — without sustenance; nirañjanaḥ — pure; pare — in the supreme; sūkṣme — subtle; vilīyeta — should merge; sā — that; saṃdhyā — twilight/transition; saṃdhiḥ — junction; ucyate — is called.
**English Translation:**  
This irregular transition (Sushumna flow) is beginningless, requires no sustenance, and is pure. When the mind merges into the supreme subtle reality during this time, that transition is truly called Sandhya (the sacred junction).

---

---

### Verse 137
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
न वेदं वेद इत्याहुर्वेदो वेदो न विद्यते ।
परात्मा विद्यते येन स वेदो वेद उच्यते ॥ १३७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
na vedaṃ veda ityāhurvedo vedo na vidyate |
parātmā vidyate yena sa vedo veda ucyate || 137 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
na — not; vedaṃ — the Veda; veda — Veda; iti — thus; āhuḥ — they say; vedaḥ — the Veda; vedaḥ — Veda; na — not; vidyate — exists; parātmā — the Supreme Soul; vidyate — is known; yena — by which; saḥ — that; vedaḥ — Veda; veda — Veda; ucyate — is called.
**English Translation:**  
The wise do not call the written texts the true Veda; the mere text is not the real Veda. That by which the Supreme Soul (Paramatman) is realized is alone called the true Veda.

---

---

### Verse 138
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
न संध्यां संधिरित्याहुः संध्या संधिर्निगद्यते ।
विषमः संधिगः प्राणः स संधिः संधिरुच्यते ॥ १३८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
na saṃdhyāṃ saṃdhirityāhuḥ saṃdhyā saṃdhirnigadyate |
viṣamaḥ saṃdhigaḥ prāṇaḥ sa saṃdhiḥ saṃdhirucyate || 138 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
na — not; saṃdhyāṃ — the twilight rituals; saṃdhiḥ — junction; iti — thus; āhuḥ — they say; saṃdhyā — twilight ritual; saṃdhiḥ — junction; nigadyate — is said; viṣamaḥ — irregular; saṃdhigaḥ — at the junction; prāṇaḥ — breath; saḥ — that; saṃdhiḥ — junction; saṃdhiḥ — true junction; ucyate — is called.
**English Translation:**  
The wise do not consider the mere external twilight rituals as the true Sandhi (junction). Rather, when the Prana enters the irregular junction (Sushumna), that transition alone is called the true Sandhi.

---

---

### Verse 139
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
श्रीदेव्युवाच ।
देवदेव महादेव सर्वसंसारतारक ।
स्थितं त्वदीयहृदये रहस्यं वद मे प्रभो ॥ १३९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śrīdevyuvāca |
devadeva mahādeva sarvasaṃsāratāraka |
sthitaṃ tvadīyahṛdaye rahasyaṃ vada me prabho || 139 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śrī-devī — Goddess Parvati; uvāca — said; deva-deva — O God of gods; mahādeva — O Great God; sarva — all; saṃsāra — worldly existence; tāraka — savior; sthitaṃ — situated; tvadīya — your; hṛdaye — in the heart; rahasyaṃ — secret; vada — tell; me — to me; prabho — O Lord.
**English Translation:**  
The Goddess Parvati said: O God of gods! O Mahadeva! O Savior from all worldly existence! O Lord, please reveal to me the supreme secret that is situated within your heart.

---

---

### Verse 140
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ईश्वर उवाच ।
स्वरज्ञानरहस्यात् तु न काचिद्विशेषदेवता ।
स्वरज्ञानरतो योगी स योगी परमो मतः ॥ १४० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
īśvara uvāca |
svarajñānarahasyāt tu na kācidviśeṣadevatā |
svarajñānarato yogī sa yogī paramo mataḥ || 140 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
īśvaraḥ — Lord Shiva; uvāca — said; svara-jñāna — knowledge of Svara (breath); rahasyāt — than the secret; tu — indeed; na — not; kācit — any; viśeṣa-devatā — greater deity; svara-jñāna-rataḥ — devoted to the knowledge of Svara; yogī — yogi; saḥ — that; yogī — yogi; paramaḥ — supreme; mataḥ — is considered.
**English Translation:**  
Lord Shiva said: There is no deity or secret more special than the secret knowledge of the Svara (breath). A Yogi who is deeply devoted to the knowledge of Svara is considered the supreme among all Yogis.

---

---

### Verse 141
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पञ्चतत्त्वाद्भवेत्सृष्टिस्तत्त्वे तत्त्वं प्रलीयते ।
पञ्चतत्त्वं परं तत्त्वं तत्त्वातीतं निरञ्जनम् ॥ १४१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pañcatattvādbhavetsṛṣṭistattve tattvaṃ pralīyate |
pañcatattvaṃ paraṃ tattvaṃ tattvātītaṃ nirañjanam || 141 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pañca-tattvāt - from the five elements; bhavet - arises; sṛṣṭiḥ - creation; tattve - in the element; tattvam - the element; pralīyate - dissolves/merges; pañca-tattvam - the five elements; param - the supreme; tattvam - reality; tattva-atītam - beyond the elements; nirañjanam - the spotless (Brahman).
**English Translation:**  
Creation arises from the five elements, and one element merges into another. The five elements are the supreme reality, but the spotless, pure Brahman is beyond these elements.

---

---

### Verse 142
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तत्त्वानां नामविज्ञेयं सिद्धियोगेन योगिनाम् ।
भूतानां दुष्टचिह्नानि जानातीदं स्वरोत्तमः ॥ १४२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tattvānāṃ nāmavijñeyaṃ siddhiyogena yoginām |
bhūtānāṃ duṣṭacihnāni jānātīdaṃ svarottamaḥ || 142 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tattvānām - of the elements; nāma - names; vijñeyam - should be known; siddhi-yogena - through the yoga of perfection; yoginām - of the yogis; bhūtānām - of living beings; duṣṭa-cihnāni - the inauspicious signs; jānāti - knows; idam - this; svara-uttamaḥ - the foremost knower of Svara.
**English Translation:**  
The yogis should know the names (and nature) of the elements through the yoga of perfection. The foremost knower of Svara (the science of breath) knows the inauspicious signs of living beings.

---

---

### Verse 143
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यापस्तथा तेजो वायुराकाशमेव च ।
पञ्चभूतात्मकं विश्वं यो जानाति स पूजितः ॥ १४३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāpastathā tejo vāyurākāśameva ca |
pañcabhūtātmakaṃ viśvaṃ yo jānāti sa pūjitaḥ || 143 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivī - earth; āpaḥ - water; tathā - and; tejaḥ - fire; vāyuḥ - air; ākāśam - ether/space; eva - indeed; ca - and; pañca-bhūta-ātmakam - composed of the five elements; viśvam - the universe; yaḥ - who; jānāti - knows; saḥ - he; pūjitaḥ - is honored.
**English Translation:**  
Earth, water, fire, air, and ether—he who knows the universe as being composed of these five elements is honored everywhere.

---

---

### Verse 144
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सर्वलोकस्थजीवानां न देहो भिन्नतत्त्वकः ।
भूलोकात्सत्यपर्यन्तं नाडीभेदः पृथक् पृथक् ॥ १४४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sarvalokasthajīvānāṃ na deho bhinnatattvakaḥ |
bhūlokātsatyaparyantaṃ nāḍībhedaḥ pṛthak pṛthak || 144 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sarva-loka-stha - residing in all realms; jīvānām - of living beings; na - not; dehaḥ - body; bhinna-tattvakaḥ - made of different elements; bhūlokāt - from the earth realm; satya-paryantam - up to the realm of truth (Satyaloka); nāḍī-bhedaḥ - the difference of nadis (channels); pṛthak pṛthak - is separate/different for each.
**English Translation:**  
For all living beings residing in all the worlds, the body is not composed of different elements. However, from the earthly realm up to the highest realm of truth, the arrangement and distinction of their subtle energy channels (nadis) vary individually.

---

---

### Verse 145
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामे वा दक्षिणे वापि उदयाः पञ्च कीर्तिताः ।
अष्टधा तत्त्वविज्ञानं शृणु वक्ष्यामि सुन्दरि ॥ १४५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāme vā dakṣiṇe vāpi udayāḥ pañca kīrtitāḥ |
aṣṭadhā tattvavijñānaṃ śṛṇu vakṣyāmi sundari || 145 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāme - in the left breath; vā - or; dakṣiṇe - in the right; vā api - or even; udayāḥ - risings/manifestations; pañca - five; kīrtitāḥ - are said; aṣṭadhā - eightfold; tattva-vijñānam - knowledge of the elements; śṛṇu - listen; vakṣyāmi - I will tell; sundari - O beautiful one.
**English Translation:**  
In either the left or the right breath, the risings (manifestations) of the five elements are recognized. Listen, O beautiful one, as I explain the eightfold knowledge of these elements.

---

---

### Verse 146
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रथमे तत्त्वसंख्यानं द्वितीये श्वाससन्धयः ।
तृतीये स्वरचिह्नानि चतुर्थे स्थानमेव च ॥ १४६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
prathame tattvasaṃkhyānaṃ dvitīye śvāsasandhayaḥ |
tṛtīye svaracihnāni caturthe sthānameva ca || 146 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
prathame - firstly; tattva-saṃkhyānam - the enumeration of elements; dvitīye - secondly; śvāsa-sandhayaḥ - the junctions of the breath; tṛtīye - thirdly; svara-cihnāni - the signs of the breath; caturthe - fourthly; sthānam - the location; eva - indeed; ca - and.
**English Translation:**  
Firstly, the enumeration of the elements; secondly, the junctions of the breath; thirdly, the identifying signs of the elements in the breath; and fourthly, their respective locations in the body.

---

---

### Verse 147
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पञ्चमे तत्त्ववर्णाश्च षष्ठे तु प्राण एव च ।
सप्तमे स्वादसंयुक्तमष्टमे गतिलक्षणम् ॥ १४७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pañcame tattvavarṇāśca ṣaṣṭhe tu prāṇa eva ca |
saptame svādasaṃyuktamaṣṭame gatilakṣaṇam || 147 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pañcame - fifthly; tattva-varṇāḥ - colors of the elements; ca - and; ṣaṣṭhe - sixthly; tu - indeed; prāṇaḥ - the vital breath itself; eva - indeed; ca - and; saptame - seventhly; svāda-saṃyuktam - associated with taste; aṣṭame - eighthly; gati-lakṣaṇam - the characteristic of their movement.
**English Translation:**  
Fifthly, the colors of the elements; sixthly, the nature of the vital breath itself; seventhly, their associated tastes; and eighthly, the characteristics of their movement.

---

---

### Verse 148
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
एवमष्टविधं प्राणं विश्वं तच्चराचरम् ।
स्वरात्परतरं देवि नान्यत्तत्त्वं शुभेक्षणे ॥ १४८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
evamaṣṭavidhaṃ prāṇaṃ viśvaṃ taccarācaram |
svarātparataraṃ devi nānyattattvaṃ śubhekṣaṇe || 148 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
evam - thus; aṣṭa-vidham - eightfold; prāṇam - vital breath; viśvam - the universe; tat - that; cara-acaram - the moving and non-moving; svarāt - than Svara (the science of breath); parataram - higher/greater; devi - O Goddess; na - not; anyat - other; tattvam - reality/knowledge; śubha-īkṣaṇe - O one with auspicious eyes.
**English Translation:**  
Thus, this vital breath in eight ways pervades the entire universe, both moving and non-moving. O Goddess with auspicious eyes, there is no knowledge higher than the science of Svara.

---

---

### Verse 149
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
निरीक्षितव्यं यत्नेन सदा प्रत्युषकालतः ।
कालस्य वञ्चनार्थाय कर्म कुर्वन्ति योगिनः ॥ १४९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
nirīkṣitavyaṃ yatnena sadā pratyuṣakālataḥ |
kālasya vañcanārthāya karma kurvanti yoginaḥ || 149 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nirīkṣitavyam - should be observed; yatnena - with effort; sadā - always; pratyuṣa-kālataḥ - from the time of early dawn; kālasya - of death/time; vañcana-arthāya - for the purpose of cheating/deceiving; karma - actions/practices; kurvanti - perform; yoginaḥ - yogis.
**English Translation:**  
It should be observed with great effort always from the time of early dawn. The yogis perform their practices in order to deceive time (death) through this knowledge.

---

---

### Verse 150
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
श्रोत्रयोरङ्गुष्ठकौ मध्याङ्गुल्यौ नासापुटद्वये ।
वक्त्रप्रान्तके चान्या अङ्गुलीर्दद्याच्च नेत्रयोः ॥ १५० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śrotrayoraṅguṣṭhakau madhyāṅgulyau nāsāpuṭadvaye |
vaktraprāntake cānyā aṅgulīrdadyācca netrayoḥ || 150 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śrotrayoḥ - on the two ears; aṅguṣṭhakau - the two thumbs; madhyāṅgulyau - the two middle fingers; nāsā-puṭa-dvaye - on the two nostrils; vaktra-prāntake - at the corners of the mouth; ca - and; anyāḥ - other; aṅgulīḥ - fingers; dadyāt - one should place; ca - and; netrayoḥ - on the two eyes.
**English Translation:**  
One should place the two thumbs on the ears, the two middle fingers on the nostrils, the other (ring and little) fingers at the corners of the mouth, and (the index fingers) on the eyes. (This refers to Shanmukhi Mudra).

---

---

### Verse 151
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अस्यान्तस्तु पृथिव्यादितत्त्वज्ञानं भवेत्क्रमात् ।
पीतश्वेतारुणश्यामैर्बिन्दुभिर्निरुपाधिकम् ॥ १५१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
asyāntastu pṛthivyāditattvajñānaṃ bhavetkramāt |
pītaśvetāruṇaśyāmairbindubhirnirupādhikam || 151 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
asya-antaḥ - inside this (practice); tu - indeed; pṛthivī-ādi - earth, etc.; tattva-jñānam - knowledge of the elements; bhavet - occurs; kramāt - sequentially; pīta-śveta-aruṇa-śyāmaiḥ - by yellow, white, red, and dark; bindubhiḥ - by dots/points of light; nirupādhikam - clear/without conditions.
**English Translation:**  
By practicing this, the distinct knowledge of the elements beginning with earth gradually arises clearly through the visualization of yellow, white, red, and dark points of light.

---

---

### Verse 152
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दर्पणेन स्वमालोक्य ततः श्वासं विनिक्षिपेत् ।
आकारैस्तु विजानीयात्तत्त्वभेदं विचक्षणः ॥ १५२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
darpaṇena svamālokya tataḥ śvāsaṃ vinikṣipet |
ākāraistu vijānīyāttattvabhedaṃ vicakṣaṇaḥ || 152 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
darpaṇena - on a mirror; svam - oneself; ālokya - having seen; tataḥ - then; śvāsam - breath; vinikṣipet - should exhale; ākāraiḥ - by the shapes (formed by condensation); tu - indeed; vijānīyāt - should know; tattva-bhedam - the distinction of elements; vicakṣaṇaḥ - the wise one.
**English Translation:**  
Looking at oneself in a mirror, one should exhale upon it. The wise one can then discern the distinct elements by the shapes formed by the vapor.

---

---

### Verse 153
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चतुरस्रं चार्धचन्द्रं त्रिकोणं वर्तुलं स्मृतम् ।
बिन्दुभिस्तु नभो ज्ञेयमाकारैस्तत्त्वलक्षणम् ॥ १५३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
caturasraṃ cārdhacandraṃ trikoṇaṃ vartulaṃ smṛtam |
bindubhistu nabho jñeyamākāraistattvalakṣaṇam || 153 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
caturasram - square; ca - and; ardha-candram - half-moon/crescent; trikoṇam - triangle; vartulam - circle; smṛtam - is considered; bindubhiḥ - by dots; tu - indeed; nabhaḥ - ether/space; jñeyam - should be known; ākāraiḥ - by shapes; tattva-lakṣaṇam - the characteristic sign of the elements.
**English Translation:**  
A square, a crescent, a triangle, and a circle are considered the signs of earth, water, fire, and air respectively. Ether should be known by a dot-like shape. These shapes are the characteristic signs of the elements.

---

---

### Verse 154
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मध्ये पृथ्वी ह्यधश्चापश्चोर्ध्वे वहति चानलः ।
तिर्यग्वायुप्रवाहश्च नभो वहति सङ्क्रमे ॥ १५४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
madhye pṛthvī hyadhaścāpaścordhve vahati cānalaḥ |
tiryagvāyupravāhaśca nabho vahati saṅkrame || 154 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
madhye - in the middle; pṛthvī - earth; hi - indeed; adhaḥ - downwards; ca - and; āpaḥ - water; ca - and; ūrdhve - upwards; vahati - flows; ca - and; analaḥ - fire; tiryak - obliquely/sideways; vāyu-pravāhaḥ - the flow of air; ca - and; nabhaḥ - ether; vahati - flows; saṅkrame - in the transition.
**English Translation:**  
The earth element flows in the middle, water flows downwards, and fire flows upwards. The flow of air is oblique, and ether flows during the transition (when breath shifts from one nostril to the other).

---

---

### Verse 155
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आपः श्वेताः क्षितिः पीता रक्तवर्णो हुताशनः ।
मारुतो नीलजीमूत आकाशः सर्ववर्णकः ॥ १५५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
āpaḥ śvetāḥ kṣitiḥ pītā raktavarṇo hutāśanaḥ |
māruto nīlajīmūta ākāśaḥ sarvavarṇakaḥ || 155 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āpaḥ - water; śvetāḥ - is white; kṣitiḥ - earth; pītā - is yellow; rakta-varṇaḥ - red colored; huta-aśanaḥ - fire; mārutaḥ - air; nīla-jīmūtaḥ - is like a dark blue cloud; ākāśaḥ - ether; sarva-varṇakaḥ - is of all colors (mixed).
**English Translation:**  
Water is white, earth is yellow, and fire is red. Air is dark blue like a rain cloud, and ether is of all colors.

---

---

### Verse 156
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
स्कन्धद्वये स्थितो वह्निर्नाभिमूले प्रभञ्जनः ।
जानुदेशे क्षितिस्तोयं पादान्ते मस्तके नभः ॥ १५६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
skandhadvaye sthito vahnirnābhimūle prabhañjanaḥ |
jānudeśe kṣitistoyaṃ pādānte mastake nabhaḥ || 156 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
skandha-dvaye - in the two shoulders; sthitaḥ - situated; vahniḥ - fire; nābhi-mūle - at the root of the navel; prabhañjanaḥ - air; jānu-deśe - in the region of the knees; kṣitiḥ - earth; toyam - water; pāda-ante - at the end of the feet; mastake - in the head; nabhaḥ - ether.
**English Translation:**  
Fire is situated in the two shoulders, and air at the root of the navel. Earth is located in the region of the knees, water in the feet, and ether in the head.

---

---

### Verse 157
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मृदियं मधुरस्वादा कषायं जलमेव च ।
तीक्ष्णं तेजः समीरोऽम्ल आकाशं कटुकं तथा ॥ १५७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
mṛdiyaṃ madhurasvādā kaṣāyaṃ jalameva ca |
tīkṣṇaṃ tejaḥ samīro'mla ākāśaṃ kaṭukaṃ tathā || 157 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
mṛt iyam - this earth; madhura-svādā - has a sweet taste; kaṣāyam - astringent; jalam - water; eva - indeed; ca - and; tīkṣṇam - pungent/sharp; tejaḥ - fire; samīraḥ - air; amlaḥ - sour; ākāśam - ether; kaṭukam - bitter; tathā - also.
**English Translation:**  
The earth element has a sweet taste, and water has an astringent taste. Fire is sharp and pungent, air is sour, and ether is bitter in taste.

---

---

### Verse 158
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अष्टाङ्गुलं वहेद्वायुरनलश्चतुरङ्गुलम् ।
द्वादशाङ्गुलं मृदियं वारुणं षोडशाङ्गुलम् ॥ १५८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
aṣṭāṅgulaṃ vahedvāyuranalaścaturaṅgulam |
dvādaśāṅgulaṃ mṛdiyaṃ vāruṇaṃ ṣoḍaśāṅgulam || 158 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
aṣṭa-aṅgulam - eight fingers (in length); vahet - flows; vāyuḥ - air; analaḥ - fire; catuḥ-aṅgulam - four fingers; dvādaśa-aṅgulam - twelve fingers; mṛt - earth; iyam - this; vāruṇam - water; ṣoḍaśa-aṅgulam - sixteen fingers.
**English Translation:**  
The breath flows to a distance of eight fingers in the air element, and four fingers in the fire element. The flow is twelve fingers long in the earth element, and sixteen fingers in the water element.

---

---

### Verse 159
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ऊर्ध्वे मृत्युरधः शान्तिस्तिर्यगुच्चाटनं तथा ।
मध्ये स्तम्भं विजानीयान्नभः सर्वं च मध्यमम् ॥ १५९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ūrdhve mṛtyuradhaḥ śāntistiryaguccāṭanaṃ tathā |
madhye stambhaṃ vijānīyānnabhaḥ sarvaṃ ca madhyamam || 159 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ūrdhve - in the upward flow (fire); mṛtyuḥ - death; adhaḥ - in the downward flow (water); śāntiḥ - peace; tiryak - in the oblique flow (air); uccāṭanam - eradication/driving away; tathā - and; madhye - in the middle flow (earth); stambham - stopping/paralyzing; vijānīyāt - one should know; nabhaḥ - ether; sarvam - all; ca - and; madhyamam - medium/neutral.
**English Translation:**  
One should know that the upward flow (fire) brings death (destruction), the downward flow (water) brings peace, the oblique flow (air) brings eradication, and the middle flow (earth) brings paralyzing (stagnation). The flow of ether yields neutral results in all matters.

---

---

### Verse 160
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यां स्थिरकर्माणि चरकर्माणि वारुणे ।
तेजसि क्रूरकर्माणि मारणोच्चाटनेऽनिले ॥ १६० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāṃ sthirakarmāṇi carakarmāṇi vāruṇe |
tejasi krūrakarmāṇi māraṇoccāṭane'nile || 160 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivyām - in earth; sthira-karmāṇi - stable/fixed actions; cara-karmāṇi - moving/dynamic actions; vāruṇe - in water; tejasi - in fire; krūra-karmāṇi - cruel/fierce actions; māraṇa-uccāṭane - killing and driving away; anile - in air.
**English Translation:**  
Stable actions should be performed during the predominance of the earth element, and dynamic actions during water. Fierce actions are appropriate during fire, and destructive acts like killing or driving away enemies are done during air.

---

---

### Verse 161
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ युद्धयात्रास्वरो विचारः —  
समरप्रवेशकाले तु पूर्णपादेन निर्गमेत् ।  
रिक्तेन शत्रुं सम्मुखं पराजयमवाप्नुयात् ॥ १६१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha yuddhayātrāsvaro vicāraḥ —*  
*samarapraveśakāle tu pūrṇapādena nirgamet |*  
*riktena śatruṁ sammukhaṁ parājayamavāpnuyāt || 161 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*atha yuddhayātrāsvaraḥ vicāraḥ* = evaluation of breath during battle and travel; *samarapraveśakāle tu* = when entering the battlefield; *pūrṇapādena nirgamet* = step forward with foot of active full side; *riktena śatrum sammukham* = facing enemy with empty side; *parājayam avāpnuyāt* = meets with defeat.

**English Translation:**  
**Swara Rules for Battle & Warfare:**  
When entering a battlefield or engaging an opponent, step forward with the foot corresponding to your **active nostril side (*Pūrṇa*)**. Facing an enemy with the empty, inactive side (*Rikta*) invites defeat and injury.

---

---

---

### Verse 162
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सूर्येण युध्यमानस्य विजयः स्यान्न संशयः ।  
चन्द्रेण सन्धिं कुर्वीत जयकाङ्क्षी नराधिपः ॥ १६२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*sūryeṇa yudhyamānasya vijayaḥ syānna saṁśayaḥ |*  
*candreṇa sandhiṁ kurvīta jayakāṅkṣī narādhipaḥ || 162 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*sūryeṇa yudhyamānasya* = fighting while right solar breath is active; *vijayaḥ syāt na saṁśayaḥ* = victory occurs without doubt; *candreṇa sandhim kurvīta* = while left lunar breath is active, make peace treaties; *jayakāṅkṣī narādhipaḥ* = a ruler desiring ultimate success.

**English Translation:**  
A warrior or ruler who engages in physical combat while the **right solar breath** (Piṅgalā) is active achieves swift, decisive victory without doubt. If the **left lunar breath** (Iḍā) is active, one should negotiate peace treaties and diplomacy.

---

---

---

### Verse 163
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूर्णनाडीं दिशं कृत्वा रिपुं स्थाप्य च शून्यगे ।  
संग्रामे क्रियमाणे तु विजयः स्यादसंशयम् ॥ १६३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*pūrṇanāḍīṁ diśaṁ kṛtvā ripuṁ sthāpya ca śūnyage |*  
*saṁgrāme kriyamāṇe tu vijayaḥ syādasaṁśayam || 163 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*pūrṇanāḍīm diśam kṛtvā* = keeping active nostril side toward opponent; *ripum sthāpya ca śūnyage* = forcing enemy onto practitioner's empty side; *saṁgrāme kriyamāṇe tu* = when battle is fought; *vijayaḥ syāt asaṁśayam* = victory is certain without doubt.

**English Translation:**  
In combat or competitive debate, position yourself so that your **active full side (*Pūrṇa*)** faces the opponent, while forcing the opponent onto your **empty inactive side (*Śūnya*)**. This disarms the adversary's vital energy and guarantees victory.

---

---

### Verse 164
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पीतः शनैर्मध्यवहो ज्ञेयो यावद्गुरुर्ध्वनिः ।
किञ्चिदुष्णः पार्थिवो वायुः स्थिरकार्यप्रसाधकः ॥ १६४ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pītaḥ śanairmadhyavaho jñeyo yāvadgururdhvaniḥ |
kiñciduṣṇaḥ pārthivo vāyuḥ sthirakāryaprasādhakaḥ || 164 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pītaḥ = yellow; śanaiḥ = slowly; madhyavahaḥ = flowing in the middle; jñeyaḥ = is to be known; yāvat = up to; guruḥ = heavy; dhvaniḥ = sound (reaching up to the chin); kiñcit = slightly; uṣṇaḥ = warm; pārthivaḥ = relating to the earth (element); vāyuḥ = breath/wind; sthira = stable; kārya = actions/tasks; prasādhakaḥ = accomplisher.

**English Translation:**  
The earth element breath (vāyu) should be known by its yellow color, slow flow in the middle of the nostril, slightly warm temperature, and a heavy sound reaching up to the chin. It is the accomplisher of all stable and permanent actions.

---

---

### Verse 165
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अधोवाही गुरुध्वानः शीघ्रगः शीतलः स्थितः ।
यः षोडशाङ्गुलो वायुः स आप्यः शुभकर्मकृत् ॥ १६५ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
adhovāhī gurudhvānaḥ śīghragaḥ śītalaḥ sthitaḥ |
yaḥ ṣoḍaśāṅgulo vāyuḥ sa āpyaḥ śubhakarmakṛt || 165 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
adho-vāhī = flowing downwards; guru-dhvānaḥ = having a heavy sound; śīghra-gaḥ = moving swiftly; śītalaḥ = cool; sthitaḥ = situated; yaḥ = which; ṣoḍaśa-aṅgulaḥ = sixteen fingers (in length); vāyuḥ = breath; saḥ = that; āpyaḥ = relating to water (element); śubha = auspicious; karma-kṛt = doer of actions.

**English Translation:**  
The water element breath flows downwards with a heavy sound, moves swiftly, is cool in nature, and extends up to sixteen fingers in length. It is favorable for accomplishing auspicious actions.

---

---

### Verse 166
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आवर्तगश्चोष्णतरो रक्ताभश्चतुरङ्गुलः ।
ऊर्ध्ववाही च यः क्रूरः कर्मकारी स तैजसः ॥ १६६ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
āvartagaścoṣṇataro raktābhaścaturaṅgulaḥ |
ūrdhvavāhī ca yaḥ krūraḥ karmakārī sa taijasaḥ || 166 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āvarta-gaḥ = moving in a circular motion (or up to the eyebrows); ca = and; uṣṇataraḥ = very hot; raktābhaḥ = reddish in appearance; caturaṅgulaḥ = four fingers (in length); ūrdhva-vāhī = flowing upwards; ca = and; yaḥ = which; krūraḥ = cruel/fierce; karma-kārī = doer of actions; saḥ = that; taijasaḥ = relating to fire (element).

**English Translation:**  
The fire element breath moves upward in a circular motion (reaching the eyebrows), is very hot, reddish in appearance, and flows up to a length of four fingers. It is associated with fierce or cruel actions.

---

---

### Verse 167
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
उष्णः शीतः कृष्णवर्णस्तिर्यग्गाम्यष्टाङ्गुलः ।
वायुः पवनसंज्ञस्तु चरकर्मप्रसाधकः ॥ १६७ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
uṣṇaḥ śītaḥ kṛṣṇavarṇastiryaggāmyaṣṭāṅgulaḥ |
vāyuḥ pavanasaṃjñastu carakarmaprasādhakaḥ || 167 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
uṣṇaḥ = hot; śītaḥ = cold; kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ = black in color; tiryak-gāmī = moving obliquely/diagonally; aṣṭa-aṅgulaḥ = eight fingers (in length); vāyuḥ = breath; pavana-saṃjñaḥ = known as the wind (element); tu = indeed; cara = moving/transitory; karma = actions; prasādhakaḥ = accomplisher.

**English Translation:**  
The air element breath is both hot and cold (lukewarm), black in color, moves obliquely, and extends up to eight fingers in length. It is indeed the accomplisher of all moving or transitory actions.

---

---

### Verse 168
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यः समीरः समरसः सर्वतत्त्वगुणावहः ।
आम्बरं तं विजानीयाद्योगिनां योगदायकम् ॥ १६८ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
yaḥ samīraḥ samarasaḥ sarvatattvaguṇāvahaḥ |
āmbaram taṃ vijānīyādyogināṃ yogadāyakam || 168 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yaḥ = which; samīraḥ = breath; samarasaḥ = of uniform essence/flow; sarva-tattva = all elements; guṇa = qualities; āvahaḥ = carrying; āmbaram = relating to space/ether; tam = that; vijānīyāt = one should know; yoginām = to the yogis; yoga-dāyakam = bestower of yoga/union.

**English Translation:**  
The breath that flows uniformly carrying the qualities of all elements should be known as the ether (space) element. It is the bestower of yoga (spiritual union) for the yogis.

---

---

### Verse 169
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पीतवर्णं चतुष्कोणं मधुरं मध्यमाश्रितम् ।
भोगदं पार्थिवं तत्त्वं प्रवाहे द्वादशाङ्गुलम् ॥ १६९ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pītavarṇaṃ catuṣkoṇaṃ madhuraṃ madhyamāśritam |
bhogadaṃ pārthivaṃ tattvaṃ pravāhe dvādaśāṅgulam || 169 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pīta-varṇam = yellow color; catuṣkoṇam = quadrangular/square shape; madhuram = sweet taste; madhyam-āśritam = flowing in the middle; bhoga-dam = bestowing enjoyment; pārthivam = earth; tattvam = element; pravāhe = in flow; dvādaśa-aṅgulam = twelve fingers.

**English Translation:**  
The earth element is yellow in color, square in shape, sweet in taste, and flows in the middle of the nostril. It grants worldly enjoyments and its flow extends up to twelve fingers in length.

---

---

### Verse 170
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
श्वेतमर्धेन्दुसङ्काशं स्वादु कषायमाद्रकम् ।
लाभकृद् वारुणं तत्त्वं प्रवाहे षोडशाङ्गुलम् ॥ १७० ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
śvetamardhendusaṅkāśaṃ svādu kaṣāyamādrakam |
lābhakṛd vāruṇaṃ tattvaṃ pravāhe ṣoḍaśāṅgulam || 170 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śvetam = white; ardha-indu-saṅkāśam = resembling a half-moon; svādu = tasty/sweet; kaṣāyam = astringent; ādrakam = moist/wet; lābha-kṛt = creating profit/gain; vāruṇam = relating to water (Varuna); tattvam = element; pravāhe = in flow; ṣoḍaśa-aṅgulam = sixteen fingers.

**English Translation:**  
The water element is white, shaped like a half-moon, has a mixed sweet and astringent taste, and is moist in nature. It brings profit and its flow extends up to sixteen fingers in length.

---

---

### Verse 171
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रक्तं त्रिकोणं तीक्ष्णं च ऊर्ध्वभागप्रवाहकम् ।
दीप्तं च तैजसं तत्त्वं प्रवाहे चतुरङ्गुलम् ॥ १७१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
raktaṃ trikoṇaṃ tīkṣṇaṃ ca ūrdhvabhāgapravāhakam |
dīptaṃ ca taijasaṃ tattvaṃ pravāhe caturaṅgulam || 171 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
raktam = red; trikoṇam = triangular; tīkṣṇam = sharp/pungent; ca = and; ūrdhva-bhāga = upward direction; pravāhakam = flowing; dīptam = luminous/bright; ca = and; taijasam = relating to fire; tattvam = element; pravāhe = in flow; caturaṅgulam = four fingers.

**English Translation:**  
The fire element is red, triangular in shape, sharp in taste, and flows upwards. It is bright and luminous, and its flow extends up to four fingers in length.

---

---

### Verse 172
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
नीलं च वर्तुलाकारं स्वादावम्लं तिर्यगाश्रितम् ।
चपलं मारुतं तत्त्वं प्रवाहेऽष्टाङ्गुलं स्मृतम् ॥ १७२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
nīlaṃ ca vartulākāraṃ svādāvamlaṃ tiryagāśritam |
capalaṃ mārutaṃ tattvaṃ pravāhe'ṣṭāṅgulaṃ smṛtam || 172 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nīlam = blue; ca = and; vartula-ākāram = circular shape; svādau = in taste; amlam = sour; tiryak = obliquely; āśritam = situated/flowing; capalam = restless/fickle; mārutam = relating to air; tattvam = element; pravāhe = in flow; aṣṭa-aṅgulam = eight fingers; smṛtam = is considered.

**English Translation:**  
The air element is blue, circular in shape, sour in taste, and flows obliquely. It is restless in nature and its flow is considered to extend up to eight fingers in length.

---

---

### Verse 173
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वर्णाकारस्वादवाहे अव्यक्तं सर्वगामि यत् ।
मोक्षदं नाभसं तत्त्वं सर्वकार्येषु निष्फलम् ॥ १७३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
varṇākārasvādavāhe avyaktaṃ sarvagāmi yat |
mokṣadaṃ nābhasaṃ tattvaṃ sarvakāryeṣu niṣphalam || 173 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
varṇa = color; ākāra = shape; svāda = taste; vāhe = in flow; avyaktam = unmanifest/indistinct; sarva-gāmi = all-pervading; yat = which; mokṣa-dam = bestowing liberation; nābhasam = relating to ether/space; tattvam = element; sarva-kāryeṣu = in all actions; niṣphalam = fruitless/ineffective.

**English Translation:**  
That which is indistinct in color, shape, taste, and flow, and is all-pervading, is the ether element. It bestows spiritual liberation but is fruitless for all worldly actions.

---

---

### Verse 174
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथ्वीजले शुभे तत्त्वे तेजो मिश्रफलोदयम् ।
हानिमृत्युकरौ पुंसां व्योममारुतावेव च ॥ १७४ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthvījale śubhe tattve tejo miśraphalodayam |
hānimṛtyukarau puṃsāṃ vyomamārutāveva ca || 174 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthvī-jale = earth and water; śubhe = auspicious; tattve = elements; tejaḥ = fire; miśra-phala-udayam = producing mixed results; hāni = loss; mṛtyu = death; karau = causers; puṃsām = for men; vyoma-mārutau = ether and air; eva = indeed; ca = and.

**English Translation:**  
The earth and water elements are auspicious, while the fire element produces mixed results. The ether and air elements cause loss and death for men.

---

---

### Verse 175
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आपूर्वं पश्चिमे पृथ्वी तेजश्च दक्षिणे तथा ।
वायुश्चोत्तरदिग्ज्ञेयो मध्ये कोणगतं नभः ॥ १७५ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
āpūrvaṃ paścime pṛthvī tejaśca dakṣiṇe tathā |
vāyuścottaradigjñeyo madhye koṇagataṃ nabhaḥ || 175 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āpūrvam = from the east; paścime = in the west; pṛthvī = earth; tejaḥ = fire; ca = and; dakṣiṇe = in the south; tathā = similarly; vāyuḥ = air; ca = and; uttara-dik = northern direction; jñeyaḥ = to be known; madhye = in the center; koṇa-gatam = situated in the corners; nabhaḥ = ether/space.

**English Translation:**  
The earth element is dominant from the East to the West, and fire in the South. The air element should be known in the North, while the ether element resides in the center and the corners.

---

---

### Verse 176
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रे पृथ्वीजले स्यातां सूर्येऽग्निस्तु यदा भवेत् ।
तदा सिद्धिर्न सन्देहः सौम्यासौम्येषु कर्मसु ॥ १७६ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
candre pṛthvījale syātāṃ sūrye'gnistu yadā bhavet |
tadā siddhirna sandehaḥ saumyāsaumyeṣu karmasu || 176 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candre = in the moon (left nostril); pṛthvī-jale = earth and water; syātām = should be; sūrye = in the sun (right nostril); agniḥ = fire; tu = indeed; yadā = when; bhavet = is present; tadā = then; siddhiḥ = success; na = no; sandehaḥ = doubt; saumya-asaumyeṣu = in gentle and fierce; karmasu = actions.

**English Translation:**  
When the earth or water elements are active during the lunar flow (left nostril), or when the fire element is active during the solar flow (right nostril), success is achieved in both gentle and fierce actions without any doubt.

---

---

### Verse 177
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
लाभः पृथ्वीकृतोऽह्नि स्यान्निशायां जलतः शुभम् ।
वह्नौ मृत्युः क्षयो वायौ नभः स्थानं दहेत् क्वचित् ॥ १७७ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
lābhaḥ pṛthvīkṛto'hni syānniśāyāṃ jalataḥ śubham |
vahnau mṛtyuḥ kṣayo vāyau nabhaḥ sthānaṃ dahet kvacit || 177 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
lābhaḥ = profit/gain; pṛthvī-kṛtaḥ = done by earth; ahni = in the day; syāt = is; niśāyām = in the night; jalataḥ = from water; śubham = auspiciousness; vahnau = in fire; mṛtyuḥ = death; kṣayaḥ = loss/destruction; vāyau = in air; nabhaḥ = ether; sthānam = place; dahet = burns; kvacit = sometimes.

**English Translation:**  
During the day, the earth element brings profit, and at night, the water element brings auspiciousness. The fire element brings death, the air element causes loss, and the ether element sometimes burns down one's very position.

---

---

### Verse 178
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवितव्ये जये लाभे कृष्यां च धनकर्मणि ।
मन्त्रार्थे युद्धप्रश्ने च गमनागमने तथा ॥ १७८ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvitavye jaye lābhe kṛṣyāṃ ca dhanakarmaṇi |
mantrārthe yuddhapraśne ca gamanāgamane tathā || 178 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvitavye = concerning life; jaye = in victory; lābhe = in profit; kṛṣyām = in agriculture; ca = and; dhana-karmaṇi = in wealth-related acts; mantra-arthe = in the purpose of mantras; yuddha-praśne = in questions regarding war; ca = and; gamana-āgamane = in going and coming; tathā = similarly.

**English Translation:**  
In matters of life, victory, profit, agriculture, wealth-related activities, achieving the purpose of mantras, questions concerning war, as well as in going and coming (the earth element is auspicious).

---

---

### Verse 179
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आयाति वारुणे तत्त्वे शत्रुरस्ति शुभः क्षितौ ।
प्रयाति वायुतोऽन्यत्र हानिमृत्युर्नभोनले ॥ १७९ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
āyāti vāruṇe tattve śatrurasti śubhaḥ kṣitau |
prayāti vāyuto'nyatra hānimṛtyurnabhonale || 179 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āyāti = comes; vāruṇe = in water; tattve = element; śatruḥ = enemy; asti = is (present); śubhaḥ = auspicious/favorable; kṣitau = in earth; prayāti = goes away; vāyutaḥ = due to air; anyatra = elsewhere; hāni-mṛtyuḥ = loss and death; nabha-anale = in ether and fire.

**English Translation:**  
If the water element is active, the enemy is arriving. The earth element is favorable indicating the enemy is staying put. In the air element, the enemy goes elsewhere, and in the ether and fire elements, there will be loss and death.

---

---

### Verse 180
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यां मूलचिन्ता स्याज्जीवस्य जलवातयोः ।
तेजसा धातुचिन्ता स्याच्छून्यमाकाशतो वदेत् ॥ १८० ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāṃ mūlacintā syājjīvasya jalavātayoḥ |
tejasā dhātucintā syācchūnyamākāśato vadet || 180 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivyām = in earth; mūla-cintā = thought of roots (plant life); syāt = is; jīvasya = of living beings; jala-vātayoḥ = in water and air; tejasā = by fire; dhātu-cintā = thought of metals/minerals; syāt = is; śūnyam = empty/nothing; ākāśataḥ = from ether; vadet = one should speak.

**English Translation:**  
In the earth element, a question relates to plant life (roots). In water and air elements, it relates to living beings. In the fire element, the thought is about metals or minerals, and in the ether element, one should predict emptiness (no specific thought).

---

---

### Verse 181
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यां बहुपादाः स्युर्द्विपदस्तोयवायुतः ।
तेजस्येव चतुष्पादो नभसा पादवर्जितः ॥ १८१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāṃ bahupādāḥ syurdvipadastoyavāyutaḥ |
tejasyeva catuṣpādo nabhasā pādavarjitaḥ || 181 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivyām = in earth; bahu-pādāḥ = many-footed (creatures/companions); syuḥ = are; dvi-padaḥ = two-footed; toya-vāyutaḥ = from water and air; tejasi = in fire; eva = indeed; catuṣpādaḥ = four-footed; nabhasā = by ether; pāda-varjitaḥ = footless.

**English Translation:**  
During the earth element, movement involves many feet (traveling with a group). In water and air elements, it involves two feet (traveling alone). In the fire element, it is four-footed (two people), and in the ether element, it is footless (no movement).

---

---

### Verse 182
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
कुजो वह्नौ रविः पृथ्व्यां सौरिरापः प्रकीर्तितः ।
वायुस्थानस्थितो राहुर्दक्षिणस्वरवाहकः ॥ १८२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
kujo vahnau raviḥ pṛthvyāṃ saurirāpaḥ prakīrtitaḥ |
vāyusthānasthito rāhurdakṣiṇasvaravāhakaḥ || 182 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
kujaḥ = Mars; vahnau = in fire; raviḥ = Sun; pṛthvyām = in earth; sauriḥ = Saturn; āpaḥ = water; prakīrtitaḥ = is proclaimed; vāyu-sthāna-sthitaḥ = situated in the place of air; rāhuḥ = Rahu; dakṣiṇa-svara-vāhakaḥ = flowing in the right nostril.

**English Translation:**  
When the right breath flows, Mars resides in the fire element, the Sun in earth, Saturn in water, and Rahu is proclaimed to be situated in the air element.

---

---

### Verse 183
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जले चन्द्रो बुधः पृथ्व्यां गुरुर्वाते सितोऽनले ।
वामनाड्यां स्थिताः सर्वे सर्वकार्येषु निश्चिताः ॥ १८३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
jale candro budhaḥ pṛthvyāṃ gururvāte sito'nale |
vāmanāḍyāṃ sthitāḥ sarve sarvakāryeṣu niścitāḥ || 183 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jale = in water; candraḥ = Moon; budhaḥ = Mercury; pṛthvyām = in earth; guruḥ = Jupiter; vāte = in air; sitaḥ = Venus; anale = in fire; vāma-nāḍyām = in the left nostril; sthitāḥ = situated; sarve = all (these); sarva-kāryeṣu = in all actions; niścitāḥ = ascertained.

**English Translation:**  
In the left nostril's flow, the Moon is in the water element, Mercury in earth, Jupiter in air, and Venus in fire. These are ascertained to be situated thus for all activities.

---

---

### Verse 184
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथ्व्यां बुधो जले चन्द्रः शुक्रो वह्नौ रविः कुजः ।
वायौ राहुः शनी व्योम्नि गुरुरेव प्रकीर्तितः ॥ १८४ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthvyāṃ budho jale candraḥ śukro vahnau raviḥ kujaḥ |
vāyau rāhuḥ śanī vyomni gurureva prakīrtitaḥ || 184 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthvyām = in earth; budhaḥ = Mercury; jale = in water; candraḥ = Moon; śukraḥ = Venus; vahnau = in fire; raviḥ = Sun; kujaḥ = Mars; vāyau = in air; rāhuḥ = Rahu; śanī = Saturn; vyomni = in ether; guruḥ = Jupiter; eva = indeed; prakīrtitaḥ = is proclaimed.

**English Translation:**  
It is proclaimed that Mercury presides in the earth element, the Moon in water, Venus, Sun and Mars in fire, Rahu and Saturn in air, and Jupiter in the ether element.

---

---

### Verse 185
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रवासप्रश्ने चादित्ये यदि राहुर्गतोऽनिले ।
तदा स चलितो ज्ञेयः स्थानान्तरमपेक्षते ॥ १८५ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pravāsapraśne cāditye yadi rāhurgato'nile |
tadā sa calito jñeyaḥ sthānāntaramapekṣate || 185 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pravāsa-praśne = in a question regarding travel; ca = and; āditye = in the solar flow (right nostril); yadi = if; rāhuḥ = Rahu; gataḥ = goes; anile = in the air element; tadā = then; saḥ = he (the traveler); calitaḥ = has moved; jñeyaḥ = should be known; sthāna-antaram = another place; apekṣate = is seeking/desiring.

**English Translation:**  
Regarding questions of a traveler, if the right nostril flows and Rahu is in the air element, it should be known that the person has moved from his previous location and is seeking another place.

---

---

### Verse 186
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आयाति वारुणे तत्त्वे तत्रैवास्ति शुभं क्षितौ ।
प्रवासी पवनेऽन्यत्र मृत्युरेवानले भवेत् ॥ १८६ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
āyāti vāruṇe tattve tatraivāsti śubhaṃ kṣitau |
pravāsī pavane'nyatra mṛtyurevānale bhavet || 186 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āyāti = comes; vāruṇe = in water; tattve = element; tatra-eva-asti = is right there; śubham = auspicious/well; kṣitau = in earth; pravāsī = the traveler; pavane = in air; anyatra = elsewhere; mṛtyuḥ = death; eva = indeed; anale = in fire; bhavet = happens.

**English Translation:**  
If the water element is active, the traveler is returning. If the earth element is active, he is safely staying right there. In the air element, the traveler has moved elsewhere, and in the fire element, his death is certain.

---

---

### Verse 187
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पार्थिवे मूलविज्ञानं शुभं कार्यं जले तथा ।
आग्नेये धातुविज्ञानं व्योम्नि शून्यं विनिर्दिशेत् ॥ १८७ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pārthive mūlavijñānaṃ śubhaṃ kāryaṃ jale tathā |
āgneye dhātuvijñānaṃ vyomni śūnyaṃ vinirdiśet || 187 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pārthive = in earth; mūla-vijñānam = knowledge of plant/root matters; śubham = auspicious; kāryam = work; jale = in water; tathā = similarly; āgneye = in fire; dhātu-vijñānam = knowledge of metals/minerals; vyomni = in ether; śūnyam = emptiness/nothingness; vinirdiśet = one should indicate.

**English Translation:**  
In the earth element, predict matters relating to plant life (roots). In water, indicate auspicious actions. In fire, it pertains to metals or minerals, and in the ether element, one should predict complete emptiness.

---

---

### Verse 188
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तुष्टिः पुष्टिर्द्रतिः क्रीडा जयहर्षौ धराजले ।
तेजोवाय्वोश्च सन्तापो ज्वरकंपः प्रवासिनः ॥ १८८ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭirdratiḥ krīḍā jayaharṣau dharājale |
tejovāyvośca santāpo jvarakaṃpaḥ pravāsinaḥ || 188 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tuṣṭiḥ = satisfaction; puṣṭiḥ = nourishment; ratiḥ = delight/love; krīḍā = play; jaya-harṣau = victory and joy; dharā-jale = in earth and water; tejaḥ-vāyvoḥ = in fire and air; ca = and; santāpaḥ = sorrow/affliction; jvara-kaṃpaḥ = fever and trembling; pravāsinaḥ = of the traveler.

**English Translation:**  
When earth and water elements are active, predict satisfaction, nourishment, delight, playfulness, victory, and joy. But in fire and air elements, the traveler suffers from sorrow, fever, and trembling.

---

---

### Verse 189
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गतायुर्मृत्युराकाशे तत्त्वस्थाने प्रकीर्तिता ।
द्वादशैते प्रयत्नेन ज्ञातव्या दैशिकैः सदा ॥ १८९ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
gatāyurmṛtyurākāśe tattvasthāne prakīrtitā |
dvādaśaite prayatnena jñātavyā daiśikaiḥ sadā || 189 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
