gata-āyuḥ = life span exhausted; mṛtyuḥ = death; ākāśe = in ether; tattva-sthāne = in the position of the element; prakīrtitā = is declared; dvādaśa-ete = these twelve (questions); prayatnena = with effort; jñātavyāḥ = should be known; daiśikaiḥ = by the astrologers/guides; sadā = always.

**English Translation:**  
If the ether element is active, it is declared that the traveler's life span has ended and death has occurred. These twelve types of elemental divinations must always be carefully studied by the wise guides.

---

---

### Verse 190
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूर्वायां पश्चिमे याम्ये उत्तरस्यां यथाक्रमम् ।
पृथिव्यादीनि भूतानि बलिष्ठानि विनिर्दिशेत् ॥ १९० ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrvāyāṃ paścime yāmye uttarasyāṃ yathākramam |
pṛthivyādīni bhūtāni baliṣṭhāni vinirdiśet || 190 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrvāyām = in the East; paścime = in the West; yāmye = in the South; uttarasyām = in the North; yathā-kramam = in respective order; pṛthivyādīni = earth and others; bhūtāni = elements; baliṣṭhāni = strongest; vinirdiśet = one should declare.

**English Translation:**  
One should declare that the elements beginning with earth (earth, water, fire, and air) are respectively the strongest in the East, West, South, and North directions.

---

---

### Verse 191
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यापस्तथा तेजो वायुराकाशमेव च ।
पञ्चभूतात्मको देहो ज्ञातव्यश्च वरानने ॥ १९१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāpastathā tejo vāyurākāśameva ca |
pañcabhūtātmako deho jñātavyaśca varānane || 191 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivī = earth; āpaḥ = water; tathā = also; tejaḥ = fire; vāyuḥ = air; ākāśam = ether; eva = indeed; ca = and; pañca-bhūta = five elements; ātmakaḥ = made of; dehaḥ = body; jñātavyaḥ = should be known; ca = and; vara-ānane = O beautiful-faced one.

**English Translation:**  
Earth, water, fire, air, and ether—these are the five elements. O beautiful-faced one, you should know that this physical body is entirely composed of these five elements.

---

---

### Verse 192
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अस्थि मांसं त्वचा नाडी रोम चैव तु पञ्चमम् ।
पृथ्वी पञ्चगुणा प्रोक्ता ब्रह्मज्ञानेन भाषिता ॥ १९२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
asthi māṃsaṃ tvacā nāḍī roma caiva tu pañcamam |
pṛthvī pañcaguṇā proktā brahmajñānena bhāṣitā || 192 ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
asthi = bone; māṃsam = flesh; tvacā = skin; nāḍī = nerves/veins; roma = hair; ca = and; eva = indeed; tu = and; pañcamam = the fifth; pṛthvī = earth; pañca-guṇā = having five attributes; proktā = is said; brahma-jñānena = by the knowledge of Brahman / by the knowers of Brahman; bhāṣitā = spoken.

**English Translation:**  
Bone, flesh, skin, nerves, and the fifth is hair. The earth element is thus said to possess these five attributes, as spoken by the knowers of Brahman.

---

---

### Verse 193
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
शुक्रं शोणितं मज्जा मूत्रं स्वेदं च पञ्चमम् ।
आपः पञ्चगुणाः प्रोक्ता ब्रह्मज्ञानेन भाषितम् ॥ 193 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
śukraṃ śoṇitaṃ majjā mūtraṃ svedaṃ ca pañcamam |
āpaḥ pañcaguṇāḥ proktā brahmajñānena bhāṣitam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
śukraṃ - semen; śoṇitaṃ - blood; majjā - marrow; mūtraṃ - urine; svedaṃ - sweat; ca - and; pañcamam - the fifth; āpaḥ - water element; pañcaguṇāḥ - having five qualities; proktā - said; brahmajñānena - by the knowledge of Brahman / by the knowers of Brahman; bhāṣitam - spoken.

**English Translation:**
Semen, blood, marrow, urine, and sweat as the fifth—these are the five qualities of the water element, as spoken by the knowers of Brahman.

---

---

### Verse 194
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
क्षुधा तृषा तथा निद्रा कान्तिरालस्यमेव च ।
तेजः पञ्चगुणं प्रोक्तं ब्रह्मज्ञानेन भाषितम् ॥ 194 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
kṣudhā tṛṣā tathā nidrā kāntirālasyameva ca |
tejaḥ pañcaguṇaṃ proktaṃ brahmajñānena bhāṣitam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
kṣudhā - hunger; tṛṣā - thirst; tathā - as well as; nidrā - sleep; kāntiḥ - luster/complexion; ālasyam - laziness; eva ca - and also; tejaḥ - fire element; pañcaguṇaṃ - having five qualities; proktaṃ - said; brahmajñānena - by the knowledge of Brahman; bhāṣitam - spoken.

**English Translation:**
Hunger, thirst, sleep, luster, and laziness—these are the five qualities of the fire element, as declared by the knowledge of Brahman.

---

---

### Verse 195
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
धावनं चलनं ग्रन्थिः सङ्कोचनप्रसारणे ।
वायोः पञ्चगुणाः प्रोक्ता ब्रह्मज्ञानेन भाषितम् ॥ 195 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
dhāvanaṃ calanaṃ granthiḥ saṅkocanaprasāraṇe |
vāyoḥ pañcaguṇāḥ proktā brahmajñānena bhāṣitam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
dhāvanaṃ - running; calanaṃ - walking; granthiḥ - forming a knot/stretching; saṅkocana - contracting; prasāraṇe - and expanding; vāyoḥ - of the air element; pañcaguṇāḥ - five qualities; proktā - said; brahmajñānena - by the knowledge of Brahman; bhāṣitam - spoken.

**English Translation:**
Running, walking, stretching (forming knots), contracting, and expanding—these are the five qualities of the air element, as stated by the knowers of Brahman.

---

---

### Verse 196
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
रागद्वेषौ तथा लज्जा भयं मोहश्च पञ्चमः ।
नभः पञ्चगुणं प्रोक्तं ब्रह्मज्ञानेन भाषितम् ॥ 196 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
rāgadveṣau tathā lajjā bhayaṃ mohaśca pañcamaḥ |
nabhaḥ pañcaguṇaṃ proktaṃ brahmajñānena bhāṣitam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
rāgadveṣau - attachment and aversion; tathā - and; lajjā - shame/modesty; bhayaṃ - fear; mohaśca - and delusion; pañcamaḥ - the fifth; nabhaḥ - ether/space element; pañcaguṇaṃ - having five qualities; proktaṃ - said; brahmajñānena - by the knowledge of Brahman; bhāṣitam - spoken.

**English Translation:**
Attachment, aversion, shame, fear, and delusion as the fifth—these are the five qualities of the space element, as described by the knowers of Brahman.

---

---

### Verse 197
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
पृथिव्याः पलानि पञ्चाशच्चत्वारिंशत्तथाम्भसः ।
अग्नेस्त्रिंशत्पुनर्वायोर्विंशतिर्नभसो दश ॥ 197 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
pṛthivyāḥ palāni pañcāśaccatvāriṃśattathāmbhasaḥ |
agnestriṃśatpunarvāyorviṃśatirnabhaso daśa ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
pṛthivyāḥ - of earth; palāni - measures (palas); pañcāśat - fifty; catvāriṃśat - forty; tathā - thus; ambhasaḥ - of water; agneḥ - of fire; triṃśat - thirty; punaḥ - then; vāyoḥ - of air; viṃśatiḥ - twenty; nabhasaḥ - of space; daśa - ten.

**English Translation:**
The measure of the Earth element is fifty palas, Water is forty, Fire is thirty, Air is twenty, and Space is ten.

---

---

### Verse 198
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
पृथिव्यां चिरकालेन लाभ आपः क्षणाद्भवेत् ।
जायते पवने स्वल्पः सिद्धोऽप्यग्नौ विनश्यति ॥ 198 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
pṛthivyāṃ cirakālena lābha āpaḥ kṣaṇādbhavet |
jāyate pavane svalpaḥ siddho'pyagnau vinaśyati ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
pṛthivyāṃ - in the earth element; cirakālena - after a long time; lābhaḥ - profit/gain; āpaḥ - in water element; kṣaṇāt - instantly/in a moment; bhavet - happens; jāyate - arises; pavane - in the air element; svalpaḥ - very little; siddhaḥ api - even that which is accomplished; agnau - in fire element; vinaśyati - is destroyed.

**English Translation:**
In the Earth element, profit comes after a long time; in Water, it happens instantly. In Air, the gain is very little, and in Fire, even what has been accomplished is destroyed.

---

---

### Verse 199
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
पृथिव्याः पञ्च चत्वार आपो गुणास्तेजसस्त्रयः ।
वायोर्द्वि नभसस्त्वेकं तत्त्वज्ञानमिदं भवेत् ॥ 199 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
pṛthivyāḥ pañca catvāra āpo guṇāstejasastrayaḥ |
vāyordvi nabhasastvekaṃ tattvajñānamidaṃ bhavet ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
pṛthivyāḥ - of earth; pañca - five; catvāra - four; āpaḥ - of water; guṇāḥ - qualities; tejasaḥ - of fire; trayaḥ - three; vāyoḥ - of air; dvi - two; nabhasaḥ tu - and of space; ekaṃ - one; tattvajñānam - knowledge of elements; idaṃ - this; bhavet - is/should be.

**English Translation:**
Earth has five qualities, Water has four, Fire has three, Air has two, and Space has one. This is the true knowledge of the elements.

---

---

### Verse 200
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
फूत्कारकृत् स्फुटिता विदीर्णा पतिता धरा ।
ददाति सर्वकार्येषु अवस्थासदृशं फलम् ॥ 200 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
phūtkārakṛt sphuṭitā vidīrṇā patitā dharā |
dadāti sarvakāryeṣu avasthāsadṛśaṃ phalam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
phūtkārakṛt - making a blowing sound; sphuṭitā - burst/cracked; vidīrṇā - torn apart; patitā - fallen; dharā - earth; dadāti - gives; sarvakāryeṣu - in all actions; avasthāsadṛśaṃ - according to its state; phalam - result/fruit.

**English Translation:**
Earth that is hissing, cracked, torn apart, or fallen gives results in all actions corresponding exactly to its state.

---

---

### Verse 201
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
धनिष्ठारोहिणीज्येष्ठानुराधाश्रवणं तथा ।
अभिजित् उत्तराषाढा पृथिवीतत्त्वमुदाहृतम् ॥ 201 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
dhaniṣṭhārohiṇījyeṣṭhānurādhāśravaṇaṃ tathā |
abhijit uttarāṣāḍhā pṛthivītattvamudāhṛtam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
dhaniṣṭhā - Dhaniṣṭhā; rohiṇī - Rohiṇī; jyeṣṭhā - Jyeṣṭhā; anurādhā - Anurādhā; śravaṇaṃ - Śravaṇa; tathā - and; abhijit - Abhijit; uttarāṣāḍhā - Uttarāṣāḍhā; pṛthivītattvam - the earth element; udāhṛtam - is called/exemplified.

**English Translation:**
The constellations of Dhaniṣṭhā, Rohiṇī, Jyeṣṭhā, Anurādhā, Śravaṇa, Abhijit, and Uttarāṣāḍhā are said to belong to the Earth element.

---

---

### Verse 202
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
पूर्वाषाढा तथाश्लेषा मूलमार्द्रा च रेवती ।
उत्तराभाद्रपदा तोयतत्त्वं शतभिषक् प्रिये ॥ 202 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
pūrvāṣāḍhā tathāśleṣā mūlamārdrā ca revatī |
uttarābhādrapadā toyatattvaṃ śatabhiṣak priye ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
pūrvāṣāḍhā - Pūrvāṣāḍhā; tathā - and; aśleṣā - Aśleṣā; mūlam - Mūla; ārdrā - Ārdrā; ca - and; revatī - Revatī; uttarābhādrapadā - Uttarābhādrapadā; toyatattvaṃ - water element; śatabhiṣak - Śatabhiṣak; priye - O beloved.

**English Translation:**
O beloved, Pūrvāṣāḍhā, Aśleṣā, Mūla, Ārdrā, Revatī, Uttarābhādrapadā, and Śatabhiṣak belong to the Water element.

---

---

### Verse 203
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
भरणी कृत्तिका पुष्यो मघा पूर्वा च फल्गुनी ।
पूर्वाभाद्रपदा स्वाती तेजस्तत्त्वमिति प्रिये ॥ 203 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
bharaṇī kṛttikā puṣyo maghā pūrvā ca phalgunī |
pūrvābhādrapadā svātī tejastattvamiti priye ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
bharaṇī - Bharaṇī; kṛttikā - Kṛttikā; puṣyaḥ - Puṣya; maghā - Maghā; pūrvā ca phalgunī - and Pūrvāphalgunī; pūrvābhādrapadā - Pūrvābhādrapadā; svātī - Svātī; tejastattvam - fire element; iti - thus; priye - O beloved.

**English Translation:**
Bharaṇī, Kṛttikā, Puṣya, Maghā, Pūrvāphalgunī, Pūrvābhādrapadā, and Svātī are known as the Fire element, O beloved.

---

---

### Verse 204
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
विशाखोत्तरफल्गुन्यौ हस्तचित्रे पुनर्वसू ।
अश्विनी मृगशीर्षं च वायुतत्त्वमुदाहृतम् ॥ 204 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
viśākhottaraphalgunyau hastacitre punarvasū |
aśvinī mṛgaśīrṣaṃ ca vāyutattvamudāhṛtam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
viśākhā-uttaraphalgunyau - Viśākhā and Uttaraphalgunī; hasta-citre - Hasta and Citrā; punarvasū - Punarvasu; aśvinī - Aśvinī; mṛgaśīrṣaṃ ca - and Mṛgaśīrṣa; vāyutattvam - air element; udāhṛtam - is said.

**English Translation:**
Viśākhā, Uttaraphalgunī, Hasta, Citrā, Punarvasu, Aśvinī, and Mṛgaśīrṣa are described as the Air element.

---

---

### Verse 205
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
वहतन्नाडीस्थितो दूतो यत्पृच्छति शुभाशुभम् ।
तत्सर्वं सिद्धिमाप्नोति शून्ये शून्यं न संशयः ॥ 205 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
vahatannāḍīsthito dūto yatpṛcchati śubhāśubham |
tatsarvaṃ siddhimāpnoti śūnye śūnyaṃ na saṃśayaḥ ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
vahat-nāḍī-sthitaḥ - situated on the side of the flowing Nadi; dūtaḥ - messenger; yat - whatever; pṛcchati - asks; śubhāśubham - auspicious or inauspicious; tat - that; sarvaṃ - all; siddhim - success; āpnoti - attains; śūnye - on the empty side; śūnyaṃ - empty (fails); na - no; saṃśayaḥ - doubt.

**English Translation:**
If a messenger stands on the side of the flowing Nadi and asks about anything auspicious or inauspicious, it all meets with success. If on the empty side, the result is empty; there is no doubt about this.

---

---

### Verse 206
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
पूर्णोऽपि निर्गमे श्वासे स्वतत्त्वेऽपि न सिद्धिदः ।
सूर्यश्चन्द्रोऽथवा नॄणां सङ्ग्रहे सर्वसिद्धिदः ॥ 206 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
pūrṇo'pi nirgame śvāse svatattve'pi na siddhidaḥ |
sūryaścandro'thavā nṝṇāṃ saṅgrahe sarvasiddhidaḥ ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
pūrṇaḥ api - even if full; nirgame - during exhalation; śvāse - breath; svatattve api - even in its own element; na - not; siddhidaḥ - giving success; sūryaḥ - the sun (pingala); candraḥ - the moon (ida); athavā - or; nṝṇāṃ - of men; saṅgrahe - in inhalation/accumulation; sarvasiddhidaḥ - bestower of all success.

**English Translation:**
Even if the Nadi is full and in its proper element, if the breath is exhaling, it does not grant success. But the sun or moon Nadi, when inhaling, bestows all success upon men.

---

---

### Verse 207
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
स्वतत्त्वे रामो जयं प्राप्तः स्वतत्त्वे च धनञ्जयः ।
कौरवा निहताः सर्वे युद्धे तत्त्वविपर्ययात् ॥ 207 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
svatattve rāmo jayaṃ prāptaḥ svatattve ca dhanañjayaḥ |
kauravā nihatāḥ sarve yuddhe tattvaviparyayāt ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
svatattve - in his own correct element; rāmaḥ - Rama; jayaṃ - victory; prāptaḥ - obtained; svatattve - in his own element; ca - and; dhanañjayaḥ - Arjuna (Dhananjaya); kauravāḥ - Kauravas; nihatāḥ - were killed; sarve - all; yuddhe - in battle; tattvaviparyayāt - due to the contrary/adverse element.

**English Translation:**
Rama achieved victory while in his favorable element, and so did Arjuna. All the Kauravas were killed in battle because they were in an adverse element.

---

---

### Verse 208
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
जन्मान्तरीयसंस्कारात् प्रसादादथवा गुरोः ।
केषाञ्चिज्जायते तत्त्ववासना विमलात्मनाम् ॥ 208 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
janmāntarīyasaṃskārāt prasādādathavā guroḥ |
keṣāñcijjāyate tattvavāsanā vimalātmanām ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
janmāntarīya - from previous births; saṃskārāt - due to impressions; prasādāt - by the grace; athavā - or; guroḥ - of the Guru; keṣāñcit - of some; jāyate - is born / arises; tattvavāsanā - inclination/knowledge of the elements; vimalātmanām - of the pure-souled ones.

**English Translation:**
Due to impressions from past lives or by the grace of a Guru, the deep knowledge and inclination for the Tattvas arises in some pure-souled individuals.

---

---

### Verse 209
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
लं बीजं धरणीं ध्यायेच्चतुरस्रां च पीतभाम् ।
सुगन्धां स्वर्णवर्णाभां प्राप्नुयाद् देहलाघवम् ॥ 209 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
laṃ bījaṃ dharaṇīṃ dhyāyeccaturasrāṃ ca pītabhām |
sugandhāṃ svarṇavarṇābhāṃ prāpnuyād dehalāghavam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
laṃ - lam; bījaṃ - seed mantra; dharaṇīṃ - earth element; dhyāyet - one should meditate on; caturasrāṃ - square-shaped; ca - and; pītabhām - yellow-colored; sugandhāṃ - fragrant; svarṇavarṇābhāṃ - shining like gold; prāpnuyāt - one attains; dehalāghavam - lightness of the body.

**English Translation:**
One should meditate on the Earth element with the seed mantra 'Lam', envisioning it as square-shaped, yellow, fragrant, and radiant like gold. By this, one attains lightness of the body.

---

---

### Verse 210
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
वं बीजं वारुणं ध्यायेत् तत्त्वमर्धशशिप्रभम् ।
क्षुत्तृष्णादिसहिष्णुत्वं जलमध्ये च मज्जनम् ॥ 210 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
vaṃ bījaṃ vāruṇaṃ dhyāyet tattvamardhaśaśiprabham |
kṣuttṛṣṇādisahiṣṇutvaṃ jalamadhye ca majjanam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
vaṃ - vam; bījaṃ - seed mantra; vāruṇaṃ - water element (pertaining to Varuna); dhyāyet - one should meditate; tattvam - element; ardhaśaśiprabham - shining like a half-moon; kṣut - hunger; tṛṣṇā - thirst; ādi - etc.; sahiṣṇutvaṃ - tolerance/endurance; jalamadhye - in the midst of water; ca - and; majjanam - submergence.

**English Translation:**
One should meditate on the Water element using the seed mantra 'Vam', shining like a half-moon. This grants the endurance to withstand hunger, thirst, and the ability to stay submerged in water.

---

---

### Verse 211
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
रं बीजं पावके ध्यायेत् त्रिकोणमरुणप्रभम् ।
बह्वन्नपानभोक्तृत्वमातपाग्निसहिष्णुता ॥ 211 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
raṃ bījaṃ pāvake dhyāyet trikoṇamaruṇaprabham |
bahvannapānabhoktṛtvamātapāgnisahiṣṇutā ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
raṃ - ram; bījaṃ - seed mantra; pāvake - on fire; dhyāyet - one should meditate; trikoṇam - triangular; aruṇaprabham - radiant red; bahu - abundant; anna - food; pāna - drink; bhoktṛtvam - ability to consume; ātapa - sunlight/heat; agni - fire; sahiṣṇutā - tolerance.

**English Translation:**
One should meditate on the Fire element with the seed mantra 'Ram', envisioning it as triangular and radiant red. It bestows the capacity to consume abundant food and drink, and tolerance to scorching heat and fire.

---

---

### Verse 212
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
यं बीजं पवने ध्यायेद् वृत्तं श्यामलप्रभम् ।
आकाशगमनाद्यं च पक्षिवद् गमनं तथा ॥ 212 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
yaṃ bījaṃ pavane dhyāyed vṛttaṃ śyāmalaprabham |
ākāśagamanādyaṃ ca pakṣivad gamanaṃ tathā ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
yaṃ - yam; bījaṃ - seed mantra; pavane - on air; dhyāyet - one should meditate; vṛttaṃ - circular; śyāmalaprabham - dark/bluish in radiance; ākāśa - sky; gamana - traveling; ādyaṃ - etc.; ca - and; pakṣivat - like a bird; gamanaṃ - flying/moving; tathā - also.

**English Translation:**
One should meditate on the Air element with the seed mantra 'Yam', seeing it as circular and dark blue in radiance. It grants powers like traveling through the sky and flying like a bird.

---

---

### Verse 213
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
हं बीजं गगने ध्यायेन्निराकारं बहुप्रभम् ।
ज्ञानं त्रिकालविषयमैश्वर्यमणिमादिकम् ॥ 213 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
haṃ bījaṃ gagane dhyāyennirākāraṃ bahuprabham |
jñānaṃ trikālaviṣayamaiśvaryamaṇimādikam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
haṃ - ham; bījaṃ - seed mantra; gagane - on ether/space; dhyāyet - one should meditate; nirākāraṃ - formless; bahuprabham - highly radiant; jñānaṃ - knowledge; trikāla - three times (past, present, future); viṣayam - regarding; aiśvaryam - lordly powers; aṇimā-ādikam - starting with Anima (becoming infinitely small).

**English Translation:**
One should meditate on the Space element with the seed mantra 'Ham', formless and highly radiant. It yields knowledge of the past, present, and future, as well as divine powers starting with Anima.

---

---

### Verse 214
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
स्वरज्ञानी नरो यत्र धनं नास्ति ततः परम् ।
गम्यते स्वरज्ञानेन ह्यनायासं फलं भवेत् ॥ 214 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
svarajñānī naro yatra dhanaṃ nāsti tataḥ param |
gamyate svarajñānena hyanāyāsaṃ phalaṃ bhavet ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
svarajñānī - knower of Svara; naraḥ - man; yatra - where; dhanaṃ - wealth; nāsti - is not; tataḥ param - greater than that; gamyate - is traversed/understood; svarajñānena - by knowledge of Svara; hi - indeed; anāyāsaṃ - without effort; phalaṃ - fruit/result; bhavet - is attained.

**English Translation:**
There is no wealth greater than a man who possesses the knowledge of Svara. By mastering the science of Svara, one undoubtedly attains all desired results effortlessly.

---

---

### Verse 215
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
श्रीदेव्युवाच ।
देवदेव महादेव महाज्ञानं स्वरोदयम् ।
त्रिकालविषयं चैव कथं भवति शङ्कर ॥ 215 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
śrīdevyuvāca |
devadeva mahādeva mahājñānaṃ svarodayam |
trikālaviṣayaṃ caiva kathaṃ bhavati śaṅkara ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
śrī-devī uvāca - Goddess Parvati said; deva-deva - O God of gods; mahādeva - O Mahadeva; mahājñānaṃ - the great knowledge; svarodayam - Svarodaya; trikāla-viṣayaṃ - concerning the three periods of time; ca eva - and indeed; kathaṃ - how; bhavati - is it; śaṅkara - O Shankara.

**English Translation:**
Shri Devi said:
O God of gods, Mahadeva, how does this great knowledge of Svarodaya encompass the past, present, and future, O Shankara?

---

---

### Verse 216
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
ईश्वर उवाच ।
अर्थकालजयप्रश्ने शुभाशुभमिति त्रिधा ।
एतत्त्रिकालविज्ञानं नान्यद् भवति सुन्दरि ॥ 216 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
īśvara uvāca |
arthakālajayapraśne śubhāśubhamiti tridhā |
etattrikālavijñānaṃ nānyad bhavati sundari ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
īśvaraḥ uvāca - Lord Shiva said; artha - wealth; kāla - time; jaya - victory; praśne - in questions regarding; śubhāśubham - auspicious and inauspicious; iti - thus; tridhā - threefold; etat - this; trikālavijñānaṃ - knowledge of the three times; na - not; anyat - anything else; bhavati - is; sundari - O beautiful one.

**English Translation:**
Lord Shiva said:
O beautiful one, in answering queries concerning wealth, time (life/death), and victory, the outcomes are auspicious or inauspicious. This threefold division constitutes the knowledge of the three periods of time; there is nothing else.

---

---

### Verse 217
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
तत्त्वे शुभाशुभं कार्यं तत्त्वे जयपराजयौ ।
तत्त्वे सुभिक्षदुर्भिक्षे तत्त्वं त्रिपदमुच्यते ॥ 217 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
tattve śubhāśubhaṃ kāryaṃ tattve jayaparājayau |
tattve subhikṣadurbhikṣe tattvaṃ tripadamucyate ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
tattve - on the Tattva (elements); śubhāśubhaṃ - auspicious and inauspicious; kāryaṃ - actions/tasks; tattve - on the Tattva; jayaparājayau - victory and defeat; tattve - on the Tattva; subhikṣadurbhikṣe - abundance and famine; tattvaṃ - the Tattva; tripadam - threefold basis; ucyate - is called.

**English Translation:**
Auspicious and inauspicious actions depend on the Tattva; victory and defeat are determined by the Tattva; abundance and famine rely on the Tattva. Thus, the Tattva is said to be the foundation of these three.

---

---

### Verse 218
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
श्रीदेव्युवाच ।
देवदेव महादेव सर्वसंसारसागरे ।
किं नराणां परं मित्रं सर्वकार्यार्थसाधकम् ॥ 218 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
śrīdevyuvāca |
devadeva mahādeva sarvasaṃsārasāgare |
kiṃ narāṇāṃ paraṃ mitraṃ sarvakāryārthasādhakam ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
śrī-devī uvāca - Shri Devi said; deva-deva - O God of gods; mahādeva - O Mahadeva; sarva-saṃsāra-sāgare - in the entire ocean of worldly existence; kiṃ - what; narāṇāṃ - of men; paraṃ mitraṃ - greatest friend; sarva-kārya-artha-sādhakam - the accomplisher of all actions and goals.

**English Translation:**
Shri Devi said:
O God of gods, Mahadeva, in this boundless ocean of worldly existence, what is the greatest friend of humanity that helps accomplish all actions and goals?

---

---

### Verse 219
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
ईश्वर उवाच ।
प्राण एव परं मित्रं प्राण एव परः सखा ।
प्राणतुल्यः परो बन्धुर्नास्ति नास्ति वरानने ॥ 219 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
īśvara uvāca |
prāṇa eva paraṃ mitraṃ prāṇa eva paraḥ sakhā |
prāṇatulyaḥ paro bandhurnāsti nāsti varānane ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
īśvaraḥ uvāca - Lord Shiva said; prāṇa - life-breath/Prana; eva - alone; paraṃ mitraṃ - greatest friend; prāṇa eva - Prana alone; paraḥ sakhā - greatest companion; prāṇatulyaḥ - equal to Prana; paraḥ bandhuḥ - greater relative; na asti - is not; na asti - is not; varānane - O beautiful-faced one.

**English Translation:**
Lord Shiva said:
Prana itself is the ultimate friend; Prana itself is the supreme companion. O beautiful-faced one, there is no relative equal to Prana; truly, there is none.

---

---

### Verse 220
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
श्रीदेव्युवाच ।
कथं प्राणस्थितो वायुर्देहः किं प्राणरूपकः ।
तत्त्वेषु सञ्चरन् प्राणो ज्ञायते योगिभिः कथम् ॥ 220 ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**
śrīdevyuvāca |
kathaṃ prāṇasthito vāyurdehaḥ kiṃ prāṇarūpakaḥ |
tattveṣu sañcaran prāṇo jñāyate yogibhiḥ katham ||

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**
śrī-devī uvāca - Shri Devi said; kathaṃ - how; prāṇa-sthitaḥ - situated in Prana; vāyuḥ - Vayu (air); dehaḥ - the body; kiṃ - is it; prāṇa-rūpakaḥ - in the form of Prana; tattveṣu - in the Tattvas (elements); sañcaran - moving; prāṇaḥ - Prana; jñāyate - is known; yogibhiḥ - by the Yogis; katham - how.

**English Translation:**
Shri Devi said:
How does Vayu reside within Prana? Is the body composed of Prana? How is the movement of Prana through the elements recognized by the Yogis?

---

---

### Verse 221
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ गर्भाधानस्वरविचारः —  
दिवा च वामभागेन रात्रौ दक्षिणवाहिना ।  
ऋतौ समे च पुंस्त्वं स्यात् विषमे कन्यका भवेत् ॥ २२१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha garbhādhānasvaravicāraḥ —*  
*divā ca vāmabhāgena rātrau dakṣiṇavāhinā |*  
*ṛtau same ca puṁstvaṁ syāt viṣame kanyakā bhavet || 221 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*garbhādhānasvaravicāraḥ* = evaluation of breath during marital union and conception; *divā ca vāmabhāgena* = during daytime left lunar nostril; *rātrau dakṣiṇavāhinā* = during nighttime right solar nostril; *ṛtau same ca puṁstvaṁ syāt* = union on even nights of post-menstrual period yields male child; *viṣame kanyakā bhavet* = on odd nights yields female child.

**English Translation:**  
**Rules for Conception and Gender Determination:**  
During the post-menstrual fertile period (*Ṛtu-kāla*), union on **even nights** (4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th nights) while the husband's **right solar breath** (Piṅgalā) and wife's **left lunar breath** (Iḍā) are active results in the conception of a **male child**. Union on **odd nights** yields a **female child**.

---

---

---

### Verse 222
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पुंसो दक्षिणनाड्यां तु नार्या वामेन सङ्गमे ।  
पुमान् सञ्जायते पुत्रः सर्वलक्षणसंयुतः ॥ २२२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*puṁso dakṣiṇanāḍyāṁ tu nāryā vāmena saṅgame |*  
*pumān sañjāyate putraḥ sarvalakṣaṇasaṁyutaḥ || 222 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*puṁsaḥ dakṣiṇanāḍyām tu* = husband's right solar nostril active; *nāryā vāmena saṅgame* = wife's left lunar nostril active during union; *pumān sañjāyate putraḥ* = a male child is born; *sarvalakṣaṇasaṁyutaḥ* = endowed with all auspicious physical and spiritual marks.

**English Translation:**  
When the husband's **right solar nostril** (Piṅgalā) and the wife's **left lunar nostril** (Iḍā) are flowing smoothly during union, a **virtuous male child** endowed with all noble marks and long life is conceived.

---

---

---

### Verse 223
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामनाड्यां तथा नार्या वामे चैव पुमानपि ।  
कन्या सञ्जायते तत्र सुशीला रूपसंयुता ॥ २२३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*vāmanāḍyāṁ tathā nāryā vāme caiva pumānapi |*  
*kanyā sañjāyate tatra suśīlā rūpasaṁyuta || 223 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*vāmanāḍyām tathā nāryā* = wife's left lunar nostril active; *vāme caiva pumānapi* = husband's left lunar nostril active as well; *kanyā sañjāyate tatra* = a female child is conceived there; *suśīlā rūpasaṁyutā* = endowed with excellent character and beauty.

**English Translation:**  
When both husband and wife have active **left lunar nostrils** (Iḍā) flowing during union, a **daughter** of refined character, intelligence, and grace is conceived.

---

---

---

### Verse 224
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सुषुम्णायां यदा गर्भो हीनाङ्गो जायते सुतः ।  
अथवा म्रियते गर्भो नात्र कार्या विचारणा ॥ २२४ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*suṣumnāyāṁ yadā garbho hīnāṅgo jāyate sutaḥ |*  
*athavā mriyate garbho nātra kāryā vicāraṇā || 224 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*suṣumnāyām yadā garbhaḥ* = if conception occurs while Sushumna is flowing; *hīnāṅgaḥ jāyate sutaḥ* = child is born with bodily defects/handicaps; *athavā mriyate garbhaḥ* = or embryo perishes/miscarries; *nātra kāryā vicāraṇā* = there is no doubt about this.

**English Translation:**  
If conception occurs while the central **Suṣumnā** breath is active (or breath is chaotic), the fetus either miscarries or the child is born with physical/developmental defects. Therefore, avoid union during Suṣumnā flow.

---

---

### Verse 225
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वाचासिद्धिश्चतुर्थे दूरदृष्टिस्तु पञ्चमे ।
षष्ठे त्वाकाशगमनं चण्डवेगस्तु सप्तमे ॥ २२५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vācāsiddhiś caturthe dūradṛṣṭistu pañcame |
ṣaṣṭhe tvākāśagamanaṃ caṇḍavegastu saptame || 225 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vācāsiddhiś (perfection of speech) caturthe (in the fourth finger/measure) dūradṛṣṭistu (clairvoyance, distant vision) pañcame (in the fifth) | ṣaṣṭhe (in the sixth) tvākāśagamanaṃ (ability to walk in the sky/levitation) caṇḍavegastu (extreme speed) saptame (in the seventh) ||
**English Translation:**  
If the breath is reduced by four digits, one attains perfection of speech (Vak-siddhi). If reduced by five, distant vision (clairvoyance). If reduced by six, the ability to travel through the sky (levitation). If reduced by seven, one attains extreme speed.

---

---

### Verse 226
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अष्टमे सिद्धयश्चैव नवमे निधयो नव ।
दशमे दशमूर्तिश्च छायानैकादशे भवेत् ॥ २२६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
aṣṭame siddhayaścaiva navame nidhayo nava |
daśame daśamūrtiśca chāyānaikādaśe bhavet || 226 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
aṣṭame (in the eighth) siddhayaścaiva (the supernatural powers indeed) navame (in the ninth) nidhayo (the treasures) nava (nine) | daśame (in the tenth) daśamūrtiśca (the ten forms) chāyānaikādaśe (absence of shadow in the eleventh) bhavet (happens) ||
**English Translation:**  
When the breath is reduced by eight digits, one attains the eight major Siddhis (supernatural powers). If reduced by nine, the nine Nidhi-s (divine treasures). If reduced by ten, one can manifest in ten different forms. If reduced by eleven digits, one's physical shadow disappears.

---

---

### Verse 227
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
द्वादशे हंसचारश्च गंगामृतरसं पिबेत् ।
आनखाग्रं प्राणपूरणे कस्य भक्ष्यं च भोजनम् ॥ २२७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dvādaśe haṃsacāraśca gaṃgāmṛtarasaṃ pibet |
ānakhāgraṃ prāṇapūraṇe kasya bhakṣyaṃ ca bhojanam || 227 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dvādaśe (in the twelfth) haṃsacāraśca (the movement of the divine swan/soul) gaṃgāmṛtarasaṃ (the nectar of the celestial Ganges) pibet (drinks) | ānakhāgraṃ (from the tips of the nails to the top of the head) prāṇapūraṇe (upon filling with prana) kasya (whose) bhakṣyaṃ (food to be chewed) ca (and) bhojanam (food to be eaten) ||
**English Translation:**  
If the breath flow is reduced by twelve digits, the breath moves like the divine swan, and one drinks the nectar of immortality. When the life-force (Prana) completely fills the body from the tip of the nails to the head, there remains no need for any worldly food or nourishment.

---

---

### Verse 228
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
एवं प्राणविधिः प्रोक्तः सर्वकार्यफलप्रदः ।
जायते गुरुवाक्येन न विद्याशास्त्रकोटिभिः ॥ २२८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
evaṃ prāṇavidhiḥ proktaḥ sarvakāryaphalapradaḥ |
jāyate guruvākyena na vidyāśāstrakoṭibhiḥ || 228 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
evaṃ (thus) prāṇavidhiḥ (the method of regulating breath) proktaḥ (is declared) sarvakāryaphalapradaḥ (bestowing the fruits of all activities) | jāyate (arises/is born) guruvākyena (by the words of the Guru) na (not) vidyāśāstrakoṭibhiḥ (by millions of sciences and scriptures) ||
**English Translation:**  
Thus, this method of regulating the vital breath, which yields successful results in all endeavors, is described. This profound knowledge is acquired only through the instructions of the Guru, and not through millions of scriptures or worldly sciences.

---

---

### Verse 229
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रातश्चन्द्रो रविः सायं यदि दैवान्न लभ्यते ।
मध्याह्नान्मध्यरात्राच्च परतस्तु प्रवर्तते ॥ २२९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
prātaścandro raviḥ sāyaṃ yadi daivānna labhyate |
madhyāhnānmadhyarātrācca paratastu pravartate || 229 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
prātaścandro (the Moon/Ida in the morning) raviḥ sāyaṃ (the Sun/Pingala in the evening) yadi (if) daivānna (due to fate, not) labhyate (is obtained) | madhyāhnānmadhyarātrācca (from midday and midnight) paratastu (after that) pravartate (it begins to flow) ||
**English Translation:**  
If, by some disruption of the natural rhythm, the Moon breath (Ida) does not flow in the morning and the Sun breath (Pingala) does not flow in the evening, they will automatically reset and begin to flow correctly after midday and midnight.

---

---

### Verse 230
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दूरयुद्धे जयी चन्द्रः समासन्ने दिवाकरः ।
वहन्नाड्या गतः पादः सर्वसिद्धिप्रदायकः ॥ २३० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dūrayuddhe jayī candraḥ samāsanne divākaraḥ |
vahannāḍyā gataḥ pādaḥ sarvasiddhipradāyakaḥ || 230 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dūrayuddhe (in a distant battle) jayī (victorious) candraḥ (the Moon) samāsanne (in a close/near battle) divākaraḥ (the Sun) | vahannāḍyā (with the flowing Nadi) gataḥ (moved) pādaḥ (foot) sarvasiddhipradāyakaḥ (bestowing all success) ||
**English Translation:**  
In a battle fought in a distant land, the flow of the Moon (Ida) brings victory, while in a close-combat or nearby battle, the flow of the Sun (Pingala) brings victory. When embarking on an expedition, stepping forward with the foot corresponding to the active (flowing) Nadi grants all successes.

---

---

### Verse 231
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यात्रारम्भे विवाहे च प्रवेशे नगरादिके ।
शुभकार्याणि सिद्ध्यन्ति चन्द्रचारेषु सर्वदा ॥ २३१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yātrārambhe vivāhe ca praveśe nagarādike |
śubhakāryāṇi siddhyanti candracāreṣu sarvadā || 231 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yātrārambhe (at the beginning of a journey) vivāhe (in marriage) ca (and) praveśe (in entering) nagarādike (a city, house, etc.) | śubhakāryāṇi (auspicious tasks) siddhyanti (are accomplished) candracāreṣu (during the flow of the Moon Nadi) sarvadā (always) ||
**English Translation:**  
When commencing a journey, performing a marriage ceremony, entering a new house or city, and undertaking auspicious deeds, everything is always successfully accomplished during the active flow of the Moon (Ida) Nadi.

---

---

### Verse 232
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अयनतिथिदिनेशैः स्वीयतत्त्वे च युक्ते
यदि वहति कदाचिद् दैवयोगेन पुंसाम् ।
स जयति रिपुसैन्यं स्तम्भमात्रस्वरेण
प्रभवति न च विघ्नो वैकुण्ठस्यापि लोके ॥ २३२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ayanatithidineśaiḥ svīyatattve ca yukte
yadi vahati kadācid daivayogena puṃsām |
sa jayati ripusainyaṃ stambhamātrasvareṇa
prabhavati na ca vighno vaikuṇṭhasyāpi loke || 232 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ayanatithidineśaiḥ (by the lords of the solstice, lunar day, and weekday) svīyatattve (in one's own element) ca (and) yukte (united) yadi (if) vahati (it flows) kadācid (sometimes) daivayogena (by destiny) puṃsām (of men) | sa (he) jayati (conquers) ripusainyaṃ (the enemy army) stambhamātrasvareṇa (merely by the arresting power of the Svara) prabhavati (arises) na ca vighno (and no obstacle) vaikuṇṭhasyāpi loke (even in the heavenly realm of Vaikuntha) ||
**English Translation:**  
If, by divine providence, a person's Svara flows in conjunction with their own corresponding element and the lords of the current Ayana (half-year), Tithi (lunar day), and day of the week, that person conquers the enemy's army merely by the restraining power of their breath. Such a person faces no obstacles, even if they were in the celestial abode of Vaikuntha.

---

---

### Verse 233
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवं रक्ष जीवं रक्ष जीवाङ्गं परिधाय च ।
जीवो जपति यो युद्धे जीवञ्जयति मेदिनीम् ॥ २३३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvaṃ rakṣa jīvaṃ rakṣa jīvāṅgaṃ paridhāya ca |
jīvo japati yo yuddhe jīvañjayati medinīm || 233 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvaṃ rakṣa (protect the living/active breath) jīvaṃ rakṣa (protect the living breath) jīvāṅgaṃ (the active side of the body) paridhāya (putting on/equipping) ca (and) | jīvo japati (the living breath repeats) yo (who) yuddhe (in battle) jīvañjayati (living, conquers) medinīm (the earth) ||
**English Translation:**  
"Protect the active Svara, protect the active Svara." By equipping one's armor or weapons on the side of the active Svara (Jiva-nadi) and mentally repeating this awareness in battle, the warrior conquers the entire earth and emerges alive.

---

---

### Verse 234
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
भूमौ नीरे च कर्तव्यं गमनं शान्तिकर्मसु ।
वह्नौ वायौ प्रदीप्तेषु खे च नोभयेष्वपि ॥ २३४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
bhūmau nīre ca kartavyaṃ gamanaṃ śāntikarmasu |
vahnau vāyau pradīpteṣu khe ca nobhayeṣvapi || 234 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
bhūmau (in earth) nīre (in water) ca (and) kartavyaṃ (should be done) gamanaṃ (movement/journey) śāntikarmasu (in peaceful acts) | vahnau (in fire) vāyau (in air) pradīpteṣu (in blazing/fierce acts) khe (in ether) ca (and) nobhayeṣvapi (not in both) ||
**English Translation:**  
For peaceful and auspicious activities, one should travel when the Earth or Water elements are active in the breath. For fierce and aggressive actions, one should act when the Fire or Air elements are dominant. When the Ether element flows, one should avoid traveling or engaging in either type of action.

---

---

### Verse 235
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवेन शस्त्रं बध्नीयाज्जीवेनैव विकाशयेत् ।
जीवेन प्रक्षिपेच्छस्त्रं युद्धे जयति सर्वदा ॥ २३५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvena śastraṃ badhnīyājjīvenaiva vikāśayet |
jīvena prakṣipecchastraṃ yuddhe jayati sarvadā || 235 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvena (by the active Svara) śastraṃ (the weapon) badhnīyāj (should bind/wear) jīvenaiva (by the active Svara indeed) vikāśayet (should draw/unsheathe) | jīvena (by the active Svara) prakṣipecchastraṃ (should strike/throw the weapon) yuddhe (in battle) jayati (conquers) sarvadā (always) ||
**English Translation:**  
A warrior should strap on his weapons on the side of the body where the breath is active. He should unsheathe the weapon using the hand on the active side, and he should strike or launch the weapon using that same active side. By doing this, he will always be victorious in battle.

---

---

### Verse 236
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आकृष्य प्राणपवनं समारुह्य च वाहनम् ।
समुत्तरे पदं दद्यात् सर्वकार्याणि साधयेत् ॥ २३६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ākṛṣya prāṇapavanaṃ samāruhya ca vāhanam |
samuttare padaṃ dadyāt sarvakāryāṇi sādhayet || 236 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ākṛṣya (having drawn in) prāṇapavanaṃ (the vital breath) samāruhya (having mounted) ca (and) vāhanam (vehicle/horse) | samuttare (upon exhaling/descending) padaṃ dadyāt (should place the foot) sarvakāryāṇi (all actions) sādhayet (accomplishes) ||
**English Translation:**  
A person should inhale and hold the vital breath while mounting a horse or a vehicle, and should place their foot in the stirrup or on the ground while exhaling. By following this method, one accomplishes all desired tasks.

---

---

### Verse 237
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूर्णे शस्त्रसामग्री पूर्णे वा स्वबलं तथा ।
कुरुते पूर्णे तत्त्वस्थो जयत्येको वसुन्धराम् ॥ २३७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrṇe śastrasāmagrī pūrṇe vā svabalaṃ tathā |
kurute pūrṇe tattvastho jayatyeko vasundharām || 237 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrṇe (in the full/active Svara) śastrasāmagrī (weapons and equipment) pūrṇe (in the active Svara) vā (or) svabalaṃ (own strength/army) tathā (similarly) | kurute (does/arranges) pūrṇe tattvastho (established in the active element) jayatyeko (alone conquers) vasundharām (the earth) ||
**English Translation:**  
One who arranges his weapons, military equipment, and army on the side of the active (full) breath, and operates while grounded in the dominant active element, can single-handedly conquer the entire earth.

---

---

### Verse 238
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
या नाडी वहते चाङ्गे तस्यामेवाधिदेवता ।
सन्मुखेऽपि दिशा तेषां सर्वकार्यफलप्रदा ॥ २३८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yā nāḍī vahate cāṅge tasyāmevādhidevatā |
sanmukhe'pi diśā teṣāṃ sarvakāryaphalapradā || 238 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yā nāḍī (whichever Nadi) vahate (flows) cāṅge (in the body) tasyāmevādhidevatā (its presiding deity is exactly there) | sanmukhe'pi (even facing forward) diśā (the direction) teṣāṃ (their) sarvakāryaphalapradā (bestows the fruit of all actions) ||
**English Translation:**  
Whichever Nadi (left or right) is currently flowing in the body, the presiding deity of that Nadi is present there. If one faces the specific direction associated with that active Nadi, it guarantees success and fruitful results for all actions.

---

---

### Verse 239
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आदौ तु क्रियते मुद्रा पश्चाद् युद्धं समाचरेत् ।
सर्पमुद्रा कृता येन तस्य सिद्धिर्न संशयः ॥ २३९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ādau tu kriyate mudrā paścād yuddhaṃ samācaret |
sarpamudrā kṛtā yena tasya siddhirna saṃśayaḥ || 239 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ādau (first) tu (indeed) kriyate (is done) mudrā (the seal/gesture) paścād (afterwards) yuddhaṃ (battle) samācaret (should undertake) | sarpamudrā (the serpent gesture) kṛtā (done) yena (by whom) tasya (his) siddhirna (success is not) saṃśayaḥ (in doubt) ||
**English Translation:**  
A warrior should first perform the appropriate hand gesture (Mudra) and then engage in battle. For the one who correctly forms the Sarpa-mudra (Serpent gesture), victory and success are absolutely certain.

---

---

### Verse 240
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रप्रचारेऽप्यथ सूर्यवह्निभटाः समायान्ति च योद्धुकामाः ।
समीरणस्तत्त्वविदां प्रतीतो या शून्यता सा प्रतिकार्यनाशम् ॥ २४० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
candrapracāre'pyatha sūryavahnibhaṭāḥ samāyānti ca yoddhukāmāḥ |
samīraṇastattvavidāṃ pratīto yā śūnyatā sā pratikāryanāśam || 240 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candrapracāre'pyatha (even during the flow of the Moon) sūryavahnibhaṭāḥ (warriors of the Sun and Fire) samāyānti (arrive) ca (and) yoddhukāmāḥ (desiring to fight) | samīraṇastattvavidāṃ (the Air element, by the knowers of elements) pratīto (understood) yā śūnyatā (which is emptiness) sā (that) pratikāryanāśam (destruction of the counter-action/efforts) ||
**English Translation:**  
Even if the Moon Nadi is flowing, if the Water (or Air) element is active, and enemy warriors associated with the Sun and Fire elements approach desiring to fight, the knowers of the Tattvas understand that the empty (inactive) state of the breath will lead to the destruction of the endeavor. (Alternatively: If the Air or Ether element flows when facing Sun/Fire enemies, it signifies a void and results in failure).

---

---

### Verse 241
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यां दिशं वहते वायुर्युद्धं तद्दिशि दापयेत् ।
जयत्येव न सन्देहः शक्रोऽपि यदि चाग्रतः ॥ २४१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yāṃ diśaṃ vahate vāyuryuddhaṃ taddiśi dāpayet |
jayatyeva na sandehaḥ śakro'pi yadi cāgrataḥ || 241 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yāṃ diśaṃ (in which direction) vahate vāyur (the air flows) yuddhaṃ (the battle) taddiśi (in that direction) dāpayet (should direct/engage) | jayatyeva (conquers indeed) na sandehaḥ (no doubt) śakro'pi (even Indra) yadi cāgrataḥ (if in front) ||
**English Translation:**  
One should launch an attack or engage in battle in the very direction towards which the active element (Air/Vayu) is flowing. By doing so, victory is guaranteed without a doubt, even if one is facing Lord Indra himself on the battlefield.

---

---

### Verse 242
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यन्नाड्यां वहते वायुस्तदङ्गे प्राणमेव च ।
आकृष्य गच्छेत् कर्णान्तं जयेदपि पुरन्दरम् ॥ २४२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yannāḍyāṃ vahate vāyustadaṅge prāṇameva ca |
ākṛṣya gacchet karṇāntaṃ jayedapi purandaram || 242 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yannāḍyāṃ (in which Nadi) vahate (flows) vāyus (the breath) tadaṅge (in that limb/side) prāṇameva ca (the vital breath as well) | ākṛṣya (having drawn) gacchet (should move) karṇāntaṃ (up to the ear) jayedapi (conquers even) purandaram (Indra) ||
**English Translation:**  
In whichever Nadi the breath is flowing, one should draw the vital energy up to the ear on that active side of the body before moving forward. By doing this, a person could defeat even Purandara (the king of gods, Indra) in battle.

---

---

### Verse 243
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रतिपक्षप्रहारेभ्यः पूर्णाङ्गे योऽभिरक्षति ।
न तस्य रिपुभिः शक्तिर्बलिष्ठैरपि हन्यते ॥ २४३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pratipakṣaprahārebhyaḥ pūrṇāṅge yo'bhirakṣati |
na tasya ripubhiḥ śaktirbaliṣṭhairapi hanyate || 243 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pratipakṣaprahārebhyaḥ (from the strikes of the opponent) pūrṇāṅge (on the full/active side of the body) yo'bhirakṣati (who protects) | na (not) tasya (his) ripubhiḥ (by enemies) śaktir (power) baliṣṭhairapi (even by the strongest) hanyate (is destroyed/killed) ||
**English Translation:**  
The warrior who defends himself by receiving the enemy's blows on the 'full' (active Svara) side of his body can never be destroyed or defeated, even by the most extraordinarily strong adversaries.

---

---

### Verse 244
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अङ्गुष्ठतर्जनीमध्ये पादाङ्गुष्ठे तथा ध्वनिः ।
युद्धकाले च कर्तव्यो लक्षयोद्धान् जयी भवेत् ॥ २४४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
aṅguṣṭhatarjanīmadhye pādāṅguṣṭhe tathā dhvaniḥ |
yuddhakāle ca kartavyo lakṣayoddhān jayī bhavet || 244 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
aṅguṣṭhatarjanīmadhye (between the thumb and index finger) pādāṅguṣṭhe (in the big toe) tathā (as well as) dhvaniḥ (sound/snapping) | yuddhakāle ca (and during battle) kartavyo (should be done) lakṣayoddhān (a hundred thousand warriors) jayī bhavet (he becomes victorious over) ||
**English Translation:**  
Making a snapping sound between the thumb and index finger, as well as applying pressure on the big toe, should be done during combat. A person employing this technique can single-handedly conquer a hundred thousand warriors.

---

---

### Verse 245
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
निशाकरे रवौ चारे मध्ये यस्य समीरणः ।
स्थितो रक्षेद् दिगन्तानि जयकाङ्क्षी गतः सदा ॥ २४५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
niśākare ravau cāre madhye yasya samīraṇaḥ |
sthito rakṣed digantāni jayakāṅkṣī gataḥ sadā || 245 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
niśākare (in the Moon) ravau (in the Sun) cāre (in the flow) madhye (in the middle/Sushumna) yasya (whose) samīraṇaḥ (breath) | sthito (situated) rakṣed (should protect) digantāni (the ends of the directions) jayakāṅkṣī (desiring victory) gataḥ sadā (always moving) ||
**English Translation:**  
If the breath flows exactly in the middle (Sushumna) rather than fully in the Moon or Sun Nadi, the seeker of victory should stay in their position and defensively protect their territory in all directions, instead of advancing aggressively.

---

---

### Verse 246
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
श्वासप्रवेशकाले तु दूतो जल्पति वाञ्छितम् ।
तस्यार्थः सिद्धिमायाति निर्गमे नैव सुन्दरी ॥ २४६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śvāsapraveśakāle tu dūto jalpati vāñchitam |
tasyārthaḥ siddhimāyāti nirgame naiva sundari || 246 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śvāsapraveśakāle (at the time of breath entering/inhalation) tu (but) dūto (the messenger) jalpati (speaks) vāñchitam (the desired matter) | tasyārthaḥ (its purpose/meaning) siddhimāyāti (attains success) nirgame (at the time of exhalation) naiva (not at all) sundari (O Beautiful One) ||
**English Translation:**  
O Beautiful One (Parvati)! If a messenger or an enquirer asks their question exactly while the practitioner is inhaling (the breath is entering), their desired object will be successfully attained. However, if the question is asked during exhalation, it will not be successful.

---

---

### Verse 247
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
लाभादीन्यपि कार्याणि पृष्टानि कीर्तितानि च ।
जीवे विशति सिद्ध्यन्ति निर्गमे नाशनं भवेत् ॥ २४७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
lābhādīnyapi kāryāṇi pṛṣṭāni kīrtitāni ca |
jīve viśati siddhyanti nirgame nāśanaṃ bhavet || 247 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
lābhādīnyapi (profits and others also) kāryāṇi (tasks/objectives) pṛṣṭāni (asked) kīrtitāni ca (and spoken of) | jīve (in the living/active Svara) viśati (entering) siddhyanti (are accomplished) nirgame (in exiting/exhaling) nāśanaṃ bhavet (destruction happens) ||
**English Translation:**  
When someone asks about acquiring profits, success, or other objectives, if the question is posed while the breath is entering (inhaling) on the active side, the objectives will be fulfilled. If asked while the breath is exiting (exhaling), the endeavor will fail.

---

---

### Verse 248
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
नरे दक्षा स्वकीया च स्त्रीणां वामा प्रशस्यते ।
कुम्भको युद्धकाले च तिस्रो नाड्यस्त्रिधा गतिः ॥ २४८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
nare dakṣā svakīyā ca strīṇāṃ vāmā praśasyate |
kumbhako yuddhakāle ca tisro nāḍyastridhā gatiḥ || 248 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nare (in a man) dakṣā (the right Nadi) svakīyā ca (and one's own) strīṇāṃ (of women) vāmā (the left Nadi) praśasyate (is praised/auspicious) | kumbhako (breath retention) yuddhakāle ca (and during battle time) tisro (three) nāḍyastridhā (Nadis, in three ways) gatiḥ (movement) ||
**English Translation:**  
For a man, his own right Nadi (Pingala) is considered highly auspicious, while for a woman, the left Nadi (Ida) is auspicious. During a battle, Kumbhaka (breath retention) is most beneficial. Thus, the three Nadis function through these three distinct modes of movement.

---

---

### Verse 249
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
हकारस्य सकारस्य विना भेदं स्वरः कथम् ।
सोऽहं हंसपदेनैव जीवो जयति सर्वदा ॥ २४९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
hakārasya sakārasya vinā bhedaṃ svaraḥ katham |
so'haṃ haṃsapadenaiva jīvo jayati sarvadā || 249 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
hakārasya (of the syllable 'Ha') sakārasya (of the syllable 'Sa') vinā bhedaṃ (without knowing the difference) svaraḥ katham (how is Svara knowledge possible?) | so'haṃ (So-Ham, He am I) haṃsapadenaiva (by the word Hamsa indeed) jīvo (the living soul) jayati sarvadā (always conquers) ||
**English Translation:**  
How can one truly understand the science of Svara without distinguishing the subtle differences between the 'Ha' (exhalation) and 'Sa' (inhalation) syllables? By meditating on the divine breath mantra "So-Ham" and "Hamsa", the living soul consistently achieves victory over all limitations.

---

---

### Verse 250
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शून्याङ्गं पूरितं कृत्वा जीवाङ्गे गोपयेज्जयम् ।
जीवाङ्गे घातमाप्नोति शून्याङ्गे रक्षते सदा ॥ २५० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śūnyāṅgaṃ pūritaṃ kṛtvā jīvāṅge gopayejjayam |
jīvāṅge ghātamāpnoti śūnyāṅge rakṣate sadā || 250 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śūnyāṅgaṃ (the empty/inactive side of the body) pūritaṃ kṛtvā (having made full/protecting it) jīvāṅge (in the active side) gopayejjayam (should secure victory) | jīvāṅge (in the active side) ghātamāpnoti (receives the blow/strike) śūnyāṅge (in the empty side) rakṣate sadā (protects always) ||
**English Translation:**  
One should guard the 'empty' (inactive breath) side of the body and launch attacks to secure victory using the 'full' (active breath) side. A warrior can absorb and neutralize enemy strikes on his active side, while he must constantly protect his inactive side.

---

---

### Verse 251
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामे वा यदि वा दक्षे यदि पृच्छति पृच्छकः ।
पूर्णे घातो न जायेत शून्ये घातं विनिर्दिशेत् ॥ २५१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāme vā yadi vā dakṣe yadi pṛcchati pṛcchakaḥ |
pūrṇe ghāto na jāyeta śūnye ghātaṃ vinirdiśet || 251 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāme (on the left) vā (or) yadi vā (or if) dakṣe (on the right) yadi (if) pṛcchati (asks) pṛcchakaḥ (the questioner) | pūrṇe (in the full/active Svara) ghāto na jāyeta (destruction/harm does not happen) śūnye (in the empty/inactive Svara) ghātaṃ (destruction/harm) vinirdiśet (one should predict) ||
**English Translation:**  
Whether a questioner approaches and asks a question from the left side or the right side, if they are standing on the side of the 'full' (active) breath, one should predict that no harm or defeat will occur. If they stand on the 'empty' (inactive) side, one should predict failure or injury.

---

---

### Verse 252
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
भूतत्त्वेन उदरे घातः पदस्थानेऽम्बुना भवेत् ।
ऊरुस्थानेऽग्नितत्त्वेन करस्थाने च वायुना ॥ २५२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
bhūtattvena udare ghātaḥ padasthāne'mbunā bhavet |
ūrusthāne'gnitattvena karasthāne ca vāyunā || 252 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
bhūtattvena (by the Earth element) udare (in the belly) ghātaḥ (injury) padasthāne'mbunā bhavet (in the place of the feet by Water it happens) | ūrusthāne'gnitattvena (in the place of the thighs by the Fire element) karasthāne ca vāyunā (and in the place of the hands by Air) ||
**English Translation:**  
If a battle or attack is queried, one can predict the location of a wound based on the active element: If the Earth element flows, the injury will be in the stomach. If the Water element flows, the feet. If the Fire element, the thighs. If the Air element flows, the wound will be on the hands.

---

---

### Verse 253
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शिरसि व्योमतत्त्वेन ज्ञातव्यो घातनिर्णयः ।
एवं पञ्चविधो घातः स्वरशास्त्रे प्रकाशितः ॥ २५३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śirasi vyomatattvena jñātavyo ghātanirṇayaḥ |
evaṃ pañcavidho ghātaḥ svaraśāstre prakāśitaḥ || 253 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śirasi (on the head) vyomatattvena (by the Ether element) jñātavyo (should be known) ghātanirṇayaḥ (the determination of the wound) | evaṃ (thus) pañcavidho (the five-fold) ghātaḥ (injury) svaraśāstre (in the Svara Shastra) prakāśitaḥ (is illuminated/declared) ||
**English Translation:**  
If the Ether (Akasha) element is active, the injury should be predicted to strike the head. In this manner, the science of Svara Shastra illuminates the determination of these five types of bodily injuries according to the dominant elements.

---

---

### Verse 254
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
युद्धकाले यदा चन्द्रः स्थायी जयति निश्चितम् ।
यदा सूर्यप्रवाहस्तु यायी विजयते तदा ॥ २५४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yuddhakāle yadā candraḥ sthāyī jayati niścitam |
yadā sūryapravāhastu yāyī vijayate tadā || 254 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yuddhakāle (at the time of battle) yadā (when) candraḥ (the Moon) sthāyī (the stationary/defending party) jayati (conquers) niścitam (certainly) | yadā (when) sūryapravāhastu (the flow of the Sun however) yāyī (the moving/attacking party) vijayate tadā (then conquers) ||
**English Translation:**  
At the time of battle, if the Moon Nadi (Ida) is flowing, the 'Sthayi' (the defender or the one attacked in his own territory) will certainly be victorious. Conversely, if the Sun Nadi (Pingala) is flowing, the 'Yayi' (the aggressor or invading force) will attain victory.

---

---

### Verse 255
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जयमध्ये तु सन्देहे नाडीमध्यं तु लक्षयेत् ।
सुषुम्नायां गते प्राणे सङ्ग्रामे शत्रुसङ्कटम् ॥ २५५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jayamadhye tu sandehe nāḍīmadhyaṃ tu lakṣayet |
suṣumnāyāṃ gate prāṇe saṅgrāme śatrusaṅkaṭam || 255 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jayamadhye (in the middle of victory) tu sandehe (but in doubt) nāḍīmadhyaṃ tu lakṣayet (one should observe the middle of the Nadis) | suṣumnāyāṃ gate prāṇe (when the breath has gone into the Sushumna) saṅgrāme (in battle) śatrusaṅkaṭam (danger from the enemy) ||
**English Translation:**  
If there is doubt regarding who will achieve victory, one should carefully observe the middle path of the Nadis. If the breath is flowing through the central Sushumna Nadi, it indicates impending crisis, danger, and potential defeat at the hands of the enemy in the battle.

---

---

### Verse 256
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यस्या नाड्या भवेच्चारस्तां दिशं युधि संश्रयेत् ।
तदाऽसौ जयमाप्नोति नात्र कार्या विचारणा ॥ २५६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yasyā nāḍyā bhaveccārastāṃ diśaṃ yudhi saṃśrayet |
tadā'sau jayamāpnoti nātra kāryā vicāraṇā || 256 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yasyā nāḍyā (of whichever Nadi) bhaveccārastāṃ (should be the flow, that) diśaṃ (direction) yudhi (in battle) saṃśrayet (one should take refuge in/face) | tadā'sau (then he) jayamāpnoti (attains victory) nātra kāryā vicāraṇā (here no second thought should be done) ||
**English Translation:**  
A warrior should firmly position himself to face the exact direction corresponding to the Nadi that is currently flowing during the combat. By aligning with the active breath's direction, he will undoubtedly achieve victory; one should not hesitate or overthink this strategy.

---

---

### Verse 257
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यदि सङ्ग्रामकाले तु वामनाडी यदा वहेत् ।
स्थायिनो विजयं विद्याद् रिपुवशं यायिनोऽपि च ॥ २५७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yadi saṅgrāmakāle tu vāmanāḍī yadā vahet |
sthāyino vijayaṃ vidyād ripuvaśaṃ yāyino'pi ca || 257 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yadi (if) saṅgrāmakāle tu (but at the time of battle) vāmanāḍī (the left Nadi) yadā vahet (when it flows) | sthāyino vijayaṃ vidyād (one should know the victory of the defender) ripuvaśaṃ yāyino'pi ca (and the subjugation by the enemy of the attacker) ||
**English Translation:**  
If the left Nadi (Ida) is flowing during the time of a battle, one should understand that the defending army (Sthayi) will be victorious, and the attacking army (Yayi) will be defeated and fall under the control of the enemy.

---

---

### Verse 258
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यदि संग्रामकाले तु सूर्यस्त्वव्याहतो वहेत् ।
तदा यायिजयं विद्यात् सदेवासुरमानवे ॥२५८॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
yadi saṃgrāmakāle tu sūryastvavyāhato vahet ।
tadā yāyijayaṃ vidyāt sadevāsuramānave ॥258॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yadi - if; saṃgrāmakāle - at the time of battle; tu - indeed; sūryaḥ - the sun (nāḍī); tu - and; avyāhataḥ - uninterrupted; vahet - flows; tadā - then; yāyi-jayaṃ - victory of the aggressor/attacker; vidyāt - one should know; sa-deva-asura-mānave - among gods, demons, and humans.

**English Translation:**  
If, at the time of battle, the solar breath (Sūrya Nādī) flows uninterruptedly, one should know that victory belongs to the attacker (aggressor), whether among gods, demons, or human beings.

---

---

### Verse 259
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रणे ददाति पृष्ठं वै वामायां प्रविशेन्नरः ।
स्थानं विषुवचारेण जयः सूर्येण धावता ॥२५९॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
raṇe dadāti pṛṣṭhaṃ vai vāmāyāṃ praviśennaraḥ ।
sthānaṃ viṣuvacāreṇa jayaḥ sūryeṇa dhāvatā ॥259॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
raṇe - in battle; dadāti - gives; pṛṣṭhaṃ - back (flees); vai - indeed; vāmāyāṃ - when the left breath (Idā) is active; praviśet - enters; naraḥ - a man; sthānaṃ - place (standoff); viṣuvacāreṇa - by the flow of Suṣumnā (equinox); jayaḥ - victory; sūryeṇa - by the solar breath; dhāvatā - while running/flowing.

**English Translation:**  
A man who enters battle while the left breath (Idā Nādī) is flowing will turn his back (flee) and be defeated by the enemy. If the Suṣumnā Nādī is flowing, it results in a standoff (staying in place). But if one advances while the solar breath (Piṅgalā) is active, he attains victory.

---

---

### Verse 260
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
युद्धे द्वयङ्गतप्रश्ने पूर्णस्य प्रथमे जयः ।
रिक्ते चैव द्वितीयस्य जयो भवति नान्यथा ॥२६०॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
yuddhe dvayaṅgatapraśne pūrṇasya prathame jayaḥ ।
rikte caiva dvitīyasya jayo bhavati nānyathā ॥260॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yuddhe - in battle; dvayaṅgatapraśne - regarding a question involving two parties; pūrṇasya - of the full (active breath); prathame - for the first; jayaḥ - victory; rikte - in the empty (inactive breath); caiva - and indeed; dvitīyasya - for the second; jayo bhavati - victory happens; na-anyathā - not otherwise.

**English Translation:**  
Regarding a question about battle involving two parties: if the question is asked when the breath is full (active), victory belongs to the first party. If asked during the empty (inactive) breath, victory goes to the second party; it cannot be otherwise.

---

---

### Verse 261
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूर्णनाडीगतः पृष्ठे शून्याङ्गं च तदाग्रतः ।
शून्यस्थाने कृतः शत्रुर्म्रियते नात्र संशयः ॥२६१॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrṇanāḍīgataḥ pṛṣṭhe śūnyāṅgaṃ ca tadāgrataḥ ।
śūnyasthāne kṛtaḥ śatrurmriyate nātra saṃśayaḥ ॥261॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrṇanāḍīgataḥ - situated in the active breath; pṛṣṭhe - at the back; śūnyāṅgaṃ - the empty side; ca - and; tadā - then; agrataḥ - in front; śūnyasthāne - in the empty place; kṛtaḥ - placed; śatruḥ - enemy; mriyate - dies; na-atra - not here; saṃśayaḥ - doubt.

**English Translation:**  
If the questioner is positioned on the side of the full breath and the enemy is at the back (or if the enemy is placed in the empty side in front), the enemy facing the empty side and situated in the empty space meets with death. There is no doubt about this.

---

---

### Verse 262
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामचारे समे नाम यस्य तस्य जयो भवेत् ।
पृच्छको दक्षिणे भागे विजयी विषमाक्षरः ॥२६२॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāmacāre same nāma yasya tasya jayo bhavet ।
pṛcchako dakṣiṇe bhāge vijayī viṣamākṣaraḥ ॥262॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāmacāre - during the flow of the left breath; same - even (number of syllables); nāma - name; yasya - whose; tasya - his; jayo bhavet - victory will happen; pṛcchakaḥ - the questioner; dakṣiṇe bhāge - on the right side; vijayī - victorious; viṣamākṣaraḥ - odd number of syllables.

**English Translation:**  
If a question is asked while the left breath is flowing, the party whose name has an even number of syllables will be victorious. If the questioner stands on the right side (or if the right breath flows), the one whose name has an odd number of syllables will be victorious.

---

---

### Verse 263
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यदा पृच्छति चन्द्रस्य तदा सन्धानमादिशेत् ।
पृच्छेद्यदा तु सूर्यस्य तदा जानीयाद्विग्रहम् ॥२६३॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
yadā pṛcchati candrasya tadā sandhānamādiśet ।
pṛcchedyadā tu sūryasya tadā jānīyādvigraham ॥263॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yadā - when; pṛcchati - asks; candrasya - of the moon (flow); tadā - then; sandhānam - peace/treaty; ādiśet - one should declare; pṛcchet - asks; yadā - when; tu - but; sūryasya - of the sun (flow); tadā - then; jānīyāt - one should know; vigraham - conflict/war.

**English Translation:**  
If a question about battle is asked when the lunar breath (Chandra Nādī) is flowing, one should declare that a peace treaty will be formed. But if the question is asked when the solar breath (Sūrya Nādī) is flowing, one should know that a conflict (war) will take place.

---

---

### Verse 264
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पार्थिवे च समं युद्धं सिद्धिर्भवति वारुणे ।
युद्धे हि तेजसे भङ्गो मृत्युर्वायौ नभस्यपि ॥२६४॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
pārthive ca samaṃ yuddhaṃ siddhirbhavati vāruṇe ।
yuddhe hi tejase bhaṅgo mṛtyurvāyau nahasyapi ॥264॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pārthive - in the earth element; ca - and; samaṃ - equal; yuddhaṃ - battle; siddhiḥ - success/victory; bhavati - happens; vāruṇe - in the water element; yuddhe - in battle; hi - indeed; tejase - in the fire element; bhaṅgaḥ - defeat/destruction; mṛtyuḥ - death; vāyau - in the air element; nabhasyapi - and in the ether element.

**English Translation:**  
If a battle commences during the earth element, it results in an equal standoff. Success and victory are attained during the water element. In the fire element, there is defeat; and if the battle happens during the air or ether element, it results in death.

---

---

### Verse 265
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
निमित्तात्प्रमादाद्वा यदा न ज्ञायतेऽनिलः ।
पृच्छाकाले तदा कुर्यादिदं यत्नेन बुद्धिमान् ॥२६५॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
nimittātpramādādvā yadā na jñāyate'nilaḥ ।
pṛcchākāle tadā kuryādidaṃ yatnena buddhimān ॥265॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nimittāt - due to some cause; pramādāt - due to carelessness/mistake; vā - or; yadā - when; na jñāyate - is not known; anilaḥ - the breath; pṛcchākāle - at the time of questioning; tadā - then; kuryāt - should do; idam - this; yatnena - with effort; buddhimān - the intelligent one.

**English Translation:**  
If, due to some reason or by mistake, the flow of the breath is not known at the time a question is asked, the intelligent practitioner should carefully perform the following action.

---

---

### Verse 266
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
निश्चलां धारणां कृत्वा पुष्पं हस्तान्निपातयेत् ।
पूर्णाङ्गे पुष्पपतनं शून्यं वा तत्फलं भवेत् ॥२६६॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
niścalāṃ dhāraṇāṃ kṛtvā puṣpaṃ hastānnipātayet ।
pūrṇāṅge puṣpapatanaṃ śūnyaṃ vā tatphalaṃ bhavet ॥266॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
niścalāṃ - steady; dhāraṇāṃ - concentration; kṛtvā - having done; puṣpaṃ - a flower; hastāt - from the hand; nipātayet - should drop; pūrṇāṅge - on the full/active side; puṣpapatanaṃ - the falling of the flower; śūnyaṃ - empty; vā - or; tat-phalaṃ - its fruit/result; bhavet - will be.

**English Translation:**  
With steady concentration, one should drop a flower from the hand onto the ground. If the flower falls on the side of the full (active) breath, the result will be fruitful and positive. If it falls away on the empty side, the result will be zero (failure).

---

---

### Verse 267
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तिष्ठन्नुपविशन्वापि प्राणमाकर्षयन्निजम् ।
मनोभङ्गमकुर्वाणः सर्वकार्येषु जीवति ॥२६७॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
tiṣṭhannupaviśanvāpi prāṇamākarṣayannijam ।
manobhaṅgamakurvāṇaḥ sarvakāryeṣu jīvati ॥267॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tiṣṭhan - standing; upaviśan - sitting; vā - or; api - even; prāṇam - life-breath; ākarṣayan - drawing in; nijam - one's own; manobhaṅgam - distraction of the mind; akurvāṇaḥ - not doing (without); sarvakāryeṣu - in all tasks; jīvati - survives/succeeds.

**English Translation:**  
Whether standing or sitting, one who draws in their own Prāṇa (life breath) while keeping the mind perfectly still (without any distraction) will succeed in all tasks and survive any endeavor.

---

---

### Verse 268
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
न शस्त्रं विविधं घोरं न चौरा न च पन्नगाः ।
न शत्रुव्याधयश्चापि शून्यस्थानस्थितं क्षमाः ॥२६८॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
na śastraṃ vividhaṃ ghoraṃ na caurā na ca pannagāḥ ।
na śatruvyādhayaścāpi śūnyasthānasthitaṃ kṣamāḥ ॥268॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
na - not; śastraṃ - weapons; vividhaṃ - various; ghoraṃ - terrible; na - not; caurā - thieves; na - not; ca - and; pannagāḥ - snakes; na - not; śatru-vyādhayaḥ - enemies and diseases; ca-api - and also; śūnyasthāna-sthitaṃ - one situated in the empty space; kṣamāḥ - are capable (of harming).

**English Translation:**  
Various terrible weapons, thieves, serpents, enemies, and diseases are incapable of harming a person who is situated in the state of the "empty space" (the inactive breath).

---

---

### Verse 269
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवेन स्थापयेद्वायुं जीवेनारम्भयेत्पुनः ।
जीवेन क्रीडते नित्यं द्यूते जयति सर्वथा ॥२६९॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvena sthāpayedvāyuṃ jīvenārambhayetpunaḥ ।
jīvena krīḍate nityaṃ dyūte jayati sarvathā ॥269॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvena - by the active breath (jīva); sthāpayet - should establish; vāyuṃ - the wind/breath; jīvena - by the active breath; ārambhayet - should start; punaḥ - again; jīvena - by the active breath; krīḍate - plays; nityaṃ - always; dyūte - in gambling; jayati - wins; sarvathā - in every way.

**English Translation:**  
One should stabilize the breath using the active breath (jīva) and commence any action with it. By always playing with the active breath, one will invariably win in gambling and games of chance.

---

---

### Verse 270
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
स्वरज्ञानिबलस्याग्रे निष्फलं कोटिधा भवेत् ।
इह लोके परत्रापि स्वरज्ञानी बली सदा ॥२७०॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
svarajñānibalasyāgre niṣphalaṃ koṭidhā bhavet ।
iha loke paratrāpi svarajñānī balī sadā ॥270॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
svarajñānibalasyāgre - in front of the power of a knower of Svara; niṣphalaṃ - useless; koṭidhā - million-fold; bhavet - becomes; iha - in this; loke - world; paratra - in the next; api - also; svarajñānī - the knower of Svara; balī - powerful; sadā - always.

**English Translation:**  
Any million-fold strength becomes completely ineffective before the power of one who knows the science of Svara. A knower of Svara is always the most powerful, both in this world and the next.

---

---

### Verse 271
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दशशतायुतलक्षदेशाधिपबलं क्वचित् ।
शतक्रतुसुरेन्द्राणां बलं कोटिगुणं भवेत् ॥२७१॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
daśaśatāyutalakṣadeśādhipabalaṃ kvacit ।
śatakratusurendrāṇāṃ balaṃ koṭiguṇaṃ bhavet ॥271॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
daśa - ten; śata - hundred; ayuta - ten thousand; lakṣa - hundred thousand; deśa-adhipa - ruler of a country; balaṃ - strength; kvacit - sometimes; śatakratu-surendrāṇāṃ - of Indra and other lords of gods; balaṃ - strength; koṭiguṇaṃ - millions of times; bhavet - becomes.

**English Translation:**  
The strength of rulers governing ten, a hundred, ten thousand, or a hundred thousand countries, or even the power of Indra and the lords of gods—the power of Svara knowledge is millions of times greater than all of them combined.

---

---

### Verse 272
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
देव्युवाच ।
परस्परं मनुष्याणां युद्धे प्रोक्तो जयस्त्वया ।
यमयुद्धे समुत्पन्ने मनुष्याणां कथं जयः ॥२७२॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
devyuvāca ।
parasparaṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ yuddhe prokto jayastvayā ।
yamayuddhe samutpanne manuṣyāṇāṃ kathaṃ jayaḥ ॥272॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
devī - Goddess Pārvatī; uvāca - said; parasparaṃ - mutually; manuṣyāṇāṃ - of humans; yuddhe - in battle; proktaḥ - explained; jayaḥ - victory; tvayā - by you; yamayuddhe - in the battle with Yama (Death); samutpanne - having arisen; manuṣyāṇāṃ - of humans; kathaṃ - how; jayaḥ - victory.

**English Translation:**  
Goddess Pārvatī said: "O Lord, You have explained how humans can attain victory in mutual combat. But when a battle with Yama (the God of Death) arises, how can a human achieve victory?"

---

---

### Verse 273
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ईश्वर उवाच ।
ध्यायेद्देवं स्थिरो जीवं जुहुयाज्जीवसङ्गमे ।
इष्टसिद्धिर्भवैत्तस्य महालाभो जयस्तथा ॥२७३॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
īśvara uvāca ।
dhyāyeddevaṃ sthiro jīvaṃ juhuyājjīvasaṅgame ।
iṣṭasiddhirbhavettasya mahālābho jayastathā ॥273॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
īśvara - Lord Śiva; uvāca - said; dhyāyet - should meditate; devaṃ - on God; sthiraḥ - being steady; jīvaṃ - the vital breath; juhuyāt - should sacrifice; jīvasaṅgame - in the union of life (Kumbhaka); iṣṭasiddhiḥ - fulfillment of desires; bhavet - will be; tasya - his; mahālābhaḥ - great gain; jayaḥ - victory; tathā - and.

**English Translation:**  
Lord Śiva said: "Remaining perfectly steady, one should meditate on God and offer the active vital breath as a sacrifice into the union of life (performing Kumbhaka). By doing this, one attains the fulfillment of all desires, immense spiritual gain, and ultimate victory even over death."

---

---

### Verse 274
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
निराकारात्समुत्पन्नं साकारं सकलं जगत् ।
तत्साकारं निराकारं ज्ञाने भवति तत्क्षणात् ॥२७४॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
nirākārātsamutpannaṃ sākāraṃ sakalaṃ jagat ।
tatsākāraṃ nirākāraṃ jñāne bhavati tatkṣaṇāt ॥274॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nirākārāt - from the formless; samutpannaṃ - originated; sākāraṃ - with form; sakalaṃ - entire; jagat - universe; tat - that; sākāraṃ - with form; nirākāraṃ - formless; jñāne - upon knowing (attaining true knowledge); bhavati - becomes; tatkṣaṇāt - instantly.

**English Translation:**  
This entire universe, possessing form, has originated from the Supreme Formless Lord. Upon attaining true knowledge, the entire manifested universe with form instantly dissolves back into that Formless Reality.

---

---

### Verse 275
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
देव्युवाच ।
नरयुद्धं यमयुद्धं त्वया प्रोक्तं महेश्वर ।
इदानीं देवदेवेश वशीकरणकं वद ॥२७५॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
devyuvāca ।
narayuddhaṃ yamayuddhaṃ tvayā proktaṃ maheśvara ।
idānīṃ devadeveśa vaśīkaraṇakaṃ vada ॥275॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
devī - Goddess Pārvatī; uvāca - said; narayuddhaṃ - human battles; yamayuddhaṃ - battle with Death; tvayā - by you; proktaṃ - explained; maheśvara - O Great Lord; idānīṃ - now; devadeveśa - O Lord of Lords; vaśīkaraṇakaṃ - the science of attraction/subjugation; vada - please tell.

**English Translation:**  
Goddess Pārvatī said: "O Maheśvara, You have described the science of victory in human battles and battles with Death. Now, O Lord of Lords, please impart the knowledge of Vaśīkaraṇa (the science of bringing others under control)."

---

---

### Verse 276
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ईश्वर उवाच ।
चन्द्रं सूर्येण चाकृष्य स्थापयेज्जीवमण्डले ।
आजन्मवशगा रामा कथितेयं तपोधनैः ॥२७६॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
īśvara uvāca ।
candraṃ sūryeṇa cākṛṣya sthāpayejjīvamaṇḍale ।
ājanmavaśagā rāmā kathiteyaṃ tapodhanaiḥ ॥276॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
īśvara - Lord Śiva; uvāca - said; candraṃ - the lunar breath (of the woman); sūryeṇa - by the solar breath (of the man); ca - and; ākṛṣya - having drawn; sthāpayet - should establish; jīvamaṇḍale - in the realm of the active breath; ājanma-vaśagā - under control for life; rāmā - the woman; kathitā-iyaṃ - this is declared; tapodhanaiḥ - by the great ascetics.

**English Translation:**  
Lord Śiva said: "If a man draws in a woman's lunar breath with his solar breath and establishes it within his own active life-breath (jīva-maṇḍala), that woman will remain under his control for her entire life. This practice, suited for married couples, has been taught by great ascetics."

---

---

### Verse 277
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवेन गृह्यते जीवो जीवो जीवाय दीयते ।
जीवस्थानगतो जीवो वशगो जीवितान्तकम् ॥२७७॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvena gṛhyate jīvo jīvo jīvāya dīyate ।
jīvasthānagato jīvo vaśago jīvitāntakam ॥277॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvena - by the active breath; gṛhyate - is grasped; jīvaḥ - the active breath (of the partner); jīvaḥ - the active breath; jīvāya - to the active breath; dīyate - is given; jīvasthānagataḥ - reached the seat of life; jīvaḥ - the vital energy; vaśagaḥ - under control; jīvitāntakam - until the end of life.

**English Translation:**  
One should grasp the partner’s active breath with their own active breath, and give their active breath into the partner’s. When this exchange reaches the very core of life energy, the person is brought completely under control until the end of life.

---

---

### Verse 278
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रात्र्यन्तयामवेलायां प्रसुप्ते कामिनीजने ।
ब्रह्मजीवं पिबेद्यस्तु बालाप्राणहरो नरः ॥२७८॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
rātryantayāmavelāyāṃ prasupte kāminījane ।
brahmajīvaṃ pibedyastu bālāprāṇaharo naraḥ ॥278॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
rātryantayāmavelāyāṃ - during the final watch of the night; prasupte - while sleeping; kāminījane - the beloved woman; brahmajīvaṃ - the Suṣumnā breath; pibet - drinks; yaḥ - who; tu - indeed; bālā-prāṇaharaḥ - one who captivates the breath/life of the young woman; naraḥ - man.

**English Translation:**  
During the final watch (quarter) of the night, while his beloved is fast asleep, the man who conceptually 'drinks' her Suṣumnā breath (Brahma-jīva) captivates her vital energy and completely wins her over.

---

---

### Verse 279
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अष्टाक्षरं जपित्वा तु तस्मिन्काले गते सति ।
तत्क्षणं दीयते चन्द्रो मोहमायाति कामिनी ॥२७९॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
aṣṭākṣaraṃ japitvā tu tasminkāle gate sati ।
tatkṣaṇaṃ dīyate candro mohamāyāti kāminī ॥279॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
aṣṭākṣaraṃ - the eight-syllabled mantra; japitvā - having chanted; tu - and; tasmin - that; kāle - time; gate - having passed; sati - being so; tatkṣaṇaṃ - instantly; dīyate - is given; candraḥ - the lunar breath; moham - infatuation/attraction; āyāti - attains; kāminī - the woman.

**English Translation:**  
After that time has passed, if one chants the eight-syllable mantra and conceptually imparts his lunar breath (Chandra Nādī) to her, the woman instantly becomes deeply infatuated and devoted.

---

---

### Verse 280
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शयने वा प्रसङ्गे वा युवत्याः स्पर्शनेऽपि वा ।
यः सूर्येण पिबेच्चन्द्रं स भवेन्मकरध्वजः ॥२८०॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
śayane vā prasaṅge vā yuvatyāḥ sparśane'pi vā ।
yaḥ sūryeṇa pibeccandraṃ sa bhavenmakaradhvajaḥ ॥280॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śayane - while sleeping; vā - or; prasaṅge - during intimacy; vā - or; yuvatyāḥ - of the young woman; sparśane - while touching; api - even; vā - or; yaḥ - who; sūryeṇa - with the solar breath; pibet - drinks; candraṃ - the lunar breath; saḥ - he; bhavet - becomes; makaradhvajaḥ - the god of love (Kāmadeva).

**English Translation:**  
Whether while resting, during physical intimacy, or even by mere touch, the man who 'drinks' the woman's lunar breath using his own solar breath becomes as attractive as Kāmadeva (the God of Love) to her.

---

---

### Verse 281
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शिव आलिङ्ग्यते शक्त्या प्रसङ्गे दक्षिणेऽपि वा ।
तत्क्षणं दापयेद्यस्तु मोहयेत्कामिनीशतम् ॥२८१॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
śiva āliṅgyate śaktyā prasaṅge dakṣiṇe'pi vā ।
tatkṣaṇaṃ dāpayedyastu mohayetkāminīśatam ॥281॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śivaḥ - the solar breath (Śiva); āliṅgyate - is embraced; śaktyā - by the lunar breath (Śakti); prasaṅge - during intercourse; dakṣiṇe - in the right breath; api - even; vā - or; tatkṣaṇaṃ - instantly; dāpayet - imparts; yaḥ - who; tu - indeed; mohayet - infatuates; kāminī-śatam - a hundred women.

**English Translation:**  
During intimacy, if the solar breath (Śiva) is actively embraced by the lunar breath (Śakti), or if a man transfers his active right breath to his partner, he attains the charm to infatuate and enchant hundreds of women.

---

---

### Verse 282
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सप्तत्रिनवपञ्चाख्यवारं सङ्गम्य सूर्यभे ।
चन्द्रे द्विचतुष्षट्कृत्वा वश्या भवति कामिनी ॥२८२॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
saptatrinavapañcākhyavāraṃ saṅgamya sūryabhe ।
candre dvicatuṣṣaṭkṛtvā vaśyā bhavati kāminī ॥282॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sapta - seven; tri - three; nava - nine; pañca - five; ākhya - named; vāraṃ - times; saṅgamya - uniting/giving; sūryabhe - in the solar phase; candre - in the lunar phase; dvi - two; catuṣ - four; ṣaṭ - six; kṛtvā - times doing; vaśyā - subjugated; bhavati - becomes; kāminī - the woman.

**English Translation:**  
If a man merges his solar breath with the woman's lunar breath three, five, seven, or nine times, or exchanges his lunar breath two, four, or six times, the woman comes entirely under his loving submission.

---

---

### Verse 283
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सूर्यचन्द्रौ समाकृष्य सर्पाकृत्याधरोष्ठयोः ।
मुखपद्मे मुखं स्पृष्ट्वा वारंवारमिदं चरेत् ॥२८३॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
sūryacandrau samākṛṣya sarpākṛtyādharoṣṭhayoḥ ।
mukhapadme mukhaṃ spṛṣṭvā vāraṃvāramidaṃ caret ॥283॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sūryacandrau - the solar and lunar breaths; samākṛṣya - having drawn together; sarpākṛtyā - in a serpentine motion; adharoṣṭhayoḥ - on the lower lips; mukhapadme - on the lotus-like mouth; mukhaṃ - mouth; spṛṣṭvā - having touched; vāraṃvāram - repeatedly; idaṃ - this; caret - should practice.

**English Translation:**  
Drawing both the solar and lunar breaths in a serpentine rhythm over the lower lips, and gently touching his mouth to her lotus-like mouth, he should practice this rhythmic exchange of breath repeatedly.

---

---

### Verse 284
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आपानं मुखपद्मस्य यावन्निद्रावशं गता ।
पश्चाज्जागृतिवेलायां चुम्बयेद्गण्डचक्षुषी ॥२८४॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
āpānaṃ mukhapadmasya yāvannidrāvaśaṃ gatā ।
paścājjāgṛtivelāyāṃ cumbayedgaṇḍacakṣuṣī ॥284॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
āpānaṃ - drinking (kissing); mukhapadmasya - of the lotus-mouth; yāvat - as long as; nidrāvaśaṃ - into the grip of sleep; gatā - she goes; paścāt - afterwards; jāgṛtivelāyāṃ - at the time of waking; cumbayet - should kiss; gaṇḍacakṣuṣī - cheeks and eyes.

**English Translation:**  
He should continue drinking from her lotus-mouth until she falls deeply asleep. Later, when she wakes up, he should tenderly kiss her cheeks and eyes.

---

---

### Verse 285
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अनेन विधिना कामी वशयेत्सर्वकामिनीः ।
इदं न वाच्यमन्यस्मिन्सत्याज्ञा पारमेश्वरी ॥२८५॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
anena vidhinā kāmī vaśayetsarvakāminīḥ ।
idaṃ na vācyamanyasminsatyājñā pārameśvarī ॥285॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
anena - by this; vidhinā - method; kāmī - a passionate man; vaśayet - can subjugate; sarva-kāminīḥ - all women; idaṃ - this; na vācyam - should not be told; anyasmin - to anyone else; satyājñā - true command; pārameśvarī - the supreme lord's.

**English Translation:**  
By this method, a passionate man can captivate the hearts of all women. However, this secret science must not be revealed to the unworthy. This is My supreme divine, true command.

---

---

### Verse 286
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**
