ऋतुस्नाता भवेन्नारी पञ्चमेऽहनि या भवेत् ।
सूर्यचन्द्रमसोर्योगे सेवनात्पुत्रसंभवः ॥२८६॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
ṛtusnātā bhavennārī pañcame'hani yā bhavet ।
sūryacandramasoryoge sevanātputrasaṃbhavaḥ ॥286॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ṛtusnātā - bathed after menstruation; bhavet - is; nārī - woman; pañcame-ahani - on the fifth day; yā - who; bhavet - is; sūryacandramasor-yoge - in the union of the sun (man) and moon (woman) breaths; sevanāt - from intercourse; putra-saṃbhavaḥ - birth of a son.

**English Translation:**  
When a woman has bathed on the fifth day after menstruation, if the couple engages in intimacy while the man's solar breath and the woman's lunar breath are active, a son will be conceived.

---

---

### Verse 287
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शङ्खवल्लीं गवां दुग्धे पृथ्व्यापो वहते यदा ।
भर्तारमेव वदेद्वाक्यं देहि देहि त्रिभिर्वचः ॥२८७॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
śaṅkhavallīṃ gavāṃ dugdhe pṛthvyāpo vahate yadā ।
bhartārameva vadedvākyaṃ dehi dehi tribhirvacaḥ ॥287॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śaṅkhavallīṃ - the Shankhapushpi herb; gavāṃ dugdhe - in cow's milk; pṛthvī-āpaḥ - earth or water elements; vahate - flows; yadā - when; bhartāram - to the husband; eva - only; vadet - should say; vākyaṃ - the sentence; dehi dehi - "give, give"; tribhiḥ - three; vacaḥ - times.

**English Translation:**  
When either the earth or water element is active, the woman should consume the Śaṅkhapuṣpī herb mixed in cow’s milk, and she should repeatedly request her husband three times, saying, "Give it to me, give it to me."

---

---

### Verse 288
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ऋतुस्नाता पिबेन्नारी ऋतुदानं तु योजयेत् ।
रूपलावण्यसंपन्नो नरसिंहः प्रसूयते ॥२८८॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
ṛtusnātā pibennārī ṛtudānaṃ tu yojayet ।
rūpalāvaṇyasaṃpanno narasiṃhaḥ prasūyate ॥288॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ṛtusnātā - bathed after menstruation; pibet - should drink; nārī - the woman; ṛtudānaṃ - the gift of fertility (intercourse); tu - and; yojayet - should perform; rūpa-lāvaṇya-saṃpannaḥ - endowed with beauty and charm; narasiṃhaḥ - a lion among men; prasūyate - is born.

**English Translation:**  
A woman cleansed after her menstrual cycle should drink this preparation, and the husband should bestow his fertility. From this union, a son endowed with exceptional beauty, charm, and the courage of a lion is born.

---

---

### Verse 289
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सुषुम्नासूर्यवाहेन ऋतुदानं च योजयेत् ।
अङ्गहीनः पुमान्यस्तु जायते चासद्विग्रहः ॥२८९॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
suṣumnāsūryavāhena ṛtudānaṃ ca yojayet ।
aṅgahīnaḥ pumānyastu jāyate cāsadvigrahaḥ ॥289॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
suṣumnā-sūrya-vāhena - with the flow of Suṣumnā alongside the solar breath; ṛtudānaṃ - fertility gift; ca - and; yojayet - gives; aṅgahīnaḥ - devoid of limbs/defective; pumān - man; yaḥ - who; tu - indeed; jāyate - is born; ca - and; asadvigrahaḥ - with a deformed body.

**English Translation:**  
However, if the husband grants the gift of fertility when his Suṣumnā Nādī is flowing alongside the solar breath, the offspring born will be physically defective and deformed in body.

---

---

### Verse 290
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
विषमाङ्के दिवारात्रौ विषमाङ्के दिनाधिपः ।
चन्द्रेणाद्यतत्त्वेषु वन्ध्या पुत्रमवाप्नुयात् ॥२९०॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
viṣamāṅke divārātrau viṣamāṅke dinādhipaḥ ।
candreṇādyatattveṣu vandhyā putramavāpnuyāt ॥290॥

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
viṣamāṅke - on odd days; divārātrau - day or night; viṣamāṅke - in odd signs/periods; dinādhipaḥ - the lord of the day (sun breath); candreṇa - with the moon breath; ādya-tattveṣu - in the first three elements (earth, water, fire); vandhyā - a barren woman; putram - a son; avāpnuyāt - can obtain.

**English Translation:**  
If intimacy occurs on an odd-numbered day following menstruation (day or night) while the man's solar breath is active and the woman's lunar breath is active, combined with the presence of the primary elements (earth, water, or fire), even a barren woman can conceive a son.

---

---

### Verse 291
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथाऽरिष्टलक्षणस्वरो विचारः —  
अहोरात्रं वहेत् सूर्यो दिनमेकं निरन्तरम् ।  
त्रिभिर्वर्षैर्भवेन्मृत्युस्तत्त्वाख्यैः प्रतिपादितम् ॥ २९१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*athā'riṣṭalakṣaṇasvaro vicāraḥ —*  
*ahorātraṁ vahet sūryo dinamekaṁ nirantaram |*  
*tribhirvarṣairbhavetmṛtyustattvākhyaiḥ pratipāditam || 291 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*athā'riṣṭalakṣaṇasvaraḥ vicāraḥ* = evaluation of breath signs portending death (Ariṣṭa); *ahorātram vahet sūryaḥ* = if right solar nostril flows continuously day and night; *dinamekam nirantaram* = for 1 single full day; *tribhiḥ varṣaiḥ bhavet mṛtyuḥ* = death occurs in 3 years; *tattvākhyaiḥ pratipāditam* = established by knowers of Tattvas.

**English Translation:**  
**Lifespan Diagnostics from Continuous Nostril Flow:**  
If the **right solar nostril** (Piṅgalā) flows continuously day and night for **1 full day** without shifting to the left, death will occur within **3 years**.

---

---

---

### Verse 292
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अहोरात्रद्वयं यस्य पिङ्गला तु निरन्तरम् ।  
द्विभिर्वर्षैर्भवेन्मृत्युस्तत्त्वज्ञानविचाक्षणैः ॥ २९२ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*ahorātradvayaṁ yasya piṅgalā tu nirantaram |*  
*dvibhirvarṣairbhavetmṛtyustattvajñānavicākṣaṇaiḥ || 292 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*ahorātradvayam yasya* = whose breath flows for 2 full days and nights; *piṅgalā tu nirantaram* = right solar Pingala continuously; *dvibhiḥ varṣaiḥ bhavet mṛtyuḥ* = death occurs in 2 years; *tattvajñānavicākṣaṇaiḥ* = according to experts in breath science.

**English Translation:**  
If the **right solar nostril** flows unceasingly day and night for **2 consecutive days**, death occurs within **2 years**.

---

---

---

### Verse 293
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अहोरात्रत्रयं यस्य पिङ्गला तु निरन्तरम् ।  
एकेन वर्षेण मृत्युः सर्वशास्त्रेषु सम्मतः ॥ २९३ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*ahorātratrayaṁ yasya piṅgalā tu nirantaram |*  
*ekena varṣeṇa mṛtyuḥ sarvaśāstreṣu sammataḥ || 293 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*ahorātratrayam yasya* = whose breath flows for 3 full days and nights; *piṅgalā tu nirantaram* = right solar nostril continuously; *ekena varṣeṇa mṛtyuḥ* = death within 1 year; *sarvaśāstreṣu sammataḥ* = agreed upon in all scriptures.

**English Translation:**  
If the **right solar nostril** flows continuously day and night for **3 consecutive days**, death occurs within **1 year**.

---

---

### Verse 294
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिवीबीजे पुत्रः कन्यका तु प्रभञ्जने ।
तेजसि गर्भपातः स्यान्नभस्यपि नपुंसकः ॥ २९४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivībīje putraḥ kanyakā tu prabhañjane |
tejasi garbhapātaḥ syānnabhasyapi napuṃsakaḥ || 294 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivī - earth element; bīje - in seed (conception); putraḥ - son; kanyakā - daughter; tu - however; prabhañjane - in air element; tejasi - in fire element; garbhapātaḥ - miscarriage/abortion; syāt - becomes; nabhasyapi - and in space element; napuṃsakaḥ - eunuch/hermaphrodite.
**English Translation:**  
If conception occurs during the active phase of the earth element, a son is born; if during the air element, a daughter. If it occurs during the fire element, it results in a miscarriage, and in the space element, a eunuch is born.

---

---

### Verse 295
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रे स्त्री पुरुषः सूर्यो मध्यभागे नपुंसकः ।
गर्भप्रश्ने यदा दूतः पूर्णे पुत्रः प्रजायते ॥ २९५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
candre strī puruṣaḥ sūryo madhyabhāge napuṃsakaḥ |
garbhapraśne yadā dūtaḥ pūrṇe putraḥ prajāyate || 295 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candre - in the moon (Iḍā nāḍī); strī - female; puruṣaḥ - male; sūryaḥ - sun (Piṅgalā nāḍī); madhyabhāge - in the middle path (Suṣumnā); napuṃsakaḥ - eunuch; garbhapraśne - regarding a question about pregnancy; yadā - when; dūtaḥ - messenger/inquirer; pūrṇe - on the full (active) side; putraḥ - son; prajāyate - is born.
**English Translation:**  
If a question about pregnancy is asked when the lunar breath (Iḍā) is active, a female child will be born; if the solar breath (Piṅgalā) is active, a male child; and if the middle breath (Suṣumnā) is active, a eunuch. If the messenger approaches from the active breath side, a son is born.

---

---

### Verse 296
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शून्ये शून्यं युगं युग्मं गर्भपातश्च संक्रमे ।
तत्त्ववित् स विजानीयात् कथितं तत् सुन्दरि ॥ २९६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śūnye śūnyaṃ yugaṃ yugmaṃ garbhapātaśca saṃkrame |
tattvavit sa vijānīyāt kathitaṃ tat sundari || 296 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śūnye - in the empty (inactive) breath; śūnyaṃ - void (no child); yugaṃ - during transition; yugmaṃ - twins; garbhapātaḥ - miscarriage; ca - and; saṃkrame - in transition of elements/breath; tattvavit - the knower of elements; sa - he; vijānīyāt - should know; kathitaṃ - told; tat - that; sundari - O beautiful one.
**English Translation:**  
If the messenger is on the empty (inactive) side, there will be no conception. If the breaths transition, twins are born. In the transition of the elements (or during Suṣumnā), there is a miscarriage. The knower of the elements should understand it as described, O beautiful one.

---

---

### Verse 297
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गर्भाधानं मारुते स्याद्दुःखी दिक्षु ख्यातो वारुणे सौख्ययुक्तः ।
गर्भस्रावः स्वल्पजीवो वह्नौ भोगी भव्यः पार्थिवे नार्थयुक्तः ॥ २९७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
garbhādhānaṃ mārute syādduḥkhī dikṣu khyāto vāruṇe saukhyayuktaḥ |
garbhasrāvaḥ svalpajīvo vahnau bhogī bhavyaḥ pārthive nārthayuktaḥ || 297 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
garbhādhānaṃ - conception; mārute - in air element; syāt - becomes; duḥkhī - sorrowful; dikṣu - in all directions; khyātaḥ - famous; vāruṇe - in water element; saukhyayuktaḥ - endowed with happiness; garbhasrāvaḥ - miscarriage; svalpajīvaḥ - short-lived; vahnau - in fire element; bhogī - enjoyer; bhavyaḥ - handsome/auspicious; pārthive - in earth element; nārthayuktaḥ - endowed with wealth.
**English Translation:**  
If conception occurs during the air element, the child will be sorrowful; if in the water element, famous everywhere and happy. In the fire element, it leads to miscarriage or a short lifespan. In the earth element, the child will be an enjoyer of life, magnificent, and wealthy.

---

---

### Verse 298
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
धनवान् सौख्ययुक्तश्च भोगवानर्थसंस्थितिः ।
स्यान्नित्यं वारुणे तच्च व्योम्नि गर्भो विनश्यति ॥ २९८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dhanavān saukhyayuktaśca bhogavānarthasaṃsthitiḥ |
syānnityaṃ vāruṇe tacca vyomni garbho vinaśyati || 298 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dhanavān - wealthy; saukhyayuktaḥ - endowed with happiness; ca - and; bhogavān - enjoying pleasures; arthasaṃsthitiḥ - stable in wealth; syāt - becomes; nityaṃ - always; vāruṇe - in the water element; tat - that; ca - and; vyomni - in the space element; garbhaḥ - pregnancy/fetus; vinaśyati - is destroyed.
**English Translation:**  
A conception during the water element always results in a child who is wealthy, happy, enjoys pleasures, and has financial stability. However, a conception during the space element leads to the destruction of the fetus.

---

---

### Verse 299
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
छिद्रे सतोत्पत्तिर्वारुणे दुहिता भवेत् ।
शेषेषु गर्भहानिः स्याज्जातमात्रस्य वा मृतिः ॥ २९९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
chidre satotpattirvāruṇe duhitā bhavet |
śeṣeṣu garbhahāniḥ syājjātamātrasya vā mṛtiḥ || 299 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
chidre - in the opening (earth element); sat - good; utpattiḥ - birth; vāruṇe - in water element; duhitā - daughter; bhavet - becomes; śeṣeṣu - in the remaining elements; garbhahāniḥ - loss of fetus; syāt - may be; jātamātrasya - of the newborn; vā - or; mṛtiḥ - death.
**English Translation:**  
If conception happens in the earth element, a good offspring is born; in the water element, a daughter is born. In the remaining elements, there will be a loss of the pregnancy or the immediate death of the newborn.

---

---

### Verse 300
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रविमध्यगतश्चन्द्रश्चन्द्रमध्यगतो रविः ।
ज्ञातव्यं गुरुतः शीघ्रं न वेदैः शास्त्रकोटिभिः ॥ ३०० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ravimadhyagataścandraścandramadhyagato raviḥ |
jñātavyaṃ gurutah śīghraṃ na vedaiḥ śāstrakoṭibhiḥ || 300 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ravi - sun; madhyagataḥ - situated in the middle; candraḥ - moon; candra - moon; madhyagataḥ - situated in the middle; raviḥ - sun; jñātavyaṃ - should be known; gurutah - from the Guru; śīghraṃ - quickly; na - not; vedaiḥ - by the Vedas; śāstrakoṭibhiḥ - by tens of millions of scriptures.
**English Translation:**  
The mystery of the moon situated within the active sun breath, and the sun situated within the active moon breath, should be quickly learned directly from the Guru, and not from the Vedas or millions of other scriptures.

---

---

### Verse 301
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चैत्रशुक्लप्रतिपदि प्रातस्तत्त्वविभेदतः ।
पश्येद्विचक्षणो योगी दक्षिणे चोत्तरायणे ॥ ३०१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
caitraśuklapratipadi prātastattvavibhedataḥ |
paśyedvicakṣaṇo yogī dakṣiṇe cottarāyaṇe || 301 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
caitra - the month of Caitra; śuklapratipadi - on the first day of the bright fortnight; prātaḥ - in the morning; tattva - elements; vibhedataḥ - by distinguishing; paśyet - should observe; vicakṣaṇaḥ - wise; yogī - practitioner of yoga; dakṣiṇe - in the southern solstice; ca - and; uttarāyaṇe - in the northern solstice.
**English Translation:**  
On the first day of the bright half of the month of Caitra, in the morning, the wise Yogi should predict the events of the coming year based on the differences of the elements, and similarly during the southern and northern solstices.

---

---

### Verse 302
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रोदयस्य वेलायां वहमानोऽथ तत्त्वतः ।
पृथिव्यापस्तथा वायुः सुभिक्षं सर्वसस्यजम् ॥ ३०२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
candrodayasya velāyāṃ vahamāno'tha tattvataḥ |
pṛthivyāpastathā vāyuḥ subhikṣaṃ sarvasasyajam || 302 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candrodayasya - of the rise of the lunar breath; velāyāṃ - at the time; vahamānaḥ - flowing; atha - then; tattvataḥ - by the element; pṛthivī - earth; āpaḥ - water; tathā - and; vāyuḥ - air; subhikṣaṃ - abundance of food; sarvasasyajam - produced by all crops.
**English Translation:**  
At the time of the lunar breath's rising, if the active element is earth, water, or air, it indicates an abundance of food and a successful harvest of all crops for the year.

---

---

### Verse 303
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तेजोव्योम्नोर्भयं घोरं दुर्भिक्षं कालतत्त्वतः ।
एवं तत्त्वफलं ज्ञेयं वर्षमासदिनेष्वपि ॥ ३०३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tejovyomnorbhayaṃ ghoraṃ durbhikṣaṃ kālatattvataḥ |
evaṃ tattvaphalaṃ jñeyaṃ varṣamāsadineṣvapi || 303 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tejaḥ - fire; vyomnoḥ - and of space; bhayaṃ - fear; ghoraṃ - terrible; durbhikṣaṃ - famine; kāla - time; tattvataḥ - from the element; evaṃ - thus; tattvaphalaṃ - the fruit of the elements; jñeyaṃ - should be known; varṣa - year; māsa - month; dineṣu - in days; api - also.
**English Translation:**  
If the fire or space elements are flowing at that time, it foretells terrible fear and famine. The results of the elements should be similarly understood for the year, the month, and even the individual days.

---

---

### Verse 304
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मध्यमा भवति क्रूरा दुष्टा सर्वेषु कर्मसु ।
देशभङ्गं महारोगं क्लेशकष्टादिदुःखदा ॥ ३०४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
madhyamā bhavati krūrā duṣṭā sarveṣu karmasu |
deśabhaṅgaṃ mahārogaṃ kleśakaṣṭādiduḥkhadā || 304 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
madhyamā - the middle breath (Suṣumnā); bhavati - is; krūrā - cruel; duṣṭā - wicked; sarveṣu - in all; karmasu - actions; deśabhaṅgaṃ - destruction of the country; mahārogaṃ - terrible disease; kleśa - affliction; kaṣṭa - hardship; ādi - etc.; duḥkha - sorrow; dā - giving.
**English Translation:**  
The middle breath (Suṣumnā) is considered cruel and inauspicious for all actions. Its flow at this crucial time brings about the destruction of the nation, great diseases, afflictions, hardships, and various sorrows.

---

---

### Verse 305
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मेषसंक्रान्तिवेलायां स्वरभेदं विचारयेत् ।
संवत्सरफलं ब्रूयाल्लोकानां तत्त्वचिन्तकः ॥ ३०५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
meṣasaṃkrāntivelāyāṃ svarabhedaṃ vicārayet |
saṃvatsaraphalaṃ brūyāllokānāṃ tattvacintakaḥ || 305 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
meṣa - Aries; saṃkrānti - transit; velāyāṃ - at the time; svarabhedaṃ - the difference in breath; vicārayet - should consider; saṃvatsaraphalaṃ - the fruit of the year; brūyāt - should declare; lokānāṃ - to the people; tattvacintakaḥ - the contemplator of the elements.
**English Translation:**  
At the time of the sun's transit into Aries (Meṣa Saṅkrānti), one should examine the differences in the breath. The contemplator of the elements can thereby predict the events of the coming year for the people.

---

---

### Verse 306
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पृथिव्यादितत्त्वेन दिनमासाब्दजं फलम् ।
शोभनं च यथा दुष्टं व्योममारुतवह्निभिः ॥ ३०६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pṛthivyāditattvena dinamāsābdajaṃ phalam |
śobhanaṃ ca yathā duṣṭaṃ vyomamārutavahnibhiḥ || 306 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pṛthivī - earth; ādi - etc.; tattvena - by the element; dina - day; māsa - month; abda - year; jaṃ - born of; phalam - fruit; śobhanaṃ - auspicious; ca - and; yathā - just as; duṣṭaṃ - inauspicious; vyoma - space; māruta - air; vahnibhiḥ - by fire.
**English Translation:**  
The flow of earth and water elements brings auspicious results for the day, month, and year, just as the space, air, and fire elements bring inauspicious and negative results.

---

---

### Verse 307
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सुभिक्षं राष्ट्रवृद्धिः स्याद्बहुसस्या वसुन्धरा ।
बहुवृष्टिस्तथा सौख्यं पृथ्वीतत्त्वं वहेद्यदि ॥ ३०७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
subhikṣaṃ rāṣṭravṛddhiḥ syādbahusasyā vasundharā |
bahuvṛṣṭistathā saukhyaṃ pṛthvītattvaṃ vahedyadi || 307 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
subhikṣaṃ - abundance of food; rāṣṭra - nation; vṛddhiḥ - growth/prosperity; syāt - becomes; bahu - much; sasyā - crops; vasundharā - the earth; bahuvṛṣṭiḥ - much rain; tathā - and; saukhyaṃ - happiness; pṛthvītattvaṃ - earth element; vahet - flows; yadi - if.
**English Translation:**  
If the earth element flows (during Meṣa Saṅkrānti), there will be an abundance of food, prosperity for the nation, and the earth will yield abundant crops. There will be plenty of rain and general happiness.

---

---

### Verse 308
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अतिवृष्टिः सुभिक्षं स्यादारोग्यं सौख्यमेव च ।
बहुसस्या तथा पृथ्वी अप्तत्त्वं च वहेद्यदि ॥ ३०८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ativṛṣṭiḥ subhikṣaṃ syādārogyaṃ saukhyameva ca |
bahusasyā tathā pṛthvī aptattvaṃ ca vahedyadi || 308 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ativṛṣṭiḥ - abundant rain; subhikṣaṃ - plentiful food; syāt - becomes; ārogyaṃ - health; saukhyam - happiness; eva - indeed; ca - and; bahusasyā - abundant crops; tathā - thus; pṛthvī - earth; aptattvaṃ - water element; ca - and; vahet - flows; yadi - if.
**English Translation:**  
If the water element flows, there will be abundant rain, plentiful food, health, and happiness. The earth will likewise be rich with abundant harvests.

---

---

### Verse 309
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दुर्भिक्षं राष्ट्रभङ्गः स्यादुत्पत्तिश्च विनश्यति ।
अल्पादल्पतरा वृष्टिरग्नितत्त्वं वहेद्यदि ॥ ३०९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
durbhikṣaṃ rāṣṭrabhaṅgaḥ syādutpattiśca vinaśyati |
alpādalpatarā vṛṣṭiragnitattvaṃ vahedyadi || 309 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
durbhikṣaṃ - famine; rāṣṭrabhaṅgaḥ - destruction of the nation; syāt - becomes; utpattiḥ - production (of crops); ca - and; vinaśyati - is destroyed; alpāt - than little; alpatarā - lesser; vṛṣṭiḥ - rain; agnitattvaṃ - fire element; vahet - flows; yadi - if.
**English Translation:**  
If the fire element flows, there will be famine and the destruction of the nation. Agricultural production will be ruined, and rainfall will be extremely scarce.

---

---

### Verse 310
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
उत्पातोपद्रवाभीतिरल्पा वृष्टिः स्युरीतयः ।
मेषसंक्रान्तिवेलायां वायुतत्त्वं वहेद्यदि ॥ ३१० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
utpātopadravābhītiralpā vṛṣṭiḥ syurītayaḥ |
meṣasaṃkrāntivelāyāṃ vāyutattvaṃ vahedyadi || 310 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
utpāta - calamities; upadrava - disturbances; bhītiḥ - fear; alpā - little; vṛṣṭiḥ - rain; syuḥ - there will be; ītayaḥ - plagues/pestilence; meṣa - Aries; saṃkrānti - transit; velāyāṃ - at the time; vāyutattvaṃ - air element; vahet - flows; yadi - if.
**English Translation:**  
If the air element flows at the time of the sun's transit into Aries, there will be calamities, disturbances, fear, very little rainfall, and pestilence.

---

---

### Verse 311
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मेषसंक्रान्तिवेलायां व्योमतत्त्वं वहेद्यदि ।
तत्रापि शून्यता ज्ञेया सस्यादीनां सुखस्य च ॥ ३११ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
meṣasaṃkrāntivelāyāṃ vyomatattvaṃ vahedyadi |
tatrāpi śūnyatā jñeyā sasyādīnāṃ sukhasya ca || 311 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
meṣa - Aries; saṃkrānti - transit; velāyāṃ - at the time; vyomatattvaṃ - space element; vahet - flows; yadi - if; tatrāpi - there also; śūnyatā - emptiness/void; jñeyā - should be known; sasyādīnāṃ - of crops, etc.; sukhasya - of happiness; ca - and.
**English Translation:**  
If the space element flows at the time of the sun's transit into Aries, one should expect a complete void and absence of crops, resources, and happiness.

---

---

### Verse 312
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूर्णे प्रवेशने श्वासे सस्यं तत्त्वेन सिध्यति ।
सूर्ये चन्द्रेऽन्यथाभूते संग्रहः सर्वसिद्धिदः ॥ ३१२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrṇe praveśane śvāse sasyaṃ tattvena sidhyati |
sūrye candre'nyathābhūte saṃgrahaḥ sarvasiddhidaḥ || 312 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrṇe - in the full (active); praveśane - entering; śvāse - breath; sasyaṃ - crop; tattvena - by the element; sidhyati - succeeds; sūrye - in the sun; candre - in the moon; anyathābhūte - being otherwise; saṃgrahaḥ - collection/hoarding; sarvasiddhidaḥ - giving all success.
**English Translation:**  
If the breath is actively entering, the crop yields success according to the ruling element. But if the sun and moon breaths flow opposite to their natural favorable order, hoarding grain will yield great profit later.

---

---

### Verse 313
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
विषमे वह्नितत्त्वं स्याज्ज्ञायते केवलं नभः ।
तत्कुर्याद्वस्तुसंग्राहं द्वौ मासौ च महर्घता ॥ ३१३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
viṣame vahnitattvaṃ syājjñāyate kevalaṃ nabhaḥ |
tatkuryādvastusaṃgrāhaṃ dvau māsau ca maharghatā || 313 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
viṣame - in the uneven (solar) breath; vahnitattvaṃ - fire element; syāt - may be; jñāyate - is known; kevalaṃ - only; nabhaḥ - space element; tat - then; kuryāt - should do; vastusaṃgrāhaṃ - collection of goods; dvau - two; māsau - months; ca - and; maharghatā - extreme expensiveness.
**English Translation:**  
If the fire element flows during the solar breath, or if only the space element is perceived, one should stock up on commodities, because there will be severe inflation and high prices for the next two months.

---

---

### Verse 314
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रौद्रे संक्रमते नाडी चन्द्रमन्ते प्रसर्पिता ।
खानिर्वायुवह्नियोगेन रौरवं जगतीतले ॥ ३१४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
raudre saṃkramate nāḍī candramante prasarpitā |
khānirvāyuvahniyogena rauravaṃ jagatītale || 314 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
raudre - during the night / sun; saṃkramate - transitions; nāḍī - breath channel; candramante - towards the end in the moon; prasarpitā - flowing; khāni - space; vāyu - air; vahni - fire; yogena - by combination; rauravaṃ - hellish conditions/calamity; jagatītale - on the surface of the earth.
**English Translation:**  
If the solar breath flows continuously at night and transitions to the lunar breath only at the very end (morning), and if this occurs with the combination of space, air, and fire elements, terrible hellish calamities will strike the earth.

---

---

### Verse 315
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
महीतत्त्वे स्वरोगः स्याज्जले च जननी तथा ।
तेजसि खेटवाटीस्थः शाकिनीपितृदोषतः ॥ ३१५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
mahītattve svarogaḥ syājjale ca jananī tathā |
tejasi kheṭavāṭīsthaḥ śākinīpitṛdoṣataḥ || 315 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
mahītattve - in the earth element; svarogaḥ - one's own disease; syāt - may be; jale - in water; ca - and; jananī - mother; tathā - similarly; tejasi - in fire; kheṭavāṭīsthaḥ - roaming in the sky/graveyard; śākinī - evil spirits; pitṛdoṣataḥ - from ancestral curse/affliction.
**English Translation:**  
If a question is asked when the earth element is active, the disease originates from the self (physical imbalance); if water, from the mother (or maternal influences). If fire is active, the disease is due to wandering spirits (Śākinīs) or ancestral afflictions.

---

---

### Verse 316
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आदौ शून्यगतो दूतः पश्चात्पूर्णे विशेद्यदि ।
मूर्च्छितोऽपि ध्रुवं जीवेद्यदर्थं परिपृच्छति ॥ ३१६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ādau śūnyagato dūtaḥ paścātpūrṇe viśedyadi |
mūrcchito'pi dhruvaṃ jīvedyadarthaṃ paripṛcchati || 316 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ādau - at first; śūnyagataḥ - gone to the empty side; dūtaḥ - messenger; paścāt - later; pūrṇe - in the full (active) side; viśet - enters; yadi - if; mūrcchitaḥ - fainted/unconscious; api - even; dhruvaṃ - certainly; jīvet - will live; yadarthaṃ - for whom; paripṛcchati - asks.
**English Translation:**  
If the messenger initially approaches from the empty (inactive) side but then moves to the full (active) side, the sick person for whom they are asking will certainly live, even if they are currently unconscious.

---

---

### Verse 317
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यस्मिन्नङ्गे स्थितो जीवस्तत्रस्थः परिपृच्छति ।
तदा जीवति जीवोऽसौ यदि रोगेण पीडितः ॥ ३१७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yasminnaṅge sthito jīvastatrasthaḥ paripṛcchati |
tadā jīvati jīvo'sau yadi rogeṇa pīḍitaḥ || 317 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yasmin - in whichever; aṅge - limb/side; sthitaḥ - situated; jīvaḥ - life force (active breath); tatrasthaḥ - stationed there; paripṛcchati - asks; tadā - then; jīvati - lives; jīvaḥ - the living being; asau - that; yadi - if; rogeṇa - by disease; pīḍitaḥ - afflicted.
**English Translation:**  
If the messenger stands on the same side as the active breath of the yogi and asks the question, the patient, even if severely afflicted by disease, will survive.

---

---

### Verse 318
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दक्षिणेन यदा वायुर्दूतो रौद्राक्षरं वदेत् ।
तदा जीवति जीवोऽसौ चन्द्रे समफलं भवेत् ॥ ३१८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dakṣiṇena yadā vāyurdūto raudrākṣaraṃ vadet |
tadā jīvati jīvo'sau candre samaphalaṃ bhavet || 318 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dakṣiṇena - through the right (solar breath); yadā - when; vāyuḥ - breath; dūtaḥ - messenger; raudrākṣaraṃ - harsh syllables; vadet - speaks; tadā - then; jīvati - lives; jīvaḥ asau - that person; candre - in the lunar breath; samaphalaṃ - equal result; bhavet - is.
**English Translation:**  
If the breath flows through the right nostril and the messenger speaks harsh words, the sick person will survive. The same positive result applies if the lunar breath is active.

---

---

### Verse 319
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जीवाकारं च बद्ध्वा तु जीवाकारं विलोक्य च ।
जीवस्थो नोदितः प्रश्ने तस्य स्याज्जीवितं फलम् ॥ ३१९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jīvākāraṃ ca baddhvā tu jīvākāraṃ vilokya ca |
jīvastho noditaḥ praśne tasya syājjīvitaṃ phalam || 319 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jīvākāraṃ - the form of life; ca - and; baddhvā - having bound; tu - indeed; jīvākāraṃ - the form of life; vilokya - having seen; ca - and; jīvasthaḥ - situated in life (active breath); noditaḥ - prompted; praśne - in question; tasya - his; syāt - becomes; jīvitaṃ - life; phalam - result.
**English Translation:**  
By focusing on the form of the life force and observing the active breath, if the yogi is situated in the active breath when the question is asked, the result will be life for the patient.

---

---

### Verse 320
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामचारे तथा दक्षे प्रवेशे यत्र वाहने ।
तत्रस्थः पृच्छते दूतस्तस्य सिद्धिर्न संशयः ॥ ३२० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāmacāre tathā dakṣe praveśe yatra vāhane |
tatrasthaḥ pṛcchate dūtastasya siddhirna saṃśayaḥ || 320 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāmacāre - in the left flow; tathā - as well as; dakṣe - in the right; praveśe - in entry; yatra - where; vāhane - in the vehicle/flow; tatrasthaḥ - stationed there; pṛcchate - asks; dūtaḥ - messenger; tasya - his; siddhiḥ - success; na - no; saṃśayaḥ - doubt.
**English Translation:**  
Whether the left or right breath is flowing, if the breath is entering (inhale) and the messenger stands on the active side while asking, success is guaranteed without a doubt.

---

---

### Verse 321
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रश्ने चाधःस्थितो जीवो नूनं जीवो हि जीवति ।
ऊर्ध्वभागस्थितो जीवो जीवो याति यमालयम् ॥ ३२१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
praśne cādhaḥsthito jīvo nūnaṃ jīvo hi jīvati |
ūrdhvabhāgasthito jīvo jīvo yāti yamālayam || 321 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
praśne - at the time of question; ca - and; adhaḥsthitaḥ - situated below; jīvaḥ - life (breath/element); nūnaṃ - certainly; jīvaḥ - the living being; hi - indeed; jīvati - lives; ūrdhvabhāgasthitaḥ - situated in the upper part; jīvaḥ - life; jīvaḥ - the living being; yāti - goes; yamālayam - to the abode of Yama (death).
**English Translation:**  
If during the question the life force (element) is situated in the lower part (earth or water), the patient will certainly live. However, if it is situated in the upper part (fire, air, space), the patient will go to the abode of Yama (will die).

---

---

### Verse 322
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
विपरीताक्षरप्रश्ने रिक्तायां पृच्छको यदि ।
विपर्ययं च विज्ञेयं विषमस्योदये सति ॥ ३२२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
viparītākṣarapraśne riktāyāṃ pṛcchako yadi |
viparyayaṃ ca vijñeyaṃ viṣamasyodaye sati || 322 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
viparītākṣarapraśne - in a question with uneven/harsh letters; riktāyāṃ - on the empty side; pṛcchakaḥ - the inquirer; yadi - if; viparyayaṃ - opposite result; ca - and; vijñeyaṃ - should be known; viṣamasya - of the uneven (Suṣumnā); udaye - on the rise; sati - being.
**English Translation:**  
If the inquirer asks a question containing harsh or uneven letters while standing on the empty side, or if the uneven middle breath (Suṣumnā) is flowing, the yogi should predict the exact opposite (negative) result.

---

---

### Verse 323
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
चन्द्रस्थाने स्थितो जीवः सूर्यस्थाने तु पृच्छकः ।
तदा प्राणवियुक्तोऽसौ यदि वैद्यशतैर्वृतः ॥ ३२३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
candrasthāne sthito jīvaḥ sūryasthāne tu pṛcchakaḥ |
tadā prāṇaviyukto'sau yadi vaidyashatairvṛtaḥ || 323 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
candrasthāne = in the place of the moon (lunar nadi); sthitaḥ = situated; jīvaḥ = breath/life force; sūryasthāne = in the place of the sun (solar nadi); tu = but; pṛcchakaḥ = the questioner/messenger; tadā = then; prāṇa-viyuktaḥ = separated from life (dead); asau = he (the patient); yadi = even if; vaidya-śataiḥ = by hundreds of physicians; vṛtaḥ = surrounded.
**English Translation:**  
If one's breath is flowing in the lunar Nadi (Ida) and the messenger (questioner) is situated on the solar side (Pingala), then the patient will surely lose their life, even if they are surrounded by hundreds of physicians.

---

---

### Verse 324
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पिङ्गलायां स्थितो जीवो वामे दूतस्तु पृच्छति ।
तदापि म्रियते रोगी यदि त्राता महेश्वरः ॥ ३२४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
piṅgalāyāṃ sthito jīvo vāme dūtastu pṛcchati |
tadāpi mriyate rogī yadi trātā maheśvaraḥ || 324 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
piṅgalāyāṃ = in the Pingala (solar) nadi; sthitaḥ = situated; jīvaḥ = breath; vāme = on the left side; dūtaḥ = the messenger; tu = but; pṛcchati = asks; tadā = then; api = even; mriyate = dies; rogī = the patient; yadi = even if; trātā = the protector; maheśvaraḥ = Lord Shiva.
**English Translation:**  
If the breath is flowing in the Pingala Nadi and the messenger asks the question while standing on the left side, then the patient will die, even if Lord Shiva Himself is the protector.

---

---

### Verse 325
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
एकस्य भूतस्य विपर्ययेण रोगाभिभूतिर्भवतीह पुंसाम् ।
तयोर्द्वये बन्धुसुहृद्विपत्तिः पक्षद्वये व्यत्ययतो मृतिः स्यात् ॥ ३२५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ekasya bhūtasya viparyayeṇa rogābhibhūtirbhavatīha puṃsām |
tayordvaye bandhusuhṛdvipattiḥ pakṣadvaye vyatyayato mṛtiḥ syāt || 325 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ekasya = of one; bhūtasya = element; viparyayeṇa = by the reversal/contrariety; roga-abhibhūtiḥ = overpowering by disease; bhavati = happens; iha = here; puṃsām = to men; tayoḥ = of them; dvaye = when two (are reversed); bandhu-suhṛd-vipattiḥ = distress to relatives and friends; pakṣadvaye = in two fortnights (one month); vyatyayataḥ = from being contrary; mṛtiḥ = death; syāt = occurs.
**English Translation:**  
When one element (Tattva) functions contrary to its nature, a person is overpowered by disease. When two elements become contrary, it brings distress to friends and relatives. If this contrary state continues for two fortnights (a month), death occurs.

---

---

### Verse 326
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मासादौ चैव पक्षादौ वत्सरादौ यथाक्रमम् ।
क्षयकालं परीक्षेत वायुचारवशात्सुधीः ॥ ३२६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
māsādau caiva pakṣādau vatsarādau yathākramam |
kṣayakālaṃ parīkṣeta vāyucāravaśātsudhīḥ || 326 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
māsa-ādau = at the beginning of a month; ca = and; eva = indeed; pakṣa-ādau = at the beginning of a fortnight; vatsara-ādau = at the beginning of a year; yathākramam = respectively/in order; kṣaya-kālam = the time of death (destruction); parīkṣeta = one should examine; vāyu-cāra-vaśāt = through the flow of the breath; sudhīḥ = the wise one.
**English Translation:**  
The wise one should examine the time of death through the flow of the breath at the beginning of a month, the beginning of a fortnight, and the beginning of a year respectively.

---

---

### Verse 327
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पञ्चभूतात्मकं दीपं शिवस्नेहेन सिञ्चितम् ।
रक्षयेत्सूर्यवातेन प्राणी जीवः स्थिरो भवेत् ॥ ३२७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pañcabhūtātmakaṃ dīpaṃ śivasnehena siñcitam |
rakṣayetsūryavātena prāṇī jīvaḥ sthiro bhavet || 327 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pañca-bhūta-ātmakam = composed of the five elements; dīpam = the lamp (the body); śiva-snehena = with the oil of Shiva (spiritual essence); siñcitam = sprinkled/filled; rakṣayet = one should protect; sūrya-vātena = by the solar breath; prāṇī = the living being; jīvaḥ = life; sthiraḥ = stable; bhavet = becomes.
**English Translation:**  
One should protect this lamp of the body, which is composed of the five elements and filled with the oil of Shiva, by regulating the solar breath. Through this, the living being's life remains stable.

---

---

### Verse 328
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मारुतं बन्धयित्वा तु सूर्यं बन्धयते यदि ।
अभ्यासाज्जीवते जीवः सूर्यकालेऽपि वञ्चिते ॥ ३२८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
mārutaṃ bandhayitvā tu sūryaṃ bandhayate yadi |
abhyāsājjīvate jīvaḥ sūryakāle'pi vañcite || 328 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
mārutam = the breath; bandhayitvā = having bound/controlled; tu = indeed; sūryam = the solar breath (Pingala); bandhayate = binds; yadi = if; abhyāsāt = through practice; jīvate = lives; jīvaḥ = the living being; sūrya-kāle = in the time of the sun (daytime); api = even; vañcite = being deceived/cheated (overcome).
**English Translation:**  
If one binds the life-breath and specifically controls the solar breath during the daytime, through such continuous practice, the living being continues to live, conquering the time of death.

---

---

### Verse 329
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
गगनात्स्रवते चन्द्रः कायपद्मानि सिञ्चयेत् ।
कर्मयोगसदाभ्यासैरमरः शशिसंश्रयात् ॥ ३२९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
gaganātsravate candraḥ kāyapadmāni siñcayet |
karmayogasadābhyāsairamaraḥ śaśisaṃśrayāt || 329 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
gaganāt = from the sky (the palate); sravate = flows; candraḥ = the moon (nectar); kāya-padmāni = the lotuses of the body; siñcayet = it should irrigate; karma-yoga-sadā-abhyāsaiḥ = by the constant practice of action and yoga; amaraḥ = immortal; śaśi-saṃśrayāt = by taking refuge in the moon.
**English Translation:**  
The nectar flows from the "moon" in the firmament (the palate) and irrigates the lotuses (chakras) of the body. Through constant practice of this yogic action and by taking refuge in the lunar breath, the yogi becomes immortal.

---

---

### Verse 330
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शशाङ्कं वारयेद्रात्रौ दिवा वार्यो दिवाकरः ।
इत्यभ्यासरतो नित्यं स योगी नात्र संशयः ॥ ३३० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śaśāṅkaṃ vārayedrātrau divā vāryo divākaraḥ |
ityabhyāsarato nityaṃ sa yogī nātra saṃśayaḥ || 330 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śaśāṅkam = the moon (lunar breath); vārayet = one should ward off/stop; rātrau = at night; divā = during the day; vāryaḥ = should be warded off/stopped; divākaraḥ = the sun (solar breath); iti = thus; abhyāsa-rataḥ = devoted to practice; nityam = constantly; saḥ = he; yogī = is a yogi; na = no; atra = here; saṃśayaḥ = doubt.
**English Translation:**  
One should stop the lunar breath during the night and the solar breath during the day. He who is constantly devoted to this practice is a true yogi, without a doubt.

---

---

### Verse 331
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अहोरात्रे यदैकत्र वहते यस्य मारुतः ।
तदा तस्य भवेन्मृत्युः पूर्णे संवत्सरत्रये ॥ ३३१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ahorātre yadaikatra vahate yasya mārutaḥ |
tadā tasya bhavenmṛtyuḥ pūrṇe saṃvatsaratraye || 331 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ahorātre = day and night; yadā = when; ekatra = in one place (one nostril); vahate = flows; yasya = whose; mārutaḥ = breath; tadā = then; tasya = his; bhavet = will be; mṛtyuḥ = death; pūrṇe = fully completed; saṃvatsara-traye = in three years.
**English Translation:**  
If a person's breath flows exclusively in one nostril continuously for a full day and night, then his death will occur upon the completion of three years.

---

---

### Verse 332
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अहोरात्रद्वयं यस्य पिङ्गलायां सदा गतिः ।
तस्य वर्षद्वयं प्रोक्तं जीवितं तत्त्ववेदिभिः ॥ ३३२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ahorātradvayaṃ yasya piṅgalāyāṃ sadā gatiḥ |
tasya varṣadvayaṃ proktaṃ jīvitaṃ tattvavedibhiḥ || 332 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ahorātra-dvayam = for two days and nights; yasya = whose; piṅgalāyām = in the Pingala nadi; sadā = always; gatiḥ = movement (of breath); tasya = his; varṣa-dvayam = two years; proktam = is said; jīvitam = life; tattva-vedibhiḥ = by the knowers of reality.
**English Translation:**  
If one's breath flows continuously in the Pingala (solar) nadi for two complete days and nights, the knowers of the elements declare that his remaining lifespan is two years.

---

---

### Verse 333
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
त्रिरात्रं वहते यस्य वायुरेकपुटे स्थितः ।
तदा संवत्सरायुस्तं प्रवदन्ति मनीषिणः ॥ ३३३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
trirātraṃ vahate yasya vāyurekapuṭe sthitaḥ |
tadā saṃvatsarāyustaṃ pravadanti manīṣiṇaḥ || 333 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
trirātram = for three nights (and days); vahate = flows; yasya = whose; vāyuḥ = breath; eka-puṭe = in one nostril; sthitaḥ = situated; tadā = then; saṃvatsara-āyuḥ = a lifespan of one year; tam = to him; pravadanti = they say; manīṣiṇaḥ = the wise ones.
**English Translation:**  
If a person's breath remains and flows exclusively in one nostril for three consecutive days and nights, the wise proclaim that he has a lifespan of one year remaining.

---

---

### Verse 334
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रात्रौ चन्द्रो दिवा सूर्यो वहेद्यस्य निरन्तरम् ।
जानीयात्तस्य वै मृत्युः षण्मासाभ्यन्तरे भवेत् ॥ ३३४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
rātrau candro divā sūryo vahedyasya nirantaram |
jānīyāttasya vai mṛtyuḥ ṣaṇmāsābhyantare bhavet || 334 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
rātrau = at night; candraḥ = the moon (left nostril); divā = during the day; sūryaḥ = the sun (right nostril); vahet = if it flows; yasya = whose; nirantaram = continuously; jānīyāt = one should know; tasya = his; vai = indeed; mṛtyuḥ = death; ṣaṇ-māsa-abhyantare = within six months; bhavet = will happen.
**English Translation:**  
If a person's breath flows continuously in the lunar nostril during the night and in the solar nostril during the day, one should know that his death will occur within six months.

---

---

### Verse 335
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
लक्ष्यं लक्षितलक्षणेन सुदृढं यद्यादरेणेक्ष्यते
क्षीणो दक्षिणपश्चिमोत्तरपुरः षट्त्रिद्विमासैकतः ।
मध्ये छिद्रमिदं भवेद्दशदिनं धूमाकुलं तद्दिने
सर्वज्ञैरपि भाषितं मुनिवरैरायुःप्रमाणं स्फुटम् ॥ ३३५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
lakṣyaṃ lakṣitalakṣaṇena sudṛḍhaṃ yadyādareṇekṣyate
kṣīṇo dakṣiṇapaścimottarapuraḥ ṣaṭtridvimāsaikataḥ |
madhye chidramidaṃ bhaveddaśadinaṃ dhūmākulaṃ taddine
sarvajñairapi bhāṣitaṃ munivarairāyuḥpramāṇaṃ sphuṭam || 335 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
lakṣyam = the object/reflection; lakṣita-lakṣaṇena = by observing its characteristics; sudṛḍham = firmly; yadi = if; ādareṇa = with care; īkṣyate = is seen; kṣīṇaḥ = diminished/faded; dakṣiṇa-paścima-uttara-puraḥ = in the South, West, North, or East; ṣaṭ-tri-dvi-māsa-ekataḥ = in six, three, two, or one month(s); madhye = in the middle; chidram = a hole; idam = this; bhavet = if there is; daśa-dinam = ten days; dhūma-ākulam = filled with smoke; tat-dine = on that very day; sarvajñaiḥ = by the omniscient ones; api = even; bhāṣitam = declared; muni-varaiḥ = by the best of sages; āyuḥ-pramāṇam = measure of lifespan; sphuṭam = clearly.
**English Translation:**  
When one's reflection (in a mirror or water) is observed carefully, if it appears faded in the South, West, North, or East, death occurs in six, three, two, or one month respectively. If there appears a hole in the middle, death comes in ten days. If it looks filled with smoke, death occurs that very day. This measure of lifespan has been clearly declared even by the omniscient best of sages.

---

---

### Verse 336
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दूतः कृष्णकषायवस्त्रवसनो दन्तक्षताखण्डितस्
तैलाभ्यक्तशरीररज्जुकरो दीनश्च पूर्णान्तरः ।
भस्माङ्गारकपालपाशमुसली सूर्यास्तमायाति यः
शून्याङ्घ्रिः पदसंस्थितो गदयुतः कालेन तस्याह्वयः ॥ ३३६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dūtaḥ kṛṣṇakaṣāyavastravasano dantakṣatākhaṇḍitas
tailābhyaktaśarīrarajjukaro dīnaśca pūrṇāntaraḥ |
bhasmāṅgārakapālapāśamusalī sūryāstamāyāti yaḥ
śūnyāṅghriḥ padasaṃsthito gadayutaḥ kālena tasyāhvayaḥ || 336 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dūtaḥ = the messenger; kṛṣṇa-kaṣāya-vastra-vasanaḥ = wearing black or reddish-brown garments; danta-kṣata-akhaṇḍitaḥ = marked with teeth wounds or bald; taila-abhyakta-śarīra-rajju-karaḥ = having a body smeared with oil and holding a rope in hand; dīnaḥ = miserable; ca = and; pūrṇāntaraḥ = unyielding/stubborn; bhasma-aṅgāra-kapāla-pāśa-musalī = carrying ashes, coals, a skull, a noose, or a pestle; sūrya-astam = at sunset; āyāti = arrives; yaḥ = who; śūnyāṅghriḥ = having empty/bare feet; pada-saṃsthitaḥ = standing on foot; gada-yutaḥ = the one afflicted with disease; kālena = by death; tasya = his; āhvayaḥ = calling.
**English Translation:**  
If a messenger arrives at sunset wearing black or brownish garments, wounded or bald, body smeared with oil, holding a rope, miserable, stubborn, or carrying ashes, hot coals, a skull, a noose, or a pestle, and stands with bare feet, it signifies that the patient is being called by Death.

---

---

### Verse 337
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अकस्माच्चित्तविकृतिरस्मात् प्रकृतिरुत्तमा ।
अकस्मादिन्द्रियोत्पातः सन्निपातस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ३३७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
akasmāccittavikṛtirasmāt prakṛtiruttamā |
akasmādindriyotpātaḥ sannipātasya lakṣaṇam || 337 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
akasmāt = suddenly; citta-vikṛtiḥ = distortion/disorder of the mind; asmāt = after this; prakṛtiḥ = nature/state; uttamā = excellent; akasmāt = suddenly; indriya-utpātaḥ = disturbance of the senses; sannipātasya = of delirium (or fatal imbalance of the humors); lakṣaṇam = symptom.
**English Translation:**  
A sudden disorder of the mind, followed suddenly by an excellent/normal state, and then a sudden disturbance of the sensory organs—these are the symptoms of Sannipata (a fatal derangement of the three bodily humors).

---

---

### Verse 338
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शरीरं शीतलं यस्य प्रकृतिर्विकृता भवेत् ।
तदरिष्टं समासेन व्यासतस्तु निबोध मे ॥ ३३८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śarīraṃ śītalaṃ yasya prakṛtirvikṛtā bhavet |
tadariṣṭaṃ samāsena vyāsatastu nibodha me || 338 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śarīram = the body; śītalam = cold; yasya = whose; prakṛtiḥ = nature/constitution; vikṛtā = corrupted/altered; bhavet = becomes; tat = that; ariṣṭam = fatal sign; samāsena = in brief; vyāsataḥ = in detail; tu = but; nibodha = listen/understand; me = from me.
**English Translation:**  
The one whose body becomes abnormally cold and whose natural constitution gets altered, this in brief is an omen of death. Now listen to me explain the signs in detail.

---

---

### Verse 339
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दुष्टशब्देषु रमते शुद्धशब्देषु कुप्यति ।
पश्चात्तापो भवेद्यस्य तस्य मृत्युर्न संशयः ॥ ३३९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
duṣṭaśabdeṣu ramate śuddhaśabdeṣu kupyati |
paścāttāpo bhavedyasya tasya mṛtyurna saṃśayaḥ || 339 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
duṣṭa-śabdeṣu = in bad/foul words; ramate = delights; śuddha-śabdeṣu = at pure/good words; kupyati = gets angry; paścāttāpaḥ = remorse/regret; bhavet = happens; yasya = whose; tasya = his; mṛtyuḥ = death; na = no; saṃśayaḥ = doubt.
**English Translation:**  
He who takes delight in foul words, becomes angry at pure and wholesome words, and constantly feels remorse, will undoubtedly meet his death.

---

---

### Verse 340
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
हुङ्कारः शीतलो यस्य फूत्कारो वह्निसन्निभः ।
महावैद्यो भवेद्यस्य तस्य मृत्युर्ध्रुवं भवेत् ॥ ३४० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
huṅkāraḥ śītalo yasya phūtkāro vahnisannibhaḥ |
mahāvaidyo bhavedyasya tasya mṛtyurdhruvaṃ bhavet || 340 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
huṅkāraḥ = inhaling sound; śītalaḥ = cold; yasya = whose; phūtkāraḥ = exhaling sound (blowing); vahni-sannibhaḥ = like fire (hot); mahā-vaidyaḥ = a great physician; bhavet = may be; yasya = whose (protector); tasya = his; mṛtyuḥ = death; dhruvam = certainly; bhavet = happens.
**English Translation:**  
The one whose inhalation is cold and exhalation is hot like fire, his death is certain, even if a great physician attempts to cure him.

---

---

### Verse 341
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
जिह्वां विष्णुपदं ध्रुवं सुरपदं सन्मातृकामण्डलम्
एतान्येवमरुन्धतीममृतरुचं शुक्रं ध्रुवं वा क्षणम् ।
एतेष्वेकतमं स्फुटं न पुरुषः पश्येत्पुरः संस्थितं
सोऽवश्यं विशतीह कालवदनं संवत्सरादूर्ध्वतः ॥ ३४१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
jihvāṃ viṣṇupadaṃ dhruvaṃ surapadaṃ sanmātṛkāmaṇḍalam
etānyevamarundhatīmamṛtarucaṃ śukraṃ dhruvaṃ vā kṣaṇam |
eteṣvekatamaṃ sphuṭaṃ na puruṣaḥ paśyetpuraḥ saṃsthitaṃ
so'vaśyaṃ viśatīha kālavadanaṃ saṃvatsarādūrdhvataḥ || 341 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
jihvām = the tongue; viṣṇupadam = the sky; dhruvam = the pole star; surapadam = the path of the gods; sat-mātṛkā-maṇḍalam = the circle of divine mothers (constellations); etāni = these; evam = thus; arundhatīm = the Arundhati star; amṛta-rucam = the moon; śukram = the Venus; dhruvam = the pole star (or Agastya); vā = or; kṣaṇam = for a moment; eteṣu = among these; ekatamam = even one; sphuṭam = clearly; na = not; puruṣaḥ = a person; paśyet = sees; puraḥ = in front; saṃsthitam = situated; saḥ = he; avaśyam = surely; viśati = enters; iha = here; kāla-vadanam = the mouth of death; saṃvatsarāt-ūrdhvataḥ = after a year.
**English Translation:**  
If a person cannot clearly see his own tongue, the sky, the Pole Star, the milky way, the constellation of the divine mothers, the star Arundhati, the Moon, Venus, or even momentarily fails to see any one of these situated before him, he will undoubtedly enter the jaws of death after one year.

---

---

### Verse 342
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रश्मिबिम्बं च सूर्यस्य वन्हेः शीतांशुमालिनः ।
अदृष्ट्वैकादशमासायुर्न तदूर्ध्वं स जीवति ॥ ३४२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
raśmibimbaṃ ca sūryasya vanheḥ śītāṃśumālinaḥ |
adṛṣṭvaikādaśamāsāyurna tadūrdhvaṃ sa jīvati || 342 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
raśmi-bimbam = the shining disc/rays; ca = and; sūryasya = of the sun; vanheḥ = of the fire; śītāṃśu-mālinaḥ = of the moon (garlanded with cool rays); adṛṣṭvā = not having seen; ekādaśa-māsa-āyuḥ = a lifespan of eleven months; na = not; tat-ūrdhvam = beyond that; saḥ = he; jīvati = lives.
**English Translation:**  
If a person can no longer perceive the bright rays and the discs of the sun, the fire, and the moon, his remaining lifespan is eleven months; he does not live beyond that.

---

---

### Verse 343
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वाप्यां पुरीषमूत्राणि सुवर्णं रजतं तथा ।
प्रत्यक्षमथवा स्वप्ने दश मासान् स जीवति ॥ ३४३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāpyāṃ purīṣamūtrāṇi suvarṇaṃ rajataṃ tathā |
pratyakṣamathavā svapne daśa māsān sa jīvati || 343 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāpyām = in a stepwell; purīṣa-mūtrāṇi = feces and urine; suvarṇam = gold; rajatam = silver; tathā = also; pratyakṣam = directly (in waking state); athavā = or; svapne = in a dream; daśa = ten; māsān = months; saḥ = he; jīvati = lives.
**English Translation:**  
If one sees feces, urine, gold, or silver in a stepwell, either directly while awake or in a dream, he will live for only ten months.

---

---

### Verse 344
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
कचित् पश्यति यो दीपं सुवर्णं च कषान्वितम् ।
विरूपाणि च भूतानि नव मासान् स जीवति ॥ ३४४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
kacit paśyati yo dīpaṃ suvarṇaṃ ca kaṣānvitam |
virūpāṇi ca bhūtāni nava māsān sa jīvati || 344 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
kacit = somewhere; paśyati = sees; yaḥ = who; dīpam = a lamp; suvarṇam = gold; ca = and; kaṣa-anvitam = tested on a touchstone (or rubbed); virūpāṇi = deformed/ugly; ca = and; bhūtāni = ghosts/beings; nava = nine; māsān = months; saḥ = he; jīvati = lives.
**English Translation:**  
Whoever sees a lamp burning unexpectedly, or gold being rubbed on a touchstone, or encounters deformed and terrifying ghosts, will live for only nine months.

---

---

### Verse 345
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
स्थूलाङ्गोऽपि कृशः कृशोऽपि सहसा स्थूलत्वमारभ्यते
प्राप्तो वा कनकप्रभां यदि भवेद् गौरोऽपि कृष्णच्छविः ।
शूरो भीरुः सुधीर् अधर्मनिपुणः शान्तो विकारी पुमान्
इत्येवं प्रकृतिं प्रयाति चपलां मासाष्टकं जीवति ॥ ३४५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sthūlāṅgo'pi kṛśaḥ kṛśo'pi sahasā sthūlatvamārabhyate
prāpto vā kanakaprabhāṃ yadi bhaved gauro'pi kṛṣṇacchaviḥ |
śūro bhīruḥ sudhīr adharmanipuṇaḥ śānto vikārī pumān
ityevaṃ prakṛtiṃ prayāti capalāṃ māsāṣṭakaṃ jīvati || 345 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sthūla-aṅgaḥ = a fat-bodied person; api = even; kṛśaḥ = thin; kṛśaḥ = thin; api = even; sahasā = suddenly; sthūlatvam = fatness; ārabhyate = begins; prāptaḥ = having obtained; vā = or; kanaka-prabhām = golden complexion; yadi = if; bhavet = becomes; gauraḥ = fair-complexioned; api = even; kṛṣṇa-cchaviḥ = dark-colored; śūraḥ = brave; bhīruḥ = coward; sudhīḥ = wise; adharma-nipuṇaḥ = skilled in unrighteousness; śāntaḥ = peaceful; vikārī = agitated/distorted; pumān = a man; iti-evam = in this way; prakṛtim = nature; prayāti = goes to; capalām = fickle/unstable; māsa-aṣṭakam = eight months; jīvati = lives.
**English Translation:**  
If a man who is fat suddenly becomes very thin, or a thin man becomes fat; if one attains a strange golden hue, or a fair person turns dark; if a brave man becomes a coward, a righteous and wise man becomes unrighteous, or a peaceful man becomes agitated—such a person whose nature becomes so drastically unstable will live for only eight months.

---

---

### Verse 346
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पीडा भवेत् पाणितले च जिह्वामूले तथा स्याद्रुधिरं च कृष्णम् ।
विद्धं न वा ज्ञायते यस्य गात्रं जीवेन्मनुष्यः स हि सप्तमासान् ॥ ३४६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pīḍā bhavet pāṇitale ca jihvāmūle tathā syādrudhiraṃ ca kṛṣṇam |
viddhaṃ na vā jñāyate yasya gātraṃ jīvenmanuṣyaḥ sa hi saptamāsān || 346 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pīḍā = pain; bhavet = there should be; pāṇitale = in the palm of the hand; ca = and; jihvāmūle = at the root of the tongue; tathā = similarly; syāt = should be; rudhiram = blood; ca = and; kṛṣṇam = black; viddham = pierced/pinched; na = not; vā = or; jñāyate = is felt/known; yasya = whose; gātram = body; jīvet = would live; manuṣyaḥ = the man; saḥ = he; hi = indeed; sapta-māsān = for seven months.
**English Translation:**  
If a person experiences pain in the palms of their hands or at the root of the tongue, if their blood turns black, or if they feel no sensation when their body is pierced or pinched, that man will live for only seven months.

---

---

### Verse 347
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मध्याङ्गुलीनां त्रितयं न वक्रं रोगं विना शुष्यति यस्य कण्ठः ।
स मुह्यते प्रश्नवशेन जाड्यात् षड्भिः स मासैः प्रलयं प्रयाति ॥ ३४७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
madhyāṅgulīnāṃ tritayaṃ na vakraṃ rogaṃ vinā śuṣyati yasya kaṇṭhaḥ |
sa muhyate praśnavaśena jāḍyāt ṣaḍbhiḥ sa māsaiḥ pralayaṃ prayāti || 347 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
madhya-aṅgulīnām = of the middle fingers; tritayam = the trio; na = not; vakram = bent/curved; rogam = disease; vinā = without; śuṣyati = dries up; yasya = whose; kaṇṭhaḥ = throat; saḥ = he; muhyate = gets confused/stupefied; praśna-vaśena = by repeated questions; jāḍyāt = due to dullness/stupor; ṣaḍbhiḥ = by six; saḥ = he; māsaiḥ = months; pralayam = death/destruction; prayāti = reaches.
**English Translation:**  
If a person's three middle fingers fail to bend, or if their throat dries up without any illness, or if they become stupefied and confused when repeatedly asked questions, that person meets their end in six months.

---

---

### Verse 348
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
न यस्य स्फुरणं किंचिद्विद्यते स्तनचर्मणि ।
सोऽवश्यं पञ्चमे मासि स्कन्धारूढो भविष्यति ॥ ३४८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
na yasya sphuraṇaṃ kiṃcidvidyate stanacarmaṇi |
so'vaśyaṃ pañcame māsi skandhārūḍho bhaviṣyati || 348 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
na = not; yasya = whose; sphuraṇam = sensation/quivering; kiṃcit = any; vidyate = exists; stana-carmaṇi = in the skin of the breasts; saḥ = he; avaśyam = certainly; pañcame = in the fifth; māsi = month; skandha-ārūḍhaḥ = carried on the shoulders (as a corpse); bhaviṣyati = will be.
**English Translation:**  
The person who feels absolutely no sensation in the skin around their breasts will undoubtedly be carried on the shoulders of others (as a corpse) in the fifth month.

---

---

### Verse 349
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
यस्य न स्फुरते ज्योतिः पीड्यते नयनद्वयम् ।
मरणं तस्य निर्दिष्टं चतुर्थे मासि निश्चितम् ॥ ३४९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yasya na sphurate jyotiḥ pīḍyate nayanadvayam |
maraṇaṃ tasya nirdiṣṭaṃ caturthe māsi niścitam || 349 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yasya = whose; na = not; sphurate = flashes/manifests; jyotiḥ = light/vision; pīḍyate = is pained; nayana-dvayam = both eyes; maraṇam = death; tasya = his; nirdiṣṭam = is indicated; caturthe = in the fourth; māsi = month; niścitam = definitely.
**English Translation:**  
If the light of vision fades from a person's eyes, and both eyes suffer from continuous pain, his death is definitely indicated to occur in the fourth month.

---

---

### Verse 350
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
दन्ताश्च वृषणौ यस्य न किंचिदपि पीड्यते ।
तृतीये मासि चावश्यं कालाज्ञायां भवेन्नरः ॥ ३५० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
dantāśca vṛṣaṇau yasya na kiṃcidapi pīḍyate |
tṛtīye māsi cāvaśyaṃ kālājñāyāṃ bhavennaraḥ || 350 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
dantāḥ = teeth; ca = and; vṛṣaṇau = testicles; yasya = whose; na = not; kiṃcit = even a little; api = even; pīḍyate = feel pain (when pressed); tṛtīye = in the third; māsi = month; ca = and; avaśyam = surely; kāla-ājñāyām = under the command of Death; bhavet = will become; naraḥ = the man.
**English Translation:**  
A man who feels absolutely no pain in his teeth or testicles (even when they are pressed) will surely fall under the command of Death in the third month.

---

---

### Verse 351
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अथ कालवञ्चनोपायः —  
कालो दूरस्थितो वापि येनोपायेन वञ्च्यते ।  
तं वदामि समासेन यथा दृष्ट्वा शिवागमे ॥ ३५१ ॥

**IAST Transliteration:**  
*atha kālavañcanopāyaḥ —*  
*kālo dūrasthito vāpi yenopāyena vañcyate |*  
*taṁ vadāmi samāsena yathā dṛṣṭvā śivāgame || 351 ||*

**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
*atha kālavañcanopāyaḥ* = now techniques for deceiving/overcoming time and death; *kālaḥ dūrasthitaḥ vāpi* = whether death is distant or near; *yena upāyena vañcyate* = by which method it is bypassed; *tam vadāmi samāsena* = I narrate that concisely; *yathā dṛṣṭvā śivāgame* = as revealed in Śiva Āgama tantras.

**English Translation:**  
**Techniques of Deceiving Death (*Kāla Vañcana*):**  
I shall now expound concisely the esoteric yogic methods revealed in the *Śiva Āgamas* by which a practitioner bypasses and overcomes impending death (*Kāla*), whether it approaches from afar or stands near.

---

---

### Verse 352
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
एकान्तं विजनं गत्वा कृत्वाऽऽदित्यं च पृष्ठतः ।
निरीक्षयेन्निजच्छायां कण्ठदेशसमारितः ॥ ३५२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ekāntaṃ vijanaṃ gatvā kṛtvā''dityaṃ ca pṛṣṭhataḥ |
nirīkṣeyannijacchāyāṃ kaṇṭhadeśasamāritaḥ || 352 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ekāntaṃ - to a solitary; vijanaṃ - secluded place; gatvā - having gone; kṛtvā - having placed; ādityaṃ - the sun; ca - and; pṛṣṭhataḥ - at the back; nirīkṣeyat - one should observe; nija-cchāyāṃ - one's own shadow; kaṇṭha-deśa - the neck region; samāritaḥ (samāhitaḥ) - focused upon.
**English Translation:**  
Going to a solitary and secluded place, keeping the sun at one's back, one should carefully observe one's own shadow, focusing the gaze specifically on its neck region.

---

---

### Verse 353
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
ततश्चाकाशमीक्षेत ह्रीं परब्रह्मणे नमः ।
अष्टोत्तरशतं जप्त्वा ततः पश्यति शङ्करम् ॥ ३५३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tataścākāśamīkṣeta hrīṃ parabrahmaṇe namaḥ |
aṣṭottaraśataṃ japtvā tataḥ paśyati śaṅkaram || 353 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tataḥ - then; ca - and; ākāśam - into the sky; īkṣeta - one should look; hrīṃ - (the seed mantra Hrim); parabrahmaṇe - to the Supreme Brahman; namaḥ - salutations; aṣṭa-uttara-śataṃ - one hundred and eight times; japtvā - having chanted; tataḥ - then; paśyati - one sees; śaṅkaram - Lord Shiva.
**English Translation:**  
Then, looking up into the open sky, one should chant the mantra "Hrīṃ Parabrahmaṇe Namaḥ" one hundred and eight times. Thereafter, the practitioner attains the vision of Lord Shiva.

---

---

### Verse 354
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शुद्धस्फटिकसङ्काशं नानारूपधरं हरम् ।
षण्मासाभ्यासयोगेन भूचराणां पतिर्भवेत् ॥
वर्षद्वयेन तेनाथ कर्ता हर्ता स्वयं प्रभुः ॥ ३५४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śuddhasphaṭikasaṅkāśaṃ nānārūpadharaṃ haram |
ṣaṇmāsābhyāsayogena bhūcarāṇāṃ patirbhavet ||
varṣadvayena tenātha kartā hartā svayaṃ prabhuḥ || 354 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śuddha-sphaṭika-saṅkāśaṃ - resembling pure crystal; nānā-rūpa-dharaṃ - assuming various forms; haram - Lord Hara (Shiva); ṣaṭ-māsa-abhyāsa-yogena - through the practice of this yoga for six months; bhūcarāṇāṃ - of the beings living on earth; patiḥ - lord; bhavet - becomes; varṣa-dvayena - in two years; tena - by that; atha - then; kartā - creator; hartā - destroyer; svayaṃ - himself; prabhuḥ - the supreme lord.
**English Translation:**  
Through this, one sees Lord Shiva, who resembles pure crystal and assumes various forms. By practicing this yoga for six months, one becomes the lord of all beings on earth. By practicing it for two years, one becomes the creator and destroyer, the Supreme Lord Himself.

---

---

### Verse 355
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
त्रिकालज्ञत्वमाप्नोति परमानन्दमेव च ।
सतताभ्यासयोगेन नास्ति किञ्चित्सुदुर्लभम् ॥ ३५५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
trikālajñatvamāpnoti paramānandameva ca |
satatābhyāsayogena nāsti kiñcitsudurlabham || 355 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
trikāla-jñatvam - omniscience of the three times (past, present, future); āpnoti - attains; parama-ānandam - supreme bliss; eva - indeed; ca - and; satata-abhyāsa-yogena - through constant practice of yoga; nāsti - there is not; kiñcit - anything; su-durlabham - very difficult to obtain.
**English Translation:**  
One attains knowledge of the three phases of time (past, present, and future) as well as supreme bliss. Through constant practice of this yoga, there is absolutely nothing that remains difficult to achieve.

---

---

### Verse 356
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तद्रूपं कृष्णवर्णं यः पश्यति व्योम्नि निर्मले ।
षण्मासान्मृत्युमाप्नोति स योगी नात्र संशयः ॥ ३५६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tadrūpaṃ kṛṣṇavarṇaṃ yaḥ paśyati vyomni nirmale |
ṣaṇmāsānmṛtyumāpnoti sa yogī nātra saṃśayaḥ || 356 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tat-rūpaṃ - that form; kṛṣṇa-varṇaṃ - black in color; yaḥ - whoever; paśyati - sees; vyomni - in the sky; nirmale - clear/cloudless; ṣaṭ-māsāt - in six months; mṛtyum - death; āpnoti - attains; saḥ - that; yogī - yogi; na - not; atra - here; saṃśayaḥ - doubt.
**English Translation:**  
If a yogi sees that vision appearing in a black color in the clear sky, he will meet his death in six months; there is no doubt about this.

---

---

### Verse 357
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पीते व्याधिभयं रक्ते नीले हानिं विनिर्दिशेत् ।
नानावर्णश्चेत्तस्मिन् सिद्धिर्योगे भवेन्महान् ॥ ३५७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pīte vyādhibhayaṃ rakte nīle hāniṃ vinirdiśet |
nānāvarṇaścettasmin siddhiryoge bhavenmahān || 357 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pīte - if yellow; vyādhi-bhayam - fear of disease; rakte - if red (it indicates fear); nīle - if blue; hāniṃ - loss; vinirdiśet - one should indicate/predict; nānā-varṇaḥ - of various colors; cet - if; tasmin - in that (vision); siddhiḥ - perfection; yoge - in yoga; bhavet - becomes; mahān - great.
**English Translation:**  
If the form appears yellow, it indicates the onset of disease; if red, it indicates fear; if blue, it predicts loss. However, if the vision appears in various brilliant colors, the yogi attains great perfections in his practice.

---

---

### Verse 358
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पदे गुल्फे च जठरे विनाशः क्रमशः भवेत् ।
विनश्यतो यदा बाहू स्वयं तु म्रियते ध्रुवम् ॥ ३५८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pade gulphe ca jaṭhare vināśaḥ kramaśaḥ bhavet |
vinaśyato yadā bāhū svayaṃ tu mriyate dhruvam || 358 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pade - of the foot; gulphe - of the ankle; ca - and; jaṭhare - of the belly; vināśaḥ - destruction/non-appearance; kramaśaḥ - gradually; bhavet - if it occurs; vinaśyataḥ - disappear; yadā - when; bāhū - the two arms; svayaṃ - himself; tu - indeed; mriyate - dies; dhruvam - certainly.
**English Translation:**  
If the feet, ankles, and belly of one's shadow gradually become invisible, or when the arms disappear in the vision, the yogi will certainly face his own death.

---

---

### Verse 359
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
वामबाहुस्तथा भार्या नश्यते नात्र संशयः ।
दक्षिणे बन्धुनाशो हि मृत्युर्मासाद्विनिर्दिशेत् ॥ ३५९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
vāmabāhustathā bhāryā naśyate nātra saṃśayaḥ |
dakṣiṇe bandhunāśo hi mṛtyurmāsādvinirdiśet || 359 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
vāma-bāhuḥ - left arm; tathā - then; bhāryā - wife; naśyate - perishes; na - not; atra - here; saṃśayaḥ - doubt; dakṣiṇe - if the right arm (is unseen); bandhu-nāśaḥ - loss of relatives; hi - indeed; mṛtyuḥ - death; māsāt - in a month; vinirdiśet - one should predict.
**English Translation:**  
If the left arm of the shadow is not seen, the wife will perish, there is no doubt about this. If the right arm is unseen, there will be a loss of relatives, and one should predict one's own death within a month.

---

---

### Verse 360
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अशिरो मासतो मृत्युर्विना जङ्घे दिनाष्टकम् ।
अष्टाभिः स्कन्धनाशेन छायालोपे ततः क्षणात् ॥ ३६० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
aśiro māsato mṛtyurvinā jaṅghe dināṣṭakam |
aṣṭābhiḥ skandhanāśena chāyālope tataḥ kṣaṇāt || 360 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
aśiraḥ - without a head; māsataḥ - in a month; mṛtyuḥ - death; vinā - without; jaṅghe - the calves/thighs; dina-aṣṭakam - eight days; aṣṭābhiḥ - in eight days; skandha-nāśena - by the destruction (invisibility) of the shoulders; chāyā-lope - on the disappearance of the shadow entirely; tataḥ - then; kṣaṇāt - instantly.
**English Translation:**  
If the shadow appears headless, death occurs in a month. If it is without thighs, death is in eight days. If the shoulders are unseen, death is also in eight days. If the shadow disappears entirely, death is instantaneous.

---

---

### Verse 361
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
प्रातः पृष्ठगते रवौ च निमिषाच्छायां शिराश्चाधरान्
दृष्ट्वाऽर्धान् मृतिस्त्वनन्तरमदो च्छायां नरः पश्यति ।
तत्कर्णांसकरास्यपार्श्वहृदयाभावे क्षणार्धात् स्वयं
दिङ्मूढो यदि नरः शिरोविगमतो मासांस्तु षड् जीवति ॥ ३६१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
prātaḥ pṛṣṭhagate ravau ca nimiṣācchāyāṃ śirāścādharān
dṛṣṭvā'rdhān mṛtistvanantaramadocchāyāṃ naraḥ paśyati |
tatkarṇāṃsakarāsyapārśvahṛdayābhāve kṣaṇārdhāt svayaṃ
diṅmūḍho yadi naraḥ śirovigamato māsāṃstu ṣaḍ jīvati || 361 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
prātaḥ - in the morning; pṛṣṭha-gate - keeping behind the back; ravau - the sun; ca - and; nimiṣāt - in a moment; chāyāṃ - the shadow; śirāḥ - head/thumbs; ca - and; adharān - lips; dṛṣṭvā - having seen; ardhān - (in half a moment); mṛtiḥ - death; tu - indeed; anantaram - afterwards; adaḥ - that; chāyāṃ - shadow; naraḥ - man; paśyati - sees; tat - its; karṇa - ears; aṃsa - shoulders; kara - hands; āsya - mouth; pārśva - sides; hṛdaya - heart; abhāve - in the absence of; kṣaṇa-ardhāt - in half a moment; svayaṃ - himself; dik-mūḍhaḥ - confused about directions; yadi - if; naraḥ - a man; śiraḥ-vigamataḥ - due to absence of the head; māsān - months; tu - indeed; ṣaṭ - six; jīvati - lives.
**English Translation:**  
In the morning, standing with the sun at one's back, if one does not see the thumbs and lips in the shadow, death occurs in a moment. If one then sees neither the shadow nor oneself, death is in half a moment. If the shadow's ears, shoulders, hands, mouth, sides, or heart are missing, death occurs in half a moment. If the head of the shadow is missing and the person becomes disoriented regarding directions, he will live for only six months.

---

---

### Verse 362
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
एकादिषोडशाहानि यदि भानौ निरन्तरम् ।
वहेद्यस्य भवेन्मृत्युः शेषादेन च मासिके ॥ ३६२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ekādiṣoḍaśāhāni yadi bhānau nirantaram |
vahedyasya bhavenmṛtyuḥ śeṣādena ca māsike || 362 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
eka-ādi - beginning with one; ṣoḍaśa-ahāni - up to sixteen days; yadi - if; bhānau - in the sun (solar nadi, Pingala); nirantaram - continuously; vahet - flows; yasya - of whom; bhavet - happens; mṛtyuḥ - death; śeṣādena - (within fifteen days); ca - and; māsike - in a month.
**English Translation:**  
If a person's breath flows continuously through the sun (Pingala) channel for sixteen consecutive days, his death will occur within fifteen days.

---

---

### Verse 363
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सम्पूर्णं वहते सूर्यश्चन्द्रमा नैव दृश्यते ।
पक्षेण जायते मृत्युः कालज्ञानेन भाषितम् ॥ ३६३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sampūrṇaṃ vahate sūryaścandramā naiva dṛśyate |
pakṣeṇa jāyate mṛtyuḥ kālajñānena bhāṣitam || 363 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sampūrṇaṃ - fully; vahate - flows; sūryaḥ - the sun (Pingala); candramā - the moon (Ida); na - not; eva - indeed; dṛśyate - is seen; pakṣeṇa - in a fortnight (15 days); jāyate - happens; mṛtyuḥ - death; kāla-jñānena - by the knowledge of time (death); bhāṣitam - is spoken/declared.
**English Translation:**  
If the solar breath flows continuously and the lunar breath is never observed, death occurs within a fortnight. This is declared by those who possess the knowledge of time and death.

---

---

### Verse 364
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
मूत्रं पुरीषं वायुश्च समकालं प्रवर्तते ।
तदाऽसौ चकितो ज्ञेयो दशाहे म्रियते ध्रुवम् ॥ ३६४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
mūtraṃ purīṣaṃ vāyuśca samakālaṃ pravartate |
tadā'sau cakito jñeyo daśāhe mriyate dhruvam || 364 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
mūtraṃ - urine; purīṣaṃ - feces; vāyuḥ - wind (flatus); ca - and; sama-kālaṃ - at the same time; pravartate - occurs/passes; tadā - then; asau - he; cakitaḥ - alarmed/bewildered; jñeyaḥ - should be known; daśa-ahe - in ten days; mriyate - dies; dhruvam - certainly.
**English Translation:**  
If a person passes urine, feces, and flatus all simultaneously, his state should be known as extremely alarming, and he will certainly die within ten days.

---

---

### Verse 365
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
सम्पूर्णं वहते चन्द्रः सूर्यो नैव च दृश्यते ।
मासेन जायते मृत्युः कालज्ञैर्नात्र संशयः ॥ ३६५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
sampūrṇaṃ vahate candraḥ sūryo naiva ca dṛśyate |
māsena jāyate mṛtyuḥ kālajñairnātra saṃśayaḥ || 365 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
sampūrṇaṃ - fully; vahate - flows; candraḥ - the moon (Ida); sūryaḥ - the sun (Pingala); na - not; eva - indeed; ca - and; dṛśyate - is seen; māsena - in a month; jāyate - happens; mṛtyuḥ - death; kāla-jñaiḥ - by the knowers of time; na - not; atra - here; saṃśayaḥ - doubt.
**English Translation:**  
If the lunar breath flows continuously and the solar breath is never seen, death occurs within a month. This has been declared by the knowers of time, and there is no doubt about it.

---

---

### Verse 366
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अरुन्धती ध्रुवश्चैव विष्णोस्त्रीणि पदानि च ।
आयुर्हीना न पश्यन्ति चतुर्थं मातृमण्डलम् ॥ ३६६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
arundhatī dhruvaścaiva viṣṇostrīṇi padāni ca |
āyurhīnā na paśyanti caturthaṃ mātṛmaṇḍalam || 366 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
arundhatī - Arundhati; dhruvaḥ - Dhruva; ca - and; eva - indeed; viṣṇoḥ - of Vishnu; trīṇi - three; padāni - steps; ca - and; āyuḥ-hīnāḥ - those whose life span is exhausted; na - not; paśyanti - see; caturthaṃ - the fourth; mātṛ-maṇḍalam - the Matru-Mandala.
**English Translation:**  
Those whose life span is exhausted cannot see Arundhati, the Pole Star (Dhruva), the three steps of Vishnu, and fourthly, the Matru-Mandala (cluster of stars).

---

---

### Verse 367
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
अरुन्धती भवेज्जिह्वा ध्रुवो नासाग्रमेव च ।
भ्रुवोर्विष्णुपदं ज्ञेयं तारकं मातृमण्डलम् ॥ ३६७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
arundhatī bhavejjihvā dhruvo nāsāgrameva ca |
bhruvorviṣṇupadaṃ jñeyaṃ tārakaṃ mātṛmaṇḍalam || 367 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
arundhatī - Arundhati; bhavet - is; jihvā - the tongue; dhruvaḥ - Dhruva; nāsā-agram - the tip of the nose; eva - indeed; ca - and; bhruvoḥ - between the eyebrows; viṣṇu-padam - the step of Vishnu; jñeyaṃ - should be known; tārakaṃ - the stars; mātṛ-maṇḍalam - the Matru-Mandala.
**English Translation:**  
In this context, the tongue is known as Arundhati, the tip of the nose is Dhruva, the space between the eyebrows is the step of Vishnu, and the stars (seen in inner vision) are the Matru-Mandala.

---

---

### Verse 368
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
नव भ्रुवौ सप्त घोषं पञ्च तारान्त्रिनासिकाम् ।
जिह्वामेकदिनं प्रोक्तं म्रियते मानवो ध्रुवम् ॥ ३६८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
nava bhruvau sapta ghoṣaṃ pañca tārāntrināsikām |
jihvāmekadinaṃ proktaṃ mriyate mānavo dhruvam || 368 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
nava - nine (days); bhruvau - eyebrows; sapta - seven (days); ghoṣaṃ - sound; pañca - five (days); tārān - stars; tri - three (days); nāsikām - nose; jihvām - tongue; eka-dinaṃ - one day; proktaṃ - is said; mriyate - dies; mānavaḥ - man; dhruvam - certainly.
**English Translation:**  
If one cannot see the eyebrows, death is in nine days; if one cannot hear sounds, in seven days; if one cannot see the stars, in five days; if one cannot see the nose, in three days; and if one cannot see the tongue, it is said that the person will certainly die in one day.

---

---

### Verse 369
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
कोणावक्ष्णोरङ्गुलीभ्यां किञ्चित्पीड्य निरीक्षयेत् ।
यदा न स्यन्दते बिन्दुर्दशाहेन भवेन्मृतः ॥ ३६९ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
koṇāvakṣṇoraṅgulībhyāṃ kiñcitpīḍya nirīkṣayet |
yadā na syandate bindurdaśāhena bhavenmṛtaḥ || 369 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
koṇau - the corners; akṣṇoḥ - of the eyes; aṅgulībhyāṃ - with the fingers; kiñcit - slightly; pīḍya - having pressed; nirīkṣayet - one should observe; yadā - when; na - not; syandate - flows/manifests; binduḥ - a drop of light; daśa-ahena - in ten days; bhavet - becomes; mṛtaḥ - dead.
**English Translation:**  
One should slightly press the corners of the eyes with the fingers and observe. If a bright circular drop of light does not manifest, the person will die within ten days.

---

---

### Verse 370
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
तीर्थस्नानेन दानेन तपसा सुकृतेन च ।
जपध्यानेन योगेन जायते कालवञ्चना ॥ ३७० ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
tīrthasnānena dānena tapasā sukṛtena ca |
japadhyānena yogena jāyate kālavañcanā || 370 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
tīrtha-snānena - by bathing in sacred places; dānena - by charity; tapasā - by penance; sukṛtena - by righteous deeds; ca - and; japa-dhyānena - by chanting and meditation; yogena - by yoga; jāyate - happens; kāla-vañcanā - cheating of death.
**English Translation:**  
Through bathing in holy places, giving charity, performing penance, engaging in righteous deeds, practicing japa (chanting) and meditation, and through the practice of yoga, one can cheat (delay) death.

---

---

### Verse 371
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
शरीरं नाशयन्त्येते दोषा धातुमलास्तथा ।
समस्तु वायुर्विज्ञेयो बलतेजोविवर्धनः ॥ ३७१ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
śarīraṃ nāśayantyete doṣā dhātumalāstathā |
samastu vāyurvijñeyo balatejovivardhanaḥ || 371 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
śarīraṃ - the body; nāśayanti - destroy; ete - these; doṣāḥ - humors (Vata, Pitta, Kapha); dhātu - tissues; malāḥ - impurities; tathā - and; samaḥ - balanced; tu - but; vāyuḥ - wind (breath); vijñeyaḥ - should be known; bala-tejaḥ-vivardhanaḥ - enhancer of strength and radiance.
**English Translation:**  
Imbalances of the doshas, dhatus, and malas destroy the physical body. However, a balanced flow of breath (Vayu) should be recognized as the true enhancer of strength and radiance.

---

---

### Verse 372
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रक्षणीयस्ततो देहो यतो धर्मादिसाधनम् ।
योगाभ्यासात्स्वमायान्ति साध्या यान्त्यप्यसाध्यताम् ।
असाध्या जीवितं घ्नन्ति तत्र नास्ति प्रतिक्रिया ॥ ३७२ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
rakṣaṇīyastato deho yato dharmādisādhanam |
yogābhyāsātsvayāyānti sādhyā yāntyapyasādhyatām |
asādhyā jīvitaṃ ghnanti tatra nāsti pratikriyā || 372 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
rakṣaṇīyaḥ - should be protected; tataḥ - therefore; dehaḥ - body; yataḥ - because; dharma-ādi-sādhanam - the instrument for fulfilling dharma; yoga-abhyāsāt - by the practice of yoga; svayāyānti - they come under control; sādhyā - curable diseases; yānti - go; api - even; asādhyatām - to curability; asādhyāḥ - the incurable ones (without yoga); jīvitaṃ - life; ghnanti - destroy; tatra - there; na - not; asti - is; pratikriyā - remedy.
**English Translation:**  
Therefore, the physical body must be protected, as it is the primary instrument for fulfilling Dharma. Through the practice of yoga, even incurable diseases become curable. For those who do not practice, incurable ailments destroy life, and for them, there is no other remedy.

---

---

### Verse 373
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
येषां हृदि स्फुरति शाश्वतमद्वितीयं
तेजस्तमोनिवहनाशकरं रहस्यम् ।
तेषामखण्डशशिरम्यसुकांतिभासां
स्वप्नेऽपि नो भवति कालभयं नराणाम् ॥ ३७३ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
yeṣāṃ hṛdi sphurati śāśvatamadvitīyaṃ
tejastamonivahanāśakaraṃ rahasyam |
teṣāmakhaṇḍaśaśiramyasukāntibhāsāṃ
svapne'pi no bhavati kālabhayaṃ narāṇām || 373 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
yeṣāṃ - whose; hṛdi - in the heart; sphurati - shines; śāśvatam - eternal; advitīyaṃ - non-dual; tejaḥ - light/knowledge; tamaḥ-nivaha-nāśa-karaṃ - which destroys the mass of darkness; rahasyam - the secret; teṣām - for those; akhaṇḍa-śaśi-ramya-su-kānti-bhāsāṃ - possessing the beautiful radiance of the full moon; svapne - in a dream; api - even; no - not; bhavati - is; kāla-bhayaṃ - fear of death; narāṇām - for the men.
**English Translation:**  
For those men in whose hearts shines the eternal, non-dual, and secret light that destroys the mass of darkness, their radiance becomes beautiful like the full moon. They do not experience the fear of death, even in their dreams.

---

---

### Verse 374
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
इडा गङ्गेति विज्ञेया पिङ्गला यमुना नदी ।
मध्ये सरस्वती विद्यात्प्रयागादिसमं तथा ॥ ३७४ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
iḍā gaṅgeti vijñeyā piṅgalā yamunā nadī |
madhye sarasvatīṃ vidyāt prayāgādisamaṃ tathā || 374 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
iḍā - the Ida nadi; gaṅgā - the Ganges; iti - thus; vijñeyā - should be known; piṅgalā - the Pingala nadi; yamunā - the Yamuna; nadī - river; madhye - in the middle; sarasvatīṃ - the Sarasvati river; vidyāt - one should know; prayāga-ādi-samaṃ - equal to Prayaga and others; tathā - similarly.
**English Translation:**  
The Ida Nadi should be understood as the river Ganges, and the Pingala Nadi as the river Yamuna. The central channel (Sushumna) should be known as the Sarasvati, and their confluence is considered equivalent to the sacred Prayaga.

---

---

### Verse 375
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
आदौ साधनमाख्यातं सद्यः प्रत्ययकारकम् ।
बद्धपद्मासनो योगी बन्धयेदुड्डियानकम् ॥ ३७५ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
ādau sādhanamākhyātaṃ sadyaḥ pratyayakārakam |
baddhapadmāsano yogī bandhayeduḍḍiyānakam || 375 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
ādau - in the beginning; sādhanam - the practice; ākhyātaṃ - is described; sadyaḥ - instantly; pratyaya-kārakam - producing results; baddha-padmāsanaḥ - bound in the lotus posture; yogī - the yogi; bandhayet - should lock; uḍḍiyānakam - the Uddiyana Bandha.
**English Translation:**  
First, the practice that instantly produces tangible results is described. The yogi, sitting firmly in the lotus posture (Padmasana), should apply the Uddiyana Bandha (the abdominal lock).

---

---

### Verse 376
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूरकः कुम्भकश्चैव रेचकश्च तृतीयकः ।
ज्ञातव्यो योगिभिर्नित्यं देहसंशुद्धये ध्रुवम् ॥ ३७६ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrakaḥ kumbhakaścaiva recakaśca tṛtīyakaḥ |
jñātavyo yogibhirnityaṃ dehasaṃśuddhaye dhruvam || 376 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrakaḥ - inhalation; kumbhakaḥ - retention; ca - and; eva - indeed; recakaḥ - exhalation; ca - and; tṛtīyakaḥ - the third; jñātavyaḥ - should be known; yogibhiḥ - by the yogis; nityaṃ - always; deha-saṃśuddhaye - for the complete purification of the body; dhruvam - certainly.
**English Translation:**  
Inhalation (Puraka), retention (Kumbhaka), and the third, exhalation (Recaka), must always be practiced by yogis for the complete purification of the body.

---

---

### Verse 377
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
पूरकः कुरुते वृद्धिं धातुसाम्यं तथैव च ।
कुम्भकः स्तम्भनं कुर्याज्जीवरक्षाविवर्धनम् ॥ ३७७ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
pūrakaḥ kurute vṛddhiṃ dhātusāmyaṃ tathaiva ca |
kumbhakaḥ stambhanaṃ kuryājjīvarakṣāvivardhanam || 377 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
pūrakaḥ - inhalation; kurute - makes; vṛddhiṃ - growth; dhātu-sāmyaṃ - balance of tissues; tathā - and; eva - indeed; ca - also; kumbhakaḥ - retention; stambhanaṃ - stopping/steadiness; kuryāt - should do; jīva-rakṣā-vivardhanam - increasing the protection of life.
**English Translation:**  
Puraka (inhalation) promotes physical growth and balances the bodily elements. Kumbhaka (retention) creates inner steadiness, which in turn enhances the protection and longevity of life.

---

---

### Verse 378
**Devanāgarī Śloka:**  
रेचको हरते पापं योगी योगपदं व्रजेत् ।
पश्चात्समानवृत्तिः सन् मृत्युवेधं च कारयेत् ॥ ३७८ ॥
**IAST Transliteration:**  
recako harate pāpaṃ yogī yogapadaṃ vrajet |
paścātsamānavṛttiḥ san mṛtyuvedhaṃ ca kārayet || 378 ||
**Word-for-Word Meaning:**  
recakaḥ - exhalation; harate - removes; pāpaṃ - sin; yogī - the yogi; yoga-padaṃ - the state of yoga; vrajet - attains; paścāt - afterwards; samāna-vṛttiḥ - equalized state; san - being; mṛtyu-vedhaṃ - conquering death; ca - and; kārayet - one should cause (accomplish).
**English Translation:**
